# Initial Rule Research

Date started: 2026-07-03

This is a running research table for candidate "code commandments": general coding rules that should apply across languages, stacks, and project types.

| # | Candidate Commandment | Extracted Rule / Evidence | Source | Source Rating | Rule Importance | Notes |
|---:|---|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| 1 | Optimize for long-term maintainability. | Code review should protect the long-term health of the codebase, including design, complexity, tests, naming, style, and documentation. | [Google Engineering Practices - What to look for in a code review](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html) | 5 | 5 | Strong general-purpose source; directly framed as cross-language engineering practice. |
| 2 | Make the code behave correctly for its users. | Reviewers should check whether the code does what the author intended and whether that behavior is good for users. | [Google Engineering Practices - What to look for in a code review](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html) | 5 | 5 | Correctness is the first non-negotiable quality gate. |
| 3 | Keep code no more complex than necessary. | Google review guidance calls out complexity at line, function, class, and system levels, and warns against unneeded future functionality. | [Google Engineering Practices - What to look for in a code review](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html) | 5 | 5 | One of the strongest repeated themes across sources. |
| 4 | Do not implement speculative features. | Avoid building things you only might need later when you do not know you need them now. | [Google Engineering Practices - What to look for in a code review](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html) | 5 | 5 | YAGNI-equivalent; reduces accidental complexity and maintenance load. |
| 5 | Choose names that reveal intent. | Google review guidance expects clear names for everything; PEP 8 frames readability as a core reason for style guidance. | [Google Engineering Practices - What to look for in a code review](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html) | 5 | 5 | Naming is low-level but universal; poor names multiply every later maintenance cost. |
| 6 | Make code readable because code is read more than written. | PEP 8 explicitly prioritizes readability and consistency because code is read much more often than it is written. | [PEP 8 - Style Guide for Python Code](https://peps.python.org/pep-0008/) | 5 | 5 | Python-specific guide, but the principle is universal. |
| 7 | Follow the local style guide and formatter. | Google style guidance says large codebases are easier to understand when code follows a consistent style. | [Google Style Guides](https://google.github.io/styleguide/) | 5 | 4 | Style is not the highest principle, but consistency removes needless reader friction. |
| 8 | Require appropriate automated tests. | Google review guidance expects appropriate unit tests and well-designed tests for changes. | [Google Engineering Practices - What to look for in a code review](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html) | 5 | 5 | Tests are a core safety net for change. |
| 9 | Make tests maintainable and actionable. | Google's SWE book emphasizes tests that fail for real bugs with clear causes; Google Testing Blog says test failures should be actionable. | [Software Engineering at Google - Unit Testing](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch12.html) | 5 | 5 | Bad tests become noise and can be worse than no signal. |
| 10 | Prefer fast, focused tests, with broader tests used deliberately. | Google's testing guidance and Fowler's test pyramid emphasize many lower-level tests and fewer broad end-to-end tests because larger tests are slower and more brittle. | [Google Testing Blog - Just Say No to More End-to-End Tests](https://testing.googleblog.com/2015/04/just-say-no-to-more-end-to-end-tests.html) | 5 | 4 | Exact mix is context-dependent; pyramid shape is still a strong heuristic. |
| 11 | Validate all untrusted input at trusted boundaries. | OWASP says to classify data sources and validate untrusted input on a trusted system before processing. | [OWASP Secure Coding Practices Checklist](https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-coding-practices-quick-reference-guide/stable-en/02-checklist/05-checklist) | 5 | 5 | Universal security rule for any code handling external data. |
| 12 | Encode or sanitize output for the destination context. | OWASP recommends tested output encoding and contextual sanitization for untrusted data sent to clients, queries, XML, LDAP, or OS commands. | [OWASP Secure Coding Practices Checklist](https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-coding-practices-quick-reference-guide/stable-en/02-checklist/05-checklist) | 5 | 5 | Pairs with validation; central to preventing injection and rendering attacks. |
| 13 | Use safe APIs instead of string-built commands or queries. | OWASP recommends strongly typed parameterized queries and secure task-specific APIs when interacting with databases and other interpreters. | [OWASP Secure Coding Practices Checklist](https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-coding-practices-quick-reference-guide/stable-en/02-checklist/05-checklist) | 5 | 5 | Generalizes beyond SQL to any command/interpreter boundary. |
| 14 | Run with least privilege. | OWASP calls for the lowest possible privilege when accessing databases; least privilege generalizes across services, files, credentials, and processes. | [OWASP Secure Coding Practices Checklist](https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-coding-practices-quick-reference-guide/stable-en/02-checklist/05-checklist) | 5 | 5 | Limits blast radius when defects or compromises happen. |
| 15 | Build security into the lifecycle, not after the fact. | NIST SSDF defines high-level secure development practices because security must be added into SDLCs deliberately. | [NIST SP 800-218 Secure Software Development Framework](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/218/final) | 5 | 5 | Applies broadly to design, implementation, review, release, and vulnerability response. |
| 16 | Make systems secure by design and secure by default. | CISA guidance emphasizes products built to protect users by default and shifts responsibility toward software producers. | [CISA Secure by Design](https://www.cisa.gov/securebydesign) | 5 | 5 | Especially important where users may not configure security correctly themselves. |
| 17 | Treat security and reliability as inseparable quality attributes. | Google's Building Secure and Reliable Systems frames security and reliability as interacting lifecycle concerns. | [Google SRE - Building Secure and Reliable Systems](https://sre.google/books/) | 5 | 5 | A system that is unreliable or insecure cannot be considered high-quality. |
| 18 | Design for people and time, not just for the current programmer. | Software Engineering at Google defines software engineering as programming integrated over time, adding maintenance and evolution as first-class concerns. | [Software Engineering at Google - What Is Software Engineering?](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch01.html) | 5 | 5 | This may be the umbrella rule behind maintainability commandments. |
| 19 | Keep APIs explicit, predictable, and backward-compatible unless intentionally versioned. | SemVer ties version changes to public API compatibility; Hyrum's Law warns that users may depend on observable behavior. | [Semantic Versioning 2.0.0](https://semver.org/) | 5 | 4 | Crucial for libraries/services; less relevant for private throwaway code. |
| 20 | Explicitly declare and isolate dependencies. | The Twelve-Factor App requires explicit dependency declaration and isolation for repeatable builds and deploys. | [The Twelve-Factor App](https://12factor.net/) | 4 | 4 | Strong for apps/services; generalizes to reproducible environments. |
| 21 | Keep configuration and secrets out of code. | Twelve-Factor separates config from code, especially deployment-specific values and credentials. | [The Twelve-Factor App](https://12factor.net/) | 4 | 5 | Security and deployability issue; hardcoded secrets are a high-severity failure. |
| 22 | Eliminate true duplication. | Pragmatic Programmer materials emphasize DRY; Artima's interview with Hunt and Thomas frames DRY and orthogonality as maintenance principles. | [Pragmatic Programmer Tips](https://pragprog.com/tips/) | 5 | 4 | Strong rule, but must not force unrelated concepts into one abstraction. |
| 23 | Do not couple things merely because they look similar today. | Community discussion around DRY warns that incidentally similar code can evolve separately and should not always be unified. | [Hacker News - Good Software Development Habits discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=42165057) | 2 | 4 | Community source, but caveat is important: DRY is about knowledge duplication, not line-count worship. |
| 24 | Hide complexity behind small, stable interfaces. | Ousterhout's design guidance focuses on managing complexity through decomposition, information hiding, and fewer dependencies. | [A Philosophy of Software Design extract](https://web.stanford.edu/~ouster/cgi-bin/aposd2ndEdExtract.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Strong candidate commandment for all nontrivial systems. |
| 25 | Refactor through small, behavior-preserving steps guarded by tests. | Fowler defines refactoring as behavior-preserving transformation and ties refactoring practice to tests and code smells. | [Martin Fowler - Refactoring](https://martinfowler.com/books/refactoring.html) | 5 | 5 | Key to improving code without destabilizing working systems. |
| 26 | Let simple design pass tests, reveal intent, remove duplication, and use the fewest elements. | Fowler summarizes Kent Beck's simple design rules in priority order: tests, intention, no duplication, fewest elements. | [Martin Fowler - Beck Design Rules](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/BeckDesignRules.html) | 5 | 5 | A compact high-quality rule set; excellent source for final commandments. |
| 27 | Handle errors explicitly and fail safely. | Go review comments emphasize handling errors; OWASP requires authentication controls and validation failures to fail securely. | [Go Code Review Comments](https://go.dev/wiki/CodeReviewComments) | 5 | 5 | Universal, though language mechanics vary. |
| 28 | Document the "why", contracts, and surprises. | Go review comments require meaningful doc comments; Google review guidance asks whether comments are clear and useful. | [Go Code Review Comments](https://go.dev/wiki/CodeReviewComments) | 5 | 4 | Comments should reduce ambiguity, not restate obvious code. |
| 29 | Make changes small enough to review and reason about. | DORA identifies working in small batches as a core capability for shorter feedback loops and reduced instability. | [DORA Capability Catalog](https://dora.dev/capabilities/) | 5 | 4 | More process-adjacent than code-only, but directly affects code quality and review safety. |
| 30 | Measure delivery with both speed and stability. | DORA metrics pair throughput with instability metrics such as change failure rate and recovery time. | [DORA Metrics](https://dora.dev/guides/dora-metrics/) | 5 | 3 | Useful systems rule; less suitable as a universal code commandment. |
| 31 | Make APIs clear at the call site and hard to misuse. | Swift prioritizes clarity where APIs are used; Rust emphasizes predictability and type safety; Microsoft API guidance emphasizes APIs that are easy to understand and maintain. | [Swift API Design Guidelines](https://www.swift.org/documentation/api-design-guidelines/) | 5 | 5 | A strong interface-level version of readability and correctness. |
| 32 | Encode invariants in types, contracts, and validation where practical. | Rust API guidance emphasizes type safety and dependability; TypeScript guidance warns against disabling type checking with `any`; NIST SSDF aims to reduce vulnerabilities during development. | [Rust API Guidelines Checklist](https://rust-lang.github.io/api-guidelines/checklist.html) | 5 | 5 | Static guarantees beat repeated runtime discipline when the codebase supports them. |
| 33 | Protect secrets and sensitive data from code, logs, errors, and telemetry. | Security sources consistently warn against exposing credentials, tokens, session identifiers, keys, and sensitive payloads. | [OWASP Secure Coding Practices Checklist](https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-coding-practices-quick-reference-guide/stable-en/02-checklist/05-checklist) | 5 | 5 | Extends the config/secrets rule into logging and runtime behavior. |
| 34 | Log and monitor what matters without leaking sensitive data. | OWASP and NIST support security-relevant logging; SRE guidance treats monitoring as necessary for reliability and recovery. | [Google SRE Books](https://sre.google/books/) | 5 | 4 | Observability is essential, but unsafe logs create security and privacy failures. |
| 35 | Treat dependencies as shipped code. | NIST and OWASP secure development practices include third-party component management; OpenSSF Scorecard evaluates dependency and project security health. | [OpenSSF Scorecard](https://www.scorecard.dev/) | 4 | 5 | Dependency risk becomes product risk once the dependency is included. |
| 36 | Design for partial failure, overload, and recovery. | SRE guidance emphasizes reliability under real production conditions; agent security/reliability research highlighted timeouts, quotas, backpressure, and graceful degradation. | [Google SRE - Operational Simplicity](https://sre.google/sre-book/simplicity/) | 5 | 5 | Universal for services and distributed systems; less applicable to tiny scripts. |
| 37 | Make automated checks part of the normal path. | NIST SSDF recommends automated tools for vulnerability and standards checks; DORA identifies test automation as a core delivery capability. | [NIST SP 800-218 Secure Software Development Framework](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/218/final) | 5 | 5 | Humans should review judgment calls; machines should repeat mechanical checks. |
| 38 | Compile, lint, and analyze cleanly at high signal levels. | CERT recommends compiling at the highest warning level and eliminating warnings; NIST SSDF recommends static analysis for vulnerabilities and secure coding compliance. | [SEI CERT MSC00-C](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/recommendations/miscellaneous-msc/msc00-c/) | 5 | 4 | Strong where compiler/analyzer ecosystems are mature; warning policies need stable toolchains. |
| 39 | Put an automated test around behavior you cannot afford to break. | Fowler's self-testing code and Google/Microsoft testing guidance frame tests as executable protection against regressions. | [Martin Fowler - Self Testing Code](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/SelfTestingCode.html) | 5 | 5 | Better wording than "test everything"; it ties testing effort to risk. |
| 40 | Add or update regression tests for every bug fix. | Testing guidance treats regression tests as the way to make known failures stay fixed. | [Microsoft Unit Testing Best Practices](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/testing/unit-testing-best-practices) | 5 | 5 | Known bugs should become executable examples. |
| 41 | Keep tests fast, isolated, repeatable, self-checking, and timely. | Microsoft summarizes the FIRST qualities; these properties make tests trusted developer feedback instead of ceremony. | [Microsoft Unit Testing Best Practices](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/testing/unit-testing-best-practices) | 5 | 5 | A concise quality bar for tests across languages. |
| 42 | Treat flaky tests as defects. | Google testing sources emphasize flakiness as a major challenge because it destroys trust in CI and test results. | [Google Testing Blog - Test Flakiness](https://testing.googleblog.com/2020/12/test-flakiness-one-of-main-challenges.html) | 5 | 5 | Quarantine may reduce noise, but root cause still needs ownership. |
| 43 | Test observable behavior through public contracts, not private implementation details. | Google testing and review guidance favors behavior-focused tests; implementation-coupled tests make refactoring expensive. | [Software Engineering at Google - Test Doubles](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch13.html) | 5 | 4 | Some critical low-level internals still deserve direct tests. |
| 44 | Use test doubles sparingly and intentionally. | Google guidance distinguishes real implementations, fakes, stubs, and mocks; overuse of mocks makes tests brittle and less representative. | [Software Engineering at Google - Test Doubles](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch13.html) | 5 | 4 | Prefer real deterministic dependencies when cheap; mock meaningful interactions only. |
| 45 | Choose test data that can actually fail. | Google testing guidance recommends non-default values, boundaries, empty/null cases, and distinct inputs to avoid tests that pass accidentally. | [Google Testing Blog - Choosing Values for Robust Tests](https://testing.googleblog.com/2026/06/choosing-values-for-robust-tests.html) | 5 | 4 | Avoid combinatorial explosion; use parameterization or generation when useful. |
| 46 | Use property-based or fuzz-style tests for broad input spaces and invariants. | Hypothesis and FsCheck show property-based testing value; OSS-Fuzz demonstrates fuzzing finding vulnerabilities and bugs at scale. | [Hypothesis JOSS Paper](https://joss.theoj.org/papers/10.21105/joss.01891.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Not mandatory for every glue function, but very strong for parsers, serializers, transforms, and protocols. |
| 47 | Design code for testability by separating pure logic from side effects. | Testing sources consistently favor isolating hard dependencies such as time, I/O, network, and external services. | [Microsoft Shift Left Testing](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/devops/develop/shift-left-make-testing-fast-reliable) | 5 | 5 | Testability is a design quality, not just a test-suite property. |
| 48 | Treat test code as production code. | Google and Microsoft testing guidance stress maintainable tests; bad tests create false confidence and drag. | [Software Engineering at Google - Unit Testing](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch12.html) | 5 | 5 | Test readability can justify some repetition; test DRY should not obscure scenarios. |
| 49 | Use coverage as a signal, not a goal. | Testing guidance warns that high coverage can miss behavior while low coverage still exposes risk. | [Microsoft Unit Testing Best Practices](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/testing/unit-testing-best-practices) | 5 | 4 | Coverage gates can help, but cannot replace behavior and risk review. |
| 50 | Prefer clarity over cleverness, terseness, or pattern display. | Community sources repeatedly favor boring, explicit code and warn that clever abstractions and cargo-cult rules harm maintenance. | [The Grug Brained Developer](https://grugbrain.dev/) | 3 | 5 | Community signal, but it strongly agrees with canonical simplicity guidance. |
| 51 | Apply principles as judgment tools, not as cargo-cult laws. | Community discussions around Clean Code, SOLID, DRY, KISS, and YAGNI repeatedly warn that blindly applying slogans causes poor design. | [Hacker News - Cargo cult programming](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=27907120) | 3 | 5 | Important meta-rule for any final commandments list. |
| 52 | Give every piece of system knowledge one authoritative home. | The Pragmatic Programmer's DRY framing is about avoiding duplicated knowledge, not just duplicated text. | [The Pragmatic Programmer DRY excerpt](https://media.pragprog.com/titles/tpp20/dry.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Stronger and more precise than "never duplicate code." |
| 53 | Put all nontrivial work under version control. | The Joel Test treats source control as a basic quality practice; Pragmatic Programmer guidance also emphasizes source control. | [The Joel Test](https://www.joelonsoftware.com/2000/08/09/the-joel-test-12-steps-to-better-code/) | 4 | 5 | Table-stakes for collaboration, recovery, traceability, and review. |
| 54 | Make builds reproducible and routinely exercised. | Joel Spolsky's daily build guidance and continuous integration sources treat regular successful builds as a core quality signal. | [Joel Spolsky - Daily Builds Are Your Friend](https://www.joelonsoftware.com/2001/01/27/daily-builds-are-your-friend/) | 4 | 5 | A codebase nobody can reliably build is not maintainable. |
| 55 | Keep tests, builds, and regression checks close to every change. | Fowler CI, Joel daily builds, and Google/Microsoft test guidance all push feedback earlier and closer to code changes. | [Martin Fowler - Continuous Integration](https://martinfowler.com/articles/continuousIntegration.html) | 5 | 5 | Delayed validation turns small mistakes into expensive archaeology. |
| 56 | Write a failing test before changing behavior when practical. | TDD guidance from Fowler and Google frames test-first work as a design and regression-safety discipline. | [Martin Fowler - Test Driven Development](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/TestDrivenDevelopment.html) | 5 | 4 | Excellent default for behavior changes; spikes and trivial changes may use characterization or post-hoc regression tests. |
| 57 | Refactor working systems incrementally; default against full rewrites. | Joel's rewrite essay warns that rewrites discard accumulated bug fixes and operational knowledge; refactoring sources favor behavior-preserving change. | [Joel Spolsky - Things You Should Never Do, Part I](https://www.joelonsoftware.com/2000/04/06/things-you-should-never-do-part-i/) | 4 | 5 | Rewrites can be justified, but should be deliberate, scoped, and evidence-backed. |
| 58 | Design for likely change, but price the flexibility. | Code Complete, APOSD, and Pragmatic Programmer reversibility themes support isolating volatility without speculative architecture. | [Code Complete - Design in Construction](https://www.microsoftpressstore.com/articles/article.aspx?p=2222451) | 5 | 5 | Flexibility for unlikely futures is often just complexity. |
| 59 | Treat design as iterative learning. | No Silver Bullet, Code Complete, and Pragmatic Programmer sources emphasize prototypes, feedback, and validation because requirements and designs evolve. | [No Silver Bullet](https://www.cs.unc.edu/techreports/86-020.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Formal environments still need checkpoints, but design is rarely perfect on the first pass. |
| 60 | Expect abstractions to leak; know the layer below. | Joel's Law of Leaky Abstractions argues that abstractions help but cannot eliminate the need to understand lower layers when things fail. | [Joel Spolsky - The Law of Leaky Abstractions](https://www.joelonsoftware.com/2002/11/11/the-law-of-leaky-abstractions/) | 4 | 4 | This is a caveat to abstraction, not an argument against abstraction. |
| 61 | Reuse proven components only when the fit is real. | No Silver Bullet and SWEBOK support reuse as a way to avoid rebuilding solved infrastructure, but dependency guidance adds risk caveats. | [SWEBOK](https://blp.ieee.org/software-engineering-body-of-knowledge-swebok/) | 5 | 4 | Reuse trades implementation risk for dependency, license, security, and maintenance risk. |
| 62 | Keep project knowledge visible and current. | Joel Test and SWEBOK sources support specs, issue tracking, schedules, and decisions as ways to reduce reliance on memory. | [The Joel Test](https://www.joelonsoftware.com/2000/08/09/the-joel-test-12-steps-to-better-code/) | 4 | 4 | Documentation must stay light enough to maintain and close enough to code to remain true. |
| 63 | Treat technical debt as an explicit liability, not background noise. | ACM Queue technical-debt guidance frames debt as a management and design concern requiring visibility and intentional repayment. | [ACM Queue - Managing Technical Debt](https://queue.acm.org/detail.cfm?id=2168798) | 4 | 5 | Hidden debt silently taxes every future change. |
| 64 | Practice software engineering as a public-interest profession. | ACM/IEEE ethics place public interest, client/user welfare, product quality, judgment, management, profession, colleagues, and self-improvement into professional obligations. | [Software Engineering Code of Ethics](https://www.acm.org/code-of-ethics/software-engineering-code) | 5 | 5 | Ethical responsibility is not separate from code quality when code affects people. |
| 65 | Design so the next likely change is easy and localized. | Parnas and architecture sources frame modularity around hiding decisions that are likely to change, not around superficial decomposition. | [Parnas - On the Criteria To Be Used in Decomposing Systems into Modules](https://wstomv.win.tue.nl/edu/2ip30/references/criteria_for_modularization.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Arguably the clearest architecture-level commandment. |
| 66 | Hide volatile decisions behind stable module boundaries. | Information hiding protects the rest of the system from changes in data structures, algorithms, policies, and external mechanisms. | [Parnas - On the Criteria To Be Used in Decomposing Systems into Modules](https://wstomv.win.tue.nl/edu/2ip30/references/criteria_for_modularization.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Related to rule 24, but sharper about volatility. |
| 67 | Give each module one coherent responsibility and one primary reason to change. | SRP, Parnas, and structured design sources all point toward cohesion as a way to reduce mental load and change blast radius. | [Single Responsibility Principle](https://blog.cleancoder.com/uncle-bob/2014/05/08/SingleReponsibilityPrinciple.html) | 4 | 5 | "One reason" means one axis of change, not one tiny method. |
| 68 | Keep coupling explicit, minimal, and directional. | Structured design, Law of Demeter, and SEI architecture sources identify hidden, cyclic, or excessive dependencies as change risks. | [Structured Design](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.5555/1241515.1241533) | 5 | 5 | Some coupling is real domain knowledge; do not abstract it away blindly. |
| 69 | Choose architecture by explicit quality attributes and tradeoffs. | SEI and ISO quality models emphasize that modifiability, security, performance, reliability, usability, and testability can conflict. | [CMU SEI Software Architecture](https://www.sei.cmu.edu/software-architecture/) | 5 | 5 | There is no universally best architecture outside a quality-attribute context. |
| 70 | Depend on abstractions only when the abstraction is more stable than the implementation. | DIP-style guidance protects stable policy from volatile mechanisms, but unstable abstractions add needless ceremony. | [Design Principles and Design Patterns](https://www.fil.univ-lille.fr/~routier/enseignement/licence/coo/cours/Principles_and_Patterns.pdf) | 4 | 4 | This is the useful version of "depend on abstractions." |
| 71 | Shape interfaces around client needs. | Interface Segregation and information-hiding guidance warn that clients should not depend on operations or details they do not use. | [Design Principles and Design Patterns](https://www.fil.univ-lille.fr/~routier/enseignement/licence/coo/cours/Principles_and_Patterns.pdf) | 4 | 4 | Too many tiny interfaces can fragment comprehension. |
| 72 | Do not force callers to know your collaborators' internals. | The Law of Demeter reduces structural knowledge leaking across object boundaries. | [Law of Demeter - General Formulation](https://www2.ccs.neu.edu/research/demeter/demeter-method/LawOfDemeter/general-formulation.html) | 5 | 4 | Not a dot-counting exercise; fluent APIs and simple values may be fine. |
| 73 | Preserve substitutability when using inheritance or polymorphism. | LSP-style guidance says subtypes must honor base contracts so callers remain correct. | [Design Principles and Design Patterns](https://www.fil.univ-lille.fr/~routier/enseignement/licence/coo/cours/Principles_and_Patterns.pdf) | 4 | 4 | Composition is often cleaner, but inheritance is fine for true subtyping. |
| 74 | Separate concerns that require different reasoning or change independently. | Dijkstra, Parnas, and SRP all support separation as a tool for focus and independent evolution. | [Dijkstra - On the role of scientific thought](https://www.cs.utexas.edu/~EWD/transcriptions/EWD04xx/EWD447.html) | 5 | 4 | Separating by habit or technical layer can scatter one cohesive behavior. |
| 75 | Treat code smells as investigation prompts, not automatic verdicts. | Fowler's code-smell framing identifies likely design problems without making context-free verdicts. | [Martin Fowler - Code Smell](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/CodeSmell.html) | 5 | 4 | Smell means "look here carefully", not "rewrite immediately." |
| 76 | Every remote call must have a timeout. | AWS and SRE sources treat timeouts as basic protection against threads, connections, and callers waiting forever during partial failure. | [AWS Builders Library - Timeouts, retries, and backoff with jitter](https://builder.aws.com/content/3EumjoZascWd1oZiEgL8ORlv3qE/timeouts-retries-and-backoff-with-jitter) | 5 | 5 | Without timeouts, one slow dependency can consume the caller. |
| 77 | Retries must be bounded, backed off, jittered, and safe. | AWS, Google Cloud, and Stripe sources emphasize backoff with jitter and idempotency criteria to avoid retry storms and duplicate side effects. | [Google Cloud - Retry strategy](https://docs.cloud.google.com/storage/docs/retry-strategy) | 5 | 5 | Naive retries can amplify outages. |
| 78 | Make state-changing operations idempotent where clients may retry. | Stripe and AWS show idempotency keys and idempotent APIs as the way to safely recover from ambiguous failures. | [Stripe API - Idempotent requests](https://docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests) | 5 | 5 | Especially important for payments, provisioning, messaging, and external side effects. |
| 79 | Use standard protocols and explicit API contracts. | OpenAPI defines a language-agnostic interface that humans and machines can understand; Microsoft API guidance emphasizes contracts, versioning, and standard web patterns. | [OpenAPI Specification](https://spec.openapis.org/oas/v3.1.0.html) | 5 | 4 | More important for external APIs than internal-only functions, but the contract principle generalizes. |
| 80 | Do not break existing public APIs silently. | Microsoft Azure API guidance says customer workloads must not break due to a service change; SemVer similarly ties major versions to incompatible API changes. | [Microsoft Azure REST API Guidelines](https://github.com/microsoft/api-guidelines/blob/vNext/azure/Guidelines.md) | 5 | 5 | Compatibility is a user-facing correctness property. |
| 81 | Make errors structured, specific, and diagnosable. | Microsoft and Stripe API docs emphasize standard status codes and error details that clients can act on. | [Microsoft REST API Design Guidance](https://microsoft.github.io/code-with-engineering-playbook/design/design-patterns/rest-api-design-guidance/) | 5 | 5 | "Something went wrong" is not enough for operators, clients, or tests. |
| 82 | Model time, dates, and time zones as hostile domain data. | "Falsehoods Programmers Believe About Time" catalogs common assumptions that fail in real systems. | [Falsehoods Programmers Believe About Time](https://infiniteundo.com/post/25326999628/falsehoods-programmers-believe-about-time) | 4 | 5 | Use established time libraries and store/compare with explicit zones, offsets, and precision semantics. |
| 83 | Preserve data integrity with constraints and transactions. | PostgreSQL documentation defines integrity through constraints and transaction behavior, and warns against oversized or dangling transactions. | [PostgreSQL Transaction Isolation](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/transaction-iso.html) | 5 | 5 | Application checks alone are not enough under concurrency. |
| 84 | Keep transactions as small as integrity allows. | PostgreSQL warns not to put more into a transaction than needed and not to leave sessions idle in transaction. | [PostgreSQL Transaction Isolation](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/transaction-iso.html) | 5 | 4 | Long transactions hurt concurrency, operations, and recovery. |
| 85 | Evolve database schemas with repeatable, tested migrations. | Fowler's evolutionary database design applies CI and automated refactoring techniques to database design changes. | [Martin Fowler - Evolutionary Database Design](https://martinfowler.com/articles/evodb.html) | 5 | 5 | Database changes are code changes and need the same discipline. |
| 86 | Build accessible interfaces from semantic foundations. | WCAG organizes accessibility around perceivable, operable, understandable, and robust content; MDN recommends semantic HTML as the right foundation. | [W3C WCAG Overview](https://www.w3.org/WAI/standards-guidelines/wcag/) | 5 | 5 | Accessibility defects are user-facing defects. |
| 87 | Preserve baseline functionality before adding enhancements. | MDN and GOV.UK describe progressive enhancement as providing essential content/functionality broadly, then layering richer behavior. | [MDN - Progressive enhancement](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Glossary/Progressive_Enhancement) | 5 | 4 | Most applicable to frontend/web, but the graceful-baseline principle generalizes. |
| 88 | Collect, store, and expose only data you need. | NIST Privacy Framework and GDPR/data-protection-by-design guidance emphasize managing privacy risk and limiting data processing. | [NIST Privacy Framework](https://www.nist.gov/privacy-framework) | 5 | 5 | Data minimization reduces security, privacy, compliance, and operational risk. |
| 89 | Make privacy and data protection defaults, not user chores. | ICO and EDPB guidance describe data protection by design and by default as considering privacy from the start and implementing safeguards. | [ICO - Data protection by design and by default](https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/accountability-and-governance/guide-to-accountability-and-governance/data-protection-by-design-and-by-default/) | 5 | 5 | Mirrors secure-by-default for privacy-sensitive systems. |
| 90 | Produce artifacts with provenance. | SLSA defines supply-chain controls for provenance and tamper resistance; higher levels protect the build, provenance, and artifact. | [SLSA](https://slsa.dev/) | 5 | 4 | Most critical for packages, releases, deployments, and shared artifacts. |
| 91 | Keep an inventory of shipped components. | NIST describes SBOMs as formal records of components and relationships that improve transparency and vulnerability response. | [NIST - Software Bill of Materials](https://www.nist.gov/itl/executive-order-14028-improving-nations-cybersecurity/software-supply-chain-security-guidance-20) | 5 | 5 | You cannot patch or assess what you do not know you ship. |
| 92 | Protect the main branch and release path. | OpenSSF Scorecard checks branch protection because protected branches enforce review/status checks and prevent rewriting public history. | [OpenSSF Scorecard checks](https://github.com/ossf/scorecard/blob/main/docs/checks.md) | 4 | 4 | More repository-process than code, but directly protects code integrity. |
| 93 | Catch insecure dependencies before they enter the codebase. | GitHub dependency review surfaces insecure dependencies, license data, dependents, and age before introduction. | [GitHub Docs - Dependency review](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/concepts/supply-chain-security/dependency-review) | 5 | 5 | Prevention is cheaper than emergency remediation after release. |
| 94 | Optimize from measurements, not guesses. | Go and Rust profiling docs emphasize identifying expensive code paths before changing code for performance. | [Go - Profiling Go Programs](https://go.dev/blog/pprof) | 5 | 5 | Performance work without measurement easily worsens clarity while missing the bottleneck. |
| 95 | Do not spend complexity on noncritical micro-optimizations. | Knuth's optimization principle warns that early focus on small efficiencies harms debugging and maintenance. | [Donald Knuth - Structured Programming with go to Statements citation](https://lawsofsoftwareengineering.com/laws/premature-optimization/) | 3 | 4 | The caveat matters: do not ignore known performance requirements or hot paths. |
| 96 | Treat memory, CPU, file handles, sockets, and threads as finite resources. | Profiling and diagnostics docs frame performance in terms of memory, CPU, blocking, mutexes, goroutines, and expensive functions. | [Go Diagnostics](https://go.dev/doc/diagnostics) | 5 | 5 | Resource leaks and unbounded use become reliability failures. |
| 97 | Avoid data races; synchronize every shared mutable access. | Go's memory model and Rust concurrency guidance define data races around unsynchronized concurrent access involving writes and strongly encourage synchronization. | [Go Memory Model](https://go.dev/ref/mem) | 5 | 5 | Race bugs are often nondeterministic, data-corrupting, and production-only. |
| 98 | Minimize shared mutable state. | Rust's concurrency model and MIT thread-safety material point toward confinement, ownership, immutability, and explicit synchronization as safer patterns. | [Rust Blog - Fearless Concurrency](https://blog.rust-lang.org/2015/04/10/Fearless-Concurrency/) | 5 | 5 | Less sharing means less synchronization burden and fewer timing-dependent bugs. |
| 99 | Design lock ordering and synchronization ownership deliberately. | CERT concurrency rules include avoiding deadlock through predefined lock order and pairing synchronization acquisition/release at the same abstraction level. | [SEI CERT C Concurrency Rules](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/rules/concurrency-con/) | 5 | 5 | Deadlocks and races are design defects, not just implementation mistakes. |
| 100 | Make concurrent code observable and testable under stress. | Concurrency bugs depend on timing; diagnostics and profiling guidance recommend profiling, tracing, and synchronization-aware analysis to understand runtime behavior. | [Go Diagnostics](https://go.dev/doc/diagnostics) | 5 | 4 | Tests alone rarely prove concurrency correctness; combine design, tooling, stress tests, and observability. |
| 101 | Build release artifacts on protected CI, not developer workstations. | SLSA and GitHub supply-chain guidance use protected build infrastructure and attestations to make artifacts verifiable and tamper-resistant. | [SLSA v1.2 build requirements](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/build-requirements) | 5 | 5 | Local release builds are hard to audit, reproduce, and protect. |
| 102 | Commit lockfiles and use frozen installs in CI. | npm `package-lock`/`npm ci` and pip repeatable-install guidance make dependency resolution explicit and repeatable. | [npm package-lock](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v11/configuring-npm/package-lock-json/) | 5 | 5 | Floating dependency resolution creates nondeterministic builds. |
| 103 | Pin release and CI dependencies exactly, preferably by hash or immutable commit. | pip secure installs, OpenSSF Scorecard, and GitHub Actions guidance support exact versions/hashes and full-length commit SHAs. | [pip secure installs](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/topics/secure-installs/) | 5 | 5 | Tags and ranges can move or resolve differently later. |
| 104 | Grant CI tokens the minimum permissions required. | GitHub Actions security and OpenSSF checks recommend least-privilege workflow tokens and job-scoped writes only when needed. | [GitHub Actions secure use](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/secure-use) | 5 | 5 | CI compromise often becomes credential and release compromise. |
| 105 | Publish with short-lived trusted identity instead of long-lived registry tokens. | npm trusted publishing, PyPI attestations, and GitHub OIDC patterns reduce static secret exposure in release workflows. | [npm trusted publishing](https://docs.npmjs.com/trusted-publishers/) | 5 | 5 | Long-lived publish tokens are high-value secrets. |
| 106 | Make release artifacts, attestations, and SBOMs immutable. | SLSA provenance and verification guidance assumes artifacts can be verified against stable metadata; corrections should ship as new releases. | [SLSA v1.2 verifying artifacts](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/verifying-artifacts) | 5 | 4 | Mutating releases breaks reproducibility and downstream trust. |
| 107 | Publish a security policy and private vulnerability reporting path. | GitHub security policy guidance, OpenSSF checks, and NIST SSDF all support clear vulnerability intake and response. | [GitHub Docs - Add a security policy](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/how-tos/report-and-fix-vulnerabilities/configure-vulnerability-reporting/add-security-policy) | 5 | 4 | More project governance than code, but essential for shipped software. |
| 108 | Deny install-time scripts and unusual dependency sources unless explicitly justified. | npm and supply-chain guidance identify install scripts, remote URL dependencies, git dependencies, and local file dependencies as higher-risk inputs. | [npm package-lock](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v11/configuring-npm/package-lock-json/) | 5 | 4 | Useful default, but some ecosystems legitimately require controlled build scripts. |
| 109 | Prefer native semantic controls over custom widgets. | WCAG/MDN accessibility guidance makes semantic platform controls the default because they bring roles, keyboard behavior, names, values, and states. | [MDN ARIA](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Accessibility/ARIA) | 5 | 5 | If custom UI is unavoidable, you own the full accessibility contract. |
| 110 | Every interactive control must be keyboard reachable, operable, and visibly focused. | WCAG and MDN keyboard guidance treat keyboard access and visible focus as core accessibility requirements. | [MDN Keyboard Accessibility](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Accessibility/Guides/Understanding_WCAG/Keyboard) | 5 | 5 | Gesture-only controls exclude users and break automation. |
| 111 | Provide programmatic text alternatives for non-text meaning. | WCAG requires non-text content and icon-only controls to have accessible names or alternatives when they convey meaning or action. | [W3C WCAG 2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/) | 5 | 5 | Decorative content should be hidden, not noisily labeled. |
| 112 | Do not rely on color, position, motion, or pointer gestures alone. | WCAG principles require information and actions to remain perceivable and operable through more than one sensory or interaction channel. | [W3C WCAG 2.2 Quick Reference](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/quickref/) | 5 | 5 | Redundant text/state/keyboard alternatives make UI robust. |
| 113 | Forms need labels, instructions, text errors, and recovery paths. | WCAG and platform accessibility guidance require understandable inputs, error identification, correction, and protection for high-impact submissions. | [W3C WCAG 2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/) | 5 | 5 | Security-sensitive flows can limit hints, but must still be accessible. |
| 114 | Authentication must work with password managers, paste, and accessible alternatives. | Accessibility and privacy/security guidance warns against memory-only or puzzle-only authentication paths. | [OWASP Session Management Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Session_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | CAPTCHA-like checks require accessible alternatives and still carry UX/privacy risk. |
| 115 | Use Unicode/UTF-8 end-to-end and preserve language and direction metadata. | W3C internationalization guidance treats language and direction metadata as necessary for correct rendering, processing, and accessibility. | [W3C Internationalization Quick Tips](https://www.w3.org/International/quicktips/) | 5 | 5 | Applies to storage, APIs, logs, exports, search, and admin tools. |
| 116 | Treat locale as data, not string decoration. | MDN `Intl` and Microsoft globalization guidance require locale-aware formatting for dates, numbers, currency, sorting, pluralization, segmentation, names, and units. | [MDN JavaScript Internationalization](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Guide/Internationalization) | 5 | 5 | Protocol-fixed formats are different from human-facing formats. |
| 117 | Externalize human-facing strings and avoid concatenating translated fragments. | Microsoft message-formatting guidance supports named/reorderable placeholders and complete translatable messages. | [Microsoft Globalization - Message formatting](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/globalization/internationalization/message-formatting) | 5 | 5 | User-visible errors, emails, and UI text are part of the product. |
| 118 | Test UI with long strings, RTL, text scaling, and real locale formats. | Apple, Microsoft, and W3C internationalization guidance recommend validation against real internationalization and accessibility conditions. | [Microsoft Globalization - International validation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/globalization/testing/why-international-validation-is-important) | 5 | 4 | Test matrix should match supported markets and platforms. |
| 119 | Keep PII, secrets, tokens, session IDs, and raw request bodies out of logs. | OWASP logging and privacy guidance treats logs as data stores that need minimization, masking, access control, and retention. | [OWASP Logging Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Logging_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Correlation can use opaque IDs, hashing, or pseudonymization instead. |
| 120 | Avoid manipulative consent and privacy settings flows. | EDPB and privacy guidance warn against hidden controls, obstruction, confusing hierarchy, and steering users away from privacy-protective choices. | [EDPB Article 25 Guidance](https://www.edpb.europa.eu/documents/guideline/guidelines-42019-on-article-25-data-protection-by-design-and-by-default_en) | 5 | 5 | A privacy choice that is hard to find or understand is not a real choice. |
| 121 | Use precise, inclusive names in code, APIs, docs, and UI. | Google/Microsoft style guidance and the Inclusive Naming Initiative discourage harmful or misleading terms such as master/slave and whitelist/blacklist. | [Google Inclusive Documentation](https://developers.google.com/style/inclusive-documentation) | 4 | 4 | Existing public APIs need migration plans and compatibility windows. |
| 122 | Validate every external boundary against an explicit contract. | JSON Schema, OpenAPI, and OWASP guidance support schema/contract validation for shape, required fields, formats, and safe binding. | [JSON Schema](https://json-schema.org/) | 5 | 5 | Decide explicitly whether unknown fields are rejected, ignored, or preserved. |
| 123 | Treat data schemas as public contracts. | API and serialization guidance treat removing fields, changing types, changing requiredness, or reusing field identifiers as breaking changes. | [Protocol Buffers - Updating a message type](https://protobuf.dev/programming-guides/proto3/#updating) | 5 | 5 | Applies to events, database contracts, files, messages, and APIs. |
| 124 | Gate schema changes with compatibility checks. | Confluent schema evolution guidance uses backward, forward, full, and transitive compatibility modes based on producer/consumer rollout order. | [Confluent Schema Evolution](https://docs.confluent.io/platform/current/schema-registry/fundamentals/schema-evolution.html) | 5 | 4 | Pick compatibility mode deliberately; "compatible with previous" is not always transitive. |
| 125 | Use expand/migrate/contract for live migrations. | Fowler's parallel change and evolutionary database design patterns support adding compatible structure, migrating safely, switching use, then removing old structure later. | [Martin Fowler - Parallel Change](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/ParallelChange.html) | 5 | 5 | Smaller offline systems can use simpler migration windows. |
| 126 | Respect HTTP method semantics. | RFC 9110 defines safe and idempotent method semantics; API design guidance relies on those semantics for clients, caches, and retries. | [RFC 9110 HTTP Semantics](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110) | 5 | 5 | `GET` must not perform unsafe actions; `POST`/`PATCH` need explicit retry semantics. |
| 127 | Define stable machine-readable error codes. | RFC 9457 Problem Details and API guidelines support structured errors with stable codes, field targets, and retryability metadata. | [RFC 9457 Problem Details](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9457) | 5 | 5 | Human messages are not a safe client branching API. |
| 128 | Serialize for evolution, not just today's parser. | JSON, Protobuf, and schema-registry guidance warn against ambiguous values and incompatible field evolution. | [RFC 8259 JSON](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259) | 5 | 5 | Avoid ambiguous numbers for money/IDs; reserve removed Protobuf fields; keep JSON object names unique. |
| 129 | Prevent lost updates with version witnesses. | HTTP conditional requests and API concurrency guidance support ETags/version fields for resources edited concurrently. | [RFC 9110 HTTP Semantics](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110) | 5 | 4 | Last-writer-wins is acceptable only when it is explicit product behavior. |
| 130 | Distinguish instants from civil time. | RFC 3339 and IANA TZDB make clear that timestamps with offsets and time-zone IDs solve different problems. | [RFC 3339 Date and Time](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3339) | 5 | 5 | UTC alone is insufficient for future local scheduled events affected by DST or law changes. |
| 131 | Define Unicode equality and identifier policy explicitly. | Unicode normalization and security guidance warn about canonical equivalence, confusables, and identifier spoofing. | [Unicode UAX #15 Normalization](https://www.unicode.org/reports/tr15/) | 5 | 4 | Normalization can be wrong for some fields; do not normalize everything blindly. |
| 132 | Emit data-change events transactionally. | Transactional outbox guidance solves the problem of keeping database writes and message/event publication consistent. | [Microservices.io - Transactional Outbox](https://microservices.io/patterns/data/transactional-outbox.html) | 4 | 4 | Consumers still need idempotency because relays can duplicate events. |
| 133 | Every mutation needs safe observability. | OpenTelemetry and API/data guidance support actor/request/trace/resource/operation/result metadata, while privacy/security sources constrain sensitive data exposure. | [OpenTelemetry](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/) | 5 | 4 | Audit and trace data need retention, access control, and PII safeguards. |
| 134 | Keep control flow analyzable. | NASA/JPL's Power of Ten bans complex control-flow constructs such as `goto`, long jumps, and recursion in safety-critical C to make review and static analysis reliable. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Language and domain matter, but analyzable control flow is a general quality target. |
| 135 | Bound loops and resource growth where failure matters. | Power of Ten requires fixed loop bounds for critical code so tools can prove execution limits. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Generalizes to backpressure, quotas, pagination, bounded queues, and defensive limits. |
| 136 | Avoid dynamic allocation in critical runtime paths unless it is explicitly bounded and handled. | Power of Ten forbids dynamic memory allocation after initialization in safety-critical code; MISRA-like rules restrict error-prone constructs. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Too strict for many apps, but useful for embedded, realtime, and reliability-critical paths. |
| 137 | Declare state at the smallest useful scope. | Power of Ten requires data objects at the smallest possible scope, reducing accidental coupling and making analysis easier. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Applies broadly: minimize lifetime and visibility of mutable state. |
| 138 | Use assertions for impossible states and invariant violations. | Power of Ten recommends assertion density for anomalous conditions that should not occur, with explicit recovery action on failure. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Assertions must be side-effect free and not replace user/input validation. |
| 139 | Check return values and validate parameters at trust boundaries. | Power of Ten requires callers to check nonvoid return values and callees to validate parameters; CERT and OWASP echo error/input discipline. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Silent ignored failures are a common root of later corruption. |
| 140 | Restrict unsafe or hard-to-analyze language features. | MISRA and Power of Ten restrict constructs that create undefined behavior, ambiguity, pointer hazards, macro hazards, and analysis blind spots. | [MISRA Compliance 2020](https://misra.org.uk/app/uploads/2021/06/MISRA-Compliance-2020.pdf) | 5 | 4 | The exact forbidden set is language-specific; the principle is universal. |
| 141 | Use current weakness data to focus secure coding effort. | CWE Top 25 ranks dangerous weaknesses such as XSS, SQL injection, CSRF, missing authorization, and out-of-bounds writes. | [MITRE CWE Top 25](https://cwe.mitre.org/top25/) | 5 | 5 | Security priorities should track real exploited and prevalent weakness classes. |
| 142 | Treat high-churn code as risky code. | Empirical studies show change history, churn, and change complexity repeatedly predict fault-proneness. | [Predicting Fault Incidence Using Software Change History](https://www.niss.org/sites/default/files/technicalreports/tr80.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Churn is a risk proxy, not proof of bad design. |
| 143 | Require meaningful code review coverage and participation. | Empirical studies link low review coverage and low reviewer participation with more post-release defects. | [Impact of Code Review Coverage and Participation on Software Quality](https://posl.ait.kyushu-u.ac.jp/~kamei/publications/McIntosh_MSR2014.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Rubber-stamp review does not satisfy this rule. |
| 144 | Use code review for shared understanding, not only bug hunting. | Microsoft and Google review studies found review supports knowledge transfer, maintainability, and shared ownership beyond defect detection. | [Expectations, Outcomes, and Challenges of Modern Code Review](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2016/02/ICSE202013-codereview.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Review processes can become slow if scope and standards are unclear. |
| 145 | Keep tests direct, focused, and assertion-clear. | Test-smell studies found smelly tests reduce comprehension and correlate with change/defect-proneness. | [Are Test Smells Really Harmful?](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10664-014-9313-0) | 5 | 5 | Evidence is strongest in Java/JUnit but the maintainability principle generalizes. |
| 146 | Enforce static analysis only when warnings are actionable and high-signal. | Google static-analysis studies found tool adoption fails when warnings are noisy, poorly integrated, or unactionable. | [Why Don't Software Developers Use Static Analysis Tools?](https://research.google/pubs/why-dont-software-developers-use-static-analysis-tools-to-find-bugs/) | 5 | 5 | Alert fatigue makes teams ignore real problems. |
| 147 | Let formatters handle layout; do not spend human review on formatting. | Empirical indentation/readability studies support consistent layout, and industry practice uses formatters to remove style churn. | [Program Indentation and Comprehensibility](https://cacm.acm.org/research/program-indentation-and-comprehensibility/) | 4 | 4 | The exact indentation style matters less than consistency and automation. |
| 148 | Use metrics as risk signals, not design truth. | Empirical understandability and defect-prediction research shows individual metrics are imperfect and context-dependent. | [Automatically Assessing Code Understandability](https://www.cs.wm.edu/~denys/pubs/TSE%2719-Understandability.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Hard thresholds encourage metric gaming. |
| 149 | Track clones when duplicated logic is intentionally retained. | Clone studies show duplication mainly becomes dangerous when copies that should change together diverge. | [Do Code Clones Matter?](https://teamscale.com/hubfs/Publications/2009-do-code-clones-matter.pdf) | 4 | 4 | Some duplication remains cheaper than premature abstraction. |
| 150 | Distinguish recoverable errors from programmer bugs. | Rust and .NET guidance distinguish expected/recoverable errors from panics/crashes; undefined behavior is worse than a clear failure. | [Rust API Guidelines - Documentation](https://rust-lang.github.io/api-guidelines/documentation.html) | 5 | 5 | Do not turn invariant violations into silent success paths. |
| 151 | Never ignore an error without an explicit reason. | Go's error-handling guidance says errors are values and still must be checked. | [Go Blog - Errors are values](https://go.dev/blog/errors-are-values) | 5 | 5 | If the error is intentionally ignored, document why it is safe. |
| 152 | Preserve error context when propagating failures. | .NET exception guidance recommends proper capture/rethrow and restoring state when methods fail; API error guidance favors structured diagnostic context. | [Microsoft .NET exception best practices](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/standard/exceptions/best-practices-for-exceptions) | 5 | 5 | Do not leak secrets in error context. |
| 153 | Do not expose internal details in user-facing errors. | OWASP says error responses should not disclose system details, stack traces, session IDs, or account information. | [OWASP Secure Coding Practices Checklist](https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-coding-practices-quick-reference-guide/stable-en/02-checklist/05-checklist) | 5 | 5 | Operators still need private diagnostic detail through logs/traces. |
| 154 | Release resources on every exit path. | Java try-with-resources and Go defer guidance show language mechanisms for reliable cleanup when exceptions, returns, or panics happen. | [Oracle Java Tutorial - try-with-resources](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/tutorial/essential/exceptions/tryResourceClose.html) | 5 | 5 | Cleanup itself can fail; account for suppressed/secondary errors. |
| 155 | Document public API failures, panics, safety requirements, and examples. | Rust API documentation conventions require sections for errors, panics, safety, and examples where relevant. | [Rust RFC 1574 - API documentation conventions](https://github.com/rust-lang/rfcs/blob/master/text/1574-more-api-documentation-conventions.md) | 5 | 5 | Good API docs define the contract, not just names and types. |
| 156 | Use documentation forms that match user needs. | Diataxis separates tutorials, how-to guides, reference, and explanation because each serves a different reader task. | [Diataxis](https://diataxis.fr/) | 5 | 4 | Not every project needs every doc type, but mixing them creates confusing docs. |
| 157 | Record consequential architecture decisions with context and consequences. | ADR guidance uses short records with status, context, decision, and consequences to preserve why a design choice was made. | [Michael Nygard - Documenting Architecture Decisions](https://www.cognitect.com/blog/2011/11/15/documenting-architecture-decisions) | 5 | 4 | ADRs should be short and maintained, not a parallel bureaucracy. |
| 158 | Update docs and changelogs when behavior changes. | Google review guidance asks whether relevant documentation changed; Keep a Changelog frames release notes as human-readable change history. | [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) | 4 | 4 | Docs that drift become misinformation. |
| 159 | Use semantic conventions for telemetry. | OpenTelemetry semantic conventions define common names for operations and data across traces, metrics, logs, profiles, and resources. | [OpenTelemetry Semantic Conventions](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/concepts/semantic-conventions/) | 5 | 4 | Consistent telemetry names make cross-service debugging possible. |
| 160 | Monitor user-visible health, not just machine internals. | Google SRE monitoring guidance focuses on signals such as latency, errors, traffic, and saturation for distributed systems. | [Google SRE - Monitoring Distributed Systems](https://sre.google/sre-book/monitoring-distributed-systems/) | 5 | 5 | Internal metrics are useful only when tied to user and service outcomes. |
| 161 | Keep development, staging, and production behavior as similar as practical. | Twelve-Factor dev/prod parity reduces surprises caused by divergent environments and deployment processes. | [The Twelve-Factor App](https://12factor.net/) | 4 | 4 | Some external integrations need safe test doubles, but drift should be deliberate. |
| 162 | Make processes disposable: fast startup, graceful shutdown, and safe termination. | Twelve-Factor disposable-process guidance expects processes to start quickly and shut down gracefully for robust deploys and scaling. | [The Twelve-Factor App](https://12factor.net/) | 4 | 4 | Essential for services; less relevant to one-shot scripts. |
| 163 | Validate configuration at startup. | Twelve-Factor config separation implies deploy-varying config must be external; operational reliability requires invalid config to fail early. | [The Twelve-Factor App - Config](https://12factor.net/config) | 4 | 5 | Failing at startup is better than running with half-valid settings. |
| 164 | Keep unsafe code small, justified, documented, and tested. | Rust unsafe guidance says `unsafe` is harder because the compiler cannot uphold memory safety; explicit unsafe blocks make risky code traceable. | [Rust Book - Unsafe Rust](https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/ch20-01-unsafe-rust.html) | 5 | 5 | Generalizes to FFI, raw pointers, reflection, eval, and privileged APIs. |
| 165 | Do not deserialize untrusted objects. | OWASP deserialization guidance says safe architecture avoids accepting serialized objects from untrusted sources or uses data-only formats and allowlists. | [OWASP Deserialization Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Deserialization_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Treat deserialization as code execution risk in many ecosystems. |
| 166 | Propagate cancellation through abandoned work. | Go context/pipelines and Java interruption/Future guidance treat cancellation as cooperative work that code must observe. | [Go Context package](https://pkg.go.dev/context) | 5 | 5 | Timeouts are incomplete if the abandoned work keeps running. |
| 167 | Bound concurrency, queues, and in-flight work. | SRE overload guidance, Go pipelines, and Java ThreadPoolExecutor sources warn that unbounded fan-out and queues convert latency into resource exhaustion. | [Google SRE - Handling Overload](https://sre.google/sre-book/handling-overload/) | 5 | 5 | Bounded systems need explicit overload behavior. |
| 168 | Shed load before collapse. | Google SRE cascading-failure guidance treats overload protection and degraded modes as ways to preserve core service behavior. | [Google SRE - Handling Overload](https://sre.google/sre-book/handling-overload/) | 5 | 5 | Degraded paths need tests and metrics or they rot. |
| 169 | Account for every spawned task, thread, or goroutine. | Go pipeline and Java ExecutorService guidance require lifecycle management through cancellation, joins, executor shutdown, or deliberate external ownership. | [Go Blog - Pipelines and cancellation](https://go.dev/blog/pipelines) | 5 | 5 | "Fire and forget" is usually a leak unless lifecycle is deliberately external. |
| 170 | Use synchronization primitives by their documented protocol. | POSIX condition variable guidance requires predicate loops because spurious wakeups and timeout races are real. | [pthread_cond_wait POSIX manual](https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/pthread_cond_wait.3p.html) | 5 | 5 | Locks, atomics, channels, condition variables, and futures each have protocols. |
| 171 | Check cleanup errors when durability can fail late. | `close(2)` warns that errors from previous writes may be reported only on final close, and that retrying close can be unsafe due to descriptor reuse. | [man7 close(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/close.2.html) | 5 | 4 | Use `fsync` first when durability matters; platform behavior differs. |
| 172 | Do not invalidate shared resources while another thread may use them. | `close(2)` and concurrency guidance warn that file descriptor reuse and concurrent resource invalidation can cause subtle cross-thread bugs. | [man7 close(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/close.2.html) | 5 | 5 | Applies to files, sockets, handles, connections, memory, and shared lifecycle state. |
| 173 | Keep docs accurate or delete them. | Google documentation guidance and docs-as-code practice value maintained, reviewed docs over stale volume. | [Google Documentation Best Practices](https://google.github.io/styleguide/docguide/best_practices.html) | 5 | 5 | Stale docs are active misinformation; archive only when history/compliance requires it. |
| 174 | Use comments to explain intent, contracts, invariants, and surprises. | Google and language style guides warn against comments that merely restate obvious code and favor comments for non-obvious decisions and reader context. | [Google C++ Style Guide - Comments](https://google.github.io/styleguide/cppguide.html#Comments) | 5 | 5 | Public API reference comments still need to state observable behavior. |
| 175 | Public APIs must document parameters, returns, failures, deprecations, and examples. | Google API reference comments, Rust documentation conventions, and Swift API guidance all treat API docs as part of the contract. | [Google API Reference Comments](https://developers.google.com/style/api-reference-comments) | 5 | 5 | Private helpers can be lighter when names and types are enough. |
| 176 | Treat an API that is hard to describe simply as design feedback. | Swift and Google API documentation guidance both connect clear description at the point of use with better interface design. | [Swift API Design Guidelines](https://www.swift.org/documentation/api-design-guidelines/) | 5 | 5 | Some domains are inherently complex, but docs should expose the complexity honestly. |
| 177 | Make the README an orientation map. | GitHub README guidance frames the README as the entry point explaining what the project does, why it is useful, how to start, and where to get help. | [GitHub Docs - About READMEs](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-readmes) | 5 | 5 | Deep manuals and generated references belong elsewhere and should be linked. |
| 178 | Keep changelogs human-curated and explicit about breaking changes. | Keep a Changelog recommends curated, human-readable release notes grouped by change type, with deprecations and breaking changes called out. | [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) | 4 | 5 | A dumped commit log is not a useful changelog. |
| 179 | Declare the public API before promising compatibility. | SemVer depends on a declared public API; without that boundary, version numbers cannot reliably communicate compatibility. | [Semantic Versioning 2.0.0](https://semver.org/) | 5 | 4 | Most important for libraries, protocols, SDKs, and consumed services. |
| 180 | Keep evergreen documentation timeless. | Google documentation style warns against time-sensitive words such as "new", "currently", and "latest" in long-lived docs. | [Google Timeless Documentation](https://developers.google.com/style/timeless-documentation) | 5 | 4 | Release notes, migration guides, and blog posts can intentionally be time-bound. |
| 181 | Treat docs as code where practical. | Write the Docs recommends plain text, version control, review, and automation for documentation maintained alongside code. | [Write the Docs - Docs as Code](https://www.writethedocs.org/guide/docs-as-code/) | 4 | 4 | Heavier workflows may not fit all contributor groups, but review and versioning matter. |
| 182 | Make examples minimal, realistic, copy-pasteable, and checked. | Google code-sample guidance emphasizes focused, correct, runnable examples that avoid distracting boilerplate. | [Google Code Samples](https://developers.google.com/style/code-samples) | 5 | 4 | Untested examples rot quickly; use doc tests or sample builds when available. |
| 183 | Prefer linking to canonical guidance over duplicating it. | Google documentation best practices favor avoiding duplicated material that can drift from the source of truth. | [Google Documentation Best Practices](https://google.github.io/styleguide/docguide/best_practices.html) | 5 | 4 | Short summaries are fine when they reduce reader friction. |
| 184 | TODO comments need an owner, issue, and removal condition. | Google Python style guidance requires TODOs to include context such as an owner or bug reference rather than vague indefinite reminders. | [Google Python Style Guide - Comments and Docstrings](https://google.github.io/styleguide/pyguide.html#38-comments-and-docstrings) | 5 | 4 | Prefer an issue tracker for work management and keep inline TODOs rare. |
| 185 | Make recoverable failure explicit in the API contract. | Go, Rust, Java, .NET, RFC 9457, and Google API guidance all expose expected failures through explicit return/error/exception contracts. | [Go Blog - Error handling and Go](https://go.dev/blog/error-handling-and-go) | 5 | 5 | The mechanism differs by language; the contract must still be visible. |
| 186 | Catch only failures you can actually handle. | .NET and CERT guidance warn against broad catch blocks that swallow errors without recovery or context-appropriate propagation. | [Microsoft .NET exception best practices](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/standard/exceptions/best-practices-for-exceptions) | 5 | 5 | Top-level handlers are still useful for logging, generic responses, and orderly shutdown. |
| 187 | Add error context without destroying the cause chain. | Go wrapping, .NET rethrow guidance, and structured API error standards preserve machine-checkable identity while adding useful context. | [Go Blog - Working with Errors in Go 1.13](https://go.dev/blog/go1.13-errors) | 5 | 5 | Sanitize context at trust boundaries so secrets and internals do not leak. |
| 188 | Do not use exceptions or panics for normal control flow. | .NET says not to use exceptions for ordinary flow; Go and Rust reserve panic-like mechanisms for abnormal or unrecoverable states. | [Microsoft .NET exception best practices](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/standard/exceptions/best-practices-for-exceptions) | 5 | 4 | Returning `Result` or an error value for expected invalid input is normal in some languages. |
| 189 | Restore invariants and roll back partial work on failure. | .NET and CERT guidance require objects and operations to preserve valid state after exceptions; transactional systems should fail atomically where possible. | [SEI CERT Java ERR rules](https://wiki.sei.cmu.edu/confluence/display/java/Exceptional+Behavior+%28ERR%29) | 4 | 5 | Requires explicit design for transactions, idempotency, and cleanup ordering. |
| 190 | Fail closed for security decisions. | OWASP fail-secure guidance says a failed security control must not grant access or bypass protection. | [OWASP Fail Securely](https://owasp.org/www-community/Fail_securely) | 4 | 5 | Availability degradation is acceptable only if privilege and data boundaries still hold. |
| 191 | Prefer containment and supervised restart over heroic recovery from unknown state. | Erlang/OTP supervision principles model faults as isolated child failures that can be restarted by supervisors. | [Erlang/OTP Supervision Principles](https://www.erlang.org/doc/system/sup_princ.html) | 5 | 4 | Best fit for services, workers, actors, jobs, and processes, not tiny pure functions. |
| 192 | Emit traces, metrics, and structured logs for production behavior. | OpenTelemetry defines vendor-neutral telemetry for traces, metrics, and logs; operational guidance treats observability as core service behavior. | [OpenTelemetry Documentation](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/) | 5 | 5 | Auto-instrumentation is useful but does not replace domain-level signals. |
| 193 | Give every service stable telemetry identity. | OpenTelemetry resource guidance uses attributes such as service name, version, deployment environment, and cloud/container metadata to identify telemetry sources. | [OpenTelemetry Resources](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/concepts/resources/) | 5 | 5 | Default service names are often useless; set them deliberately. |
| 194 | Propagate trace context across service and async boundaries. | OpenTelemetry context propagation carries trace identity through calls and asynchronous work so distributed behavior can be reconstructed. | [OpenTelemetry Context Propagation](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/concepts/context-propagation/) | 5 | 5 | Propagated context is metadata, not a safe place for secrets. |
| 195 | Keep telemetry attributes bounded and low-cardinality. | OpenTelemetry semantic conventions and metrics guidance warn that names and attributes need consistency and bounded cardinality to remain useful and affordable. | [OpenTelemetry Metrics Semantic Conventions](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/semconv/general/metrics/) | 5 | 5 | Do not put user IDs, raw paths, or unbounded values in metric labels. |
| 196 | Write logs as structured event streams, not private log files. | Twelve-Factor logs guidance treats logs as event streams written to stdout/stderr for the runtime or collector to route. | [The Twelve-Factor App - Logs](https://12factor.net/logs) | 4 | 4 | Local development may still use files; production apps should not own log routing. |
| 197 | Alert on user-facing symptoms and actionable SLO burn, not metric noise. | Google SRE monitoring and alerting guidance focuses on user-visible service health and actionable pages. | [Google SRE - Monitoring Distributed Systems](https://sre.google/sre-book/monitoring-distributed-systems/) | 5 | 5 | Low-traffic systems need careful windows to avoid false precision. |
| 198 | Expose separate readiness, liveness, and startup health checks when orchestrated. | Kubernetes distinguishes probes that decide traffic routing, restart behavior, and slow startup protection. | [Kubernetes - Liveness, Readiness, and Startup Probes](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/liveness-readiness-startup-probes/) | 5 | 5 | Liveness should not fail just because a downstream dependency is temporarily unhealthy. |
| 199 | Build once, release immutably, and attach config at release time. | Twelve-Factor build/release/run guidance separates code build, deploy-time config, and runtime execution. | [The Twelve-Factor App - Build, release, run](https://12factor.net/build-release-run) | 4 | 4 | Dynamic config is fine when typed, audited, validated, and deliberately designed. |
| 200 | Deploy small, automated, reversible changes with telemetry-gated rollout. | Google SRE canarying and release guidance use staged exposure, automation, and monitoring to limit blast radius. | [Google SRE Workbook - Canarying Releases](https://sre.google/workbook/canarying-releases/) | 5 | 5 | Rollback automation is only as good as the signal quality. |
| 201 | Feature flags need types, defaults, targeting context, and defined error behavior. | OpenFeature standardizes flag evaluation concepts such as provider behavior, evaluation context, hooks, and typed values. | [OpenFeature Specification](https://openfeature.dev/specification/) | 5 | 4 | Avoid vendor lock-in and validate rollout keys for deterministic targeting. |
| 202 | Every temporary feature flag needs an owner and removal plan. | Feature-flag lifecycle guidance treats stale flags as operational and cognitive debt. | [LaunchDarkly - Technical debt from feature flags](https://launchdarkly.com/docs/guides/flags/technical-debt) | 3 | 4 | Vendor source, but the debt pattern is widely observed. |
| 203 | Use kill switches for risky non-core integrations. | Feature-management and operational guidance support fast disablement of risky paths during incidents. | [AWS AppConfig Feature Flags](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/appconfig/latest/userguide/appconfig-creating-configuration-and-profile-feature-flags.html) | 4 | 4 | The kill switch must be simpler and more reliable than the failing integration. |
| 204 | Gracefully shut down: stop accepting work, drain, release, and exit on time. | Kubernetes pod termination and Twelve-Factor disposability require services to handle termination signals and stop safely. | [Kubernetes - Pod Termination](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/pod-lifecycle/#pod-termination) | 5 | 5 | Test shutdown under the real orchestrator and timeout. |
| 205 | Treat observability pipeline configuration as production code. | OpenTelemetry Collector configuration controls receivers, processors, exporters, filtering, batching, sampling, and redaction. | [OpenTelemetry Collector](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/collector/) | 5 | 4 | Pipeline changes can drop data, explode cost, or leak sensitive data. |
| 206 | Keep changes reviewable as one conceptual change. | Google's small-CL guidance and review research favor small, focused changes that reviewers can understand and safely approve. | [Google Engineering Practices - Small CLs](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/developer/small-cls.html) | 5 | 5 | Generated code, mechanical renames, and deletions may be large but still reviewable. |
| 207 | Review slowly enough to think. | Code inspection research shows defect detection drops when reviewers inspect too much code too quickly or for too long. | [Kemerer and Paulk - Impact of Design and Code Reviews](https://sites.pitt.edu/~ckemerer/PSP_Data.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Numeric limits are review triggers, not universal law. |
| 208 | Use cognitive complexity as a readability alarm. | Sonar's Cognitive Complexity metric weights nesting and flow breaks in ways intended to match human comprehension better than path counts alone. | [SonarSource Cognitive Complexity](https://www.sonarsource.com/docs/CognitiveComplexity.pdf) | 4 | 4 | Treat metrics as prompts for judgment, not automatic verdicts. |
| 209 | Use cyclomatic complexity as a path and test-risk signal. | McCabe's metric measures independent control-flow paths and remains useful for testing and branch-risk awareness. | [McCabe - A Complexity Measure](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1109/TSE.1976.233837) | 5 | 4 | It does not capture all human readability costs. |
| 210 | Flatten control flow where it preserves clarity. | Cognitive-complexity and readability studies identify nesting as a major comprehension cost. | [Empirical Validation of Cognitive Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.12520) | 5 | 5 | Some domain logic is naturally hierarchical; do not flatten into obscurity. |
| 211 | Keep functions small enough to understand as one unit. | Method-size research, Google style guidance, and safety-critical rules all treat very large functions as maintainability and review risks. | [Maintainable Method Size in Java](https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~rtholmes/papers/msr_2022_chowdhury.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Extraction can hurt locality if it creates needless indirection. |
