| 212 | Limit live local state and parameter load. | Working-memory research and coding-style guidance support treating too many simultaneously relevant locals or parameters as a comprehension smell. | [Cowan - The Magical Number 4 in Short-Term Memory](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/behavioral-and-brain-sciences/article/magical-number-4-in-shortterm-memory-a-reconsideration-of-mental-storage-capacity/44023F1147D4A1D44BDC0AD226838496) | 5 | 4 | Do not turn memory-capacity research into a literal variable-count law. |
| 213 | Split large classes by responsibility, not arbitrary line count. | Code-smell research links large, responsibility-mixed classes with comprehension and maintainability problems. | [Palomba et al. - Code smells and maintainability](https://fpalomba.github.io/pdf/Journals/J9.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Metrics should trigger review; responsibility boundaries decide design. |
| 214 | Prefer regular, explicit, idiomatic code over clever compactness. | Confusing-code research shows small legal constructs can reliably mislead readers even when behavior is correct. | [Thinking Aloud About Confusing Code](https://ssl.engineering.nyu.edu/papers/gopstein_thinking_fse2020.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Ban surprise, not expertise; established local idioms can reduce load. |
| 215 | Treat layout as cognition support. | Readability research and style guides connect consistent layout, naming, spacing, and line width with comprehension. | [Learning a Metric for Code Readability](https://web.eecs.umich.edu/~weimerw/p/weimer-tse2010-readability-preprint.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Let formatters enforce layout so reviews can focus on behavior. |
| 216 | Prefer memory-safe languages and runtimes for new risky code. | NSA/CISA guidance says memory-safe languages reduce reliance on programmer discipline for classes of vulnerabilities common in systems code. | [NSA/CISA - Memory Safe Languages](https://media.defense.gov/2025/Jun/23/2003742198/-1/-1/0/CSI_MEMORY_SAFE_LANGUAGES_REDUCING_VULNERABILITIES_IN_MODERN_SOFTWARE_DEVELOPMENT.PDF) | 5 | 5 | Especially strong for parsers, codecs, network-facing code, crypto, and privileged components. |
| 217 | Treat undefined behavior as a security bug. | SEI CERT and Rust reference guidance warn that undefined behavior invalidates program reasoning and can create exploitable outcomes. | [SEI CERT MSC15-C](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/recommendations/miscellaneous-msc/msc15-c/) | 5 | 5 | Strongest for C, C++, Rust unsafe, FFI, and compiler-sensitive low-level code. |
| 218 | Model absence, failure, and state explicitly in types. | Rust, Kotlin, TypeScript, and C# guidance separate nullable/optional/fallible states from ordinary values to make unsafe assumptions visible. | [Kotlin Null Safety](https://kotlinlang.org/docs/null-safety.html) | 5 | 5 | Static checks help most; runtime validation is still needed at external boundaries. |
| 219 | Do not use null or sentinel values as ordinary control flow. | Null-safety guidance across Kotlin, TypeScript, and C# shows the value of distinguishing present, absent, invalid, and failed states. | [TypeScript strictNullChecks](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/strictNullChecks.html) | 5 | 5 | Migration can be expensive; phase it with compiler/linter support. |
| 220 | Express ownership and lifetime in the type or API. | Rust ownership and C++ Core Guidelines both make ownership transfer, borrowing, and cleanup responsibility explicit. | [Rust Book - Understanding Ownership](https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/ch04-00-understanding-ownership.html) | 5 | 5 | Raw references, pointers, and handles should be presumed non-owning unless declared otherwise. |
| 221 | Validate invariants after construction, parsing, and deserialization. | OWASP deserialization and language security guidance treat object construction from external data as a point where invalid or dangerous states can enter. | [MITRE CWE-502](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/502.html) | 5 | 5 | Better yet, make invalid states unrepresentable after parsing. |
| 222 | Do not silence safety systems casually. | Null-forgiving operators, unchecked casts, `any`, raw pointer escapes, unsafe blocks, and unchecked unwraps are safety escape hatches that bypass normal guarantees. | [C# Nullable Reference Types](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/fundamentals/null-safety/nullable-reference-types) | 5 | 5 | When justified, require a local proof, narrow scope, tests, and review. |
| 223 | Prefer secure-by-default APIs over warning-only documentation. | Secure-by-design guidance and safe language APIs reduce the burden on every caller by making dangerous behavior explicit opt-in. | [CISA Secure by Design](https://www.cisa.gov/securebydesign) | 5 | 5 | Library and framework code should make the safe path the easy path. |
| 224 | Avoid foreseeable harm in code, design, and release decisions. | ACM ethics explicitly frames avoiding harm as a professional computing duty; unsafe code quality decisions can harm users, data, property, and public trust. | [ACM Code of Ethics](https://www.acm.org/code-of-ethics) | 5 | 5 | Harm includes security, privacy, accessibility, reliability, misuse, and operational failure. |
| 225 | Be honest about software limitations, risks, and uncertainty. | ACM and IEEE/ACM ethics require honesty, trustworthy communication, and realistic claims about software work and its consequences. | [Software Engineering Code of Ethics](https://www.acm.org/code-of-ethics/software-engineering-code) | 5 | 5 | Applies to docs, release notes, test claims, estimates, error states, and known defects. |
| 226 | Surface material safety, security, and reliability risks to the right people. | IEEE/ACM ethics call for public-interest action and disclosure of actual or potential danger to users, the public, or the environment. | [IEEE Computer Society Code of Ethics](https://www.computer.org/education/code-of-ethics) | 5 | 5 | Escalation should be documented and proportional; do not bury known high-impact risks in implementation details. |
| 227 | Give every reusable codebase an explicit license. | GitHub licensing guidance and Choose a License both warn that sharing source without a license does not grant normal open-source reuse rights. | [GitHub Docs - Licensing a repository](https://docs.github.com/articles/licensing-a-repository) | 5 | 5 | This is an engineering hygiene rule, not legal advice; proprietary/internal repos still need explicit usage boundaries. |
| 228 | Track provenance for third-party code, assets, data, and generated output. | SPDX models SBOMs as package/component records including composition, provenance, licensing, and related risk data. | [SPDX Specification](https://spdx.github.io/spdx-spec/v3.0.1/) | 5 | 5 | "I copied it from somewhere" is not enough to ship, audit, update, or remove it later. |
| 229 | Make license and copyright metadata machine-readable. | REUSE and SPDX provide conventions for unambiguous, machine-readable licensing across files and packages. | [REUSE](https://reuse.software/) | 5 | 4 | Especially useful in mixed-license repos, vendored code, generated code, examples, docs, and assets. |
| 230 | Preserve required notices and attribution when redistributing code. | Common licenses such as MIT and Apache-2.0 impose notice-preservation requirements for redistribution. | [Apache License 2.0](https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0) | 5 | 5 | Do not strip headers, LICENSE files, NOTICE files, or attribution from vendored/copied code. |
| 231 | Automate open-source compliance checks before release. | OpenChain ISO/IEC 5230 defines process requirements for managing open-source license compliance across products and services. | [OpenChain ISO/IEC 5230](https://openchainproject.org/license-compliance) | 5 | 4 | Tooling cannot interpret every legal nuance, but it catches inventory, notice, and policy drift early. |
| 232 | Give every mutating operation a stable operation identity. | Idempotency-key and repeatable-request guidance show that ambiguous network failures require a way to distinguish retry of the same intent from a new operation. | [IETF Idempotency-Key draft](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header-00) | 4 | 5 | Keys need retention windows, payload matching, and concurrency handling; never reuse keys for different intent. |
| 233 | Retry only when operation, error, and remaining deadline make retry safe. | AWS reliability guidance and Google API retry configuration separate retryable failures from permanent or unsafe ones. | [AWS REL05-BP03 - Control and limit retry calls](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/reliability-pillar/rel_mitigate_interaction_failure_limit_retries.html) | 5 | 5 | Avoid retrying at multiple stack layers; retries must respect the caller's total time budget. |
| 234 | Propagate deadlines as a shrinking budget across service hops. | gRPC deadline guidance expects clients to set deadlines and servers to stop work once the deadline expires. | [gRPC Deadlines](https://grpc.io/docs/guides/deadlines/) | 5 | 5 | Resetting the timeout at each hop creates hidden overload and bad tail latency. |
| 235 | Use circuit breakers for persistent downstream faults. | Azure's circuit-breaker pattern distinguishes transient failures worth retrying from sustained failures that should fail fast. | [Azure Circuit Breaker pattern](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/circuit-breaker) | 4 | 4 | Breakers need thresholds, half-open probes, metrics, and user-safe fallback behavior. |
| 236 | Treat message delivery as at-least-once unless a narrower guarantee is explicit. | SQS standard queues, RabbitMQ acknowledgements, and common consumer patterns all assume redelivery can happen. | [Amazon SQS at-least-once delivery](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSSimpleQueueService/latest/SQSDeveloperGuide/standard-queues-at-least-once-delivery.html) | 4 | 5 | Publisher acknowledgement, broker durability, and consumer success are different boundaries. |
| 237 | Never claim exactly-once without naming the scope and dedupe window. | Kafka, SQS FIFO, and Azure Service Bus document exactness or deduplication within specific transactional, queue, or time-window scopes. | [Apache Kafka design - delivery semantics](https://kafka.apache.org/41/design/design/) | 5 | 5 | External side effects such as payments, email, and third-party APIs usually escape broker-level guarantees. |
| 238 | Make ordering a keyed contract, not an ambient property. | SQS FIFO ordering and Kafka partition ordering are scoped to message groups or partitions, not global system behavior. | [Amazon SQS FIFO delivery logic](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSSimpleQueueService/latest/SQSDeveloperGuide/FIFO-queues-understanding-logic.html) | 4 | 4 | Global ordering reduces concurrency and availability; cross-key order should be assumed absent. |
| 239 | Pair durable outbox with idempotent inbox/consumer handling. | Transactional outbox patterns solve database/message dual writes, but relays can duplicate messages, so consumers still need dedupe. | [AWS Transactional Outbox pattern](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/transactional-outbox.html) | 4 | 5 | The outbox makes publishing durable; the inbox or consumer ledger makes repeated delivery safe. |
| 240 | Use sagas and compensation for cross-service consistency. | Azure and AWS describe compensating transactions and sagas for distributed workflows where single ACID rollback is unavailable. | [Azure Compensating Transaction pattern](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/compensating-transaction) | 4 | 4 | Compensation is business logic, can fail, and must be designed and tested like the forward path. |
| 241 | Keep API contracts backward-compatible through partial failure. | Google API compatibility guidance treats APIs as long-lived contracts, and structured problem details help clients handle failures without scraping messages. | [Google AIP-180 - Backwards compatibility](https://google.aip.dev/180) | 4 | 5 | Additive changes are safer than semantic changes; clients must tolerate unknown fields and partial outages. |
| 242 | Declare and pin every build input. | Reproducible-build and hermetic-build guidance require stable source, dependencies, tools, environment, and build instructions. | [Reproducible Builds - Definition](https://reproducible-builds.org/docs/definition/) | 5 | 5 | Ambient host state is a hidden dependency; timestamps, paths, and tool versions may need normalization. |
| 243 | Build and test from a clean reference environment. | CI and SLSA guidance favor controlled, isolated, reproducible build environments over developer-machine residue. | [SLSA v1.2 build requirements](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/build-requirements) | 5 | 5 | Self-hosted runners need real isolation and cleanup guarantees. |
| 244 | Keep main green; broken builds outrank feature work. | Continuous integration and DORA guidance treat mainline health as central to fast, safe delivery. | [Martin Fowler - Continuous Integration](https://martinfowler.com/articles/continuousIntegration.html) | 5 | 5 | If repair is not quick, revert first and debug off-main. |
| 245 | Stage automated feedback from cheap high-signal checks to expensive checks. | Continuous delivery guidance uses fast build/unit checks before broader acceptance and release gates. | [Continuous Delivery - Continuous Testing](https://continuousdelivery.com/foundations/test-automation/) | 5 | 4 | Slow checks still matter; they should gate release at the right stage. |
| 246 | Make tests deterministic and hermetic unless explicitly classified otherwise. | Bazel test guidance and flaky-test research identify uncontrolled clock, filesystem, network, randomness, order, and shared state as flake sources. | [Bazel Test Encyclopedia](https://bazel.build/reference/test-encyclopedia) | 5 | 5 | External E2E tests can exist, but should be separated from merge-critical trust unless stabilized. |
| 247 | Treat cache as an optimization, not a source of truth. | Remote-cache and SLSA guidance assume cached outputs are safe only when actions are reproducible and cache writes are trusted. | [Bazel Remote Caching](https://bazel.build/remote/caching) | 5 | 5 | Disabling cache must not change outputs; never cache secrets. |
| 248 | Make the build graph complete and explicit. | Build-systems research and recursive-make critiques show that missing dependency edges cause stale or incorrect outputs. | [Build Systems a la Carte](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/build-systems-final.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Generated dependency metadata is fine if reproducible and validated. |
| 249 | Generated code needs one source of truth and an automated freshness gate. | Go generate makes generation explicit, while generated artifacts commonly drift when sources and outputs are both edited manually. | [Go Blog - Generating code](https://go.dev/blog/generate) | 4 | 4 | Checking in generated code is valid for bootstrapping or clients, but CI should regenerate and diff. |
| 250 | Release immutable, digest-addressed artifacts with provenance. | SLSA and in-toto use artifact identity and supply-chain provenance to make releases auditable and tamper-evident. | [in-toto](https://in-toto.io/) | 5 | 5 | Rebuilding separately per environment destroys traceability. |
| 251 | Treat pipelines as owned production code. | Release-engineering and branching/CI guidance treat build, test, and release automation as engineered systems with owners and policies. | [Google SRE - Release Engineering](https://sre.google/sre-book/release-engineering/) | 5 | 4 | Product teams should own breakages with platform guardrails. |
| 252 | Protect CI, release credentials, provenance keys, and cache writes with least privilege. | SLSA separates provenance authority from build steps, and branch protection/cache docs show CI controls are part of the trusted release path. | [SLSA v1.2 build requirements](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/build-requirements) | 5 | 5 | Keep an audited break-glass path so controls do not become operational deadlock. |
| 253 | Put durable invariants in the datastore where possible. | Database constraints enforce `NOT NULL`, uniqueness, foreign-key, and check rules even when application paths or concurrency change. | [PostgreSQL Constraints](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/ddl-constraints.html) | 5 | 5 | Cross-service invariants may still need workflows, reconciliation, or explicit domain checks. |
| 254 | Treat transaction boundaries and isolation levels as correctness requirements. | Database docs and isolation-level research show that transaction isolation choices affect anomalies and correctness, not just performance. | [Berenson et al. - A Critique of ANSI SQL Isolation Levels](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2016/02/tr-95-51.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Higher isolation can reduce throughput and require retries; specify retry behavior with the isolation choice. |
| 255 | Defend every read-modify-write against lost updates. | Database locking and isolation sources show ordinary reads before writes are not enough under concurrency. | [MySQL InnoDB locking reads](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/innodb-locking-reads.html) | 5 | 5 | Use row locks, version columns, compare-and-swap, atomic updates, or serializable transactions. |
| 256 | Use online schema-change primitives and understand their locks. | PostgreSQL concurrent indexes, MySQL online DDL, and gh-ost exist because naive DDL can block production writes. | [PostgreSQL CREATE INDEX](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createindex.html) | 5 | 5 | "Online" still has cutover, uniqueness, invalid-index, long-transaction, and extra-I/O risks. |
| 257 | A backup is not real until a restore drill proves it. | AWS recovery guidance and GitLab's outage postmortem show backups can exist but fail under actual restore conditions. | [AWS Well-Architected - Periodic recovery testing](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/2025-02-25/framework/rel_backing_up_data_periodic_recovery_testing_data.html) | 5 | 5 | Restore drills must cover RPO, RTO, integrity, keys, permissions, schemas, object stores, and runbooks. |
| 258 | Design for point-in-time recovery, not just snapshots. | PostgreSQL WAL archiving and disaster-recovery guidance support restoring to a chosen point in time after corruption or deletion. | [PostgreSQL Continuous Archiving and PITR](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/continuous-archiving.html) | 5 | 5 | Multi-store systems need a consistent restore point or replay plan. |
| 259 | Do not confuse replication, failover, and backup. | Incident reports show replicated deletion or corruption is still deletion or corruption, not recovery. | [GitLab database outage postmortem](https://about.gitlab.com/blog/postmortem-of-database-outage-of-january-31/) | 5 | 5 | Consider delayed replicas, PITR, immutable backups, and separate recovery credentials. |
| 260 | Make retention and deletion explicit lifecycle state. | GDPR, ICO, and NIST sanitization guidance require deliberate treatment of erasure, storage limitation, disposal, and backup handling. | [ICO - Right to erasure](https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/individual-rights/individual-rights/right-to-erasure/) | 5 | 5 | Legal holds and statutory retention can override deletion; backups may need "beyond use" handling until rotation. |
| 261 | Give every large migration, backfill, restore, and deletion job operational controls. | Online-migration tooling and outage reports show long-running data jobs need dry-run, progress, throttle, resume, and abort paths. | [GitHub - gh-ost online migration tool](https://github.blog/news-insights/company-news/gh-ost-github-s-online-migration-tool-for-mysql/) | 4 | 4 | This adds cost, but it is cheaper than manual incident recovery at scale. |
| 262 | Encode durable correctness claims as checked artifacts. | Hoare logic, design-by-contract, and modern verifiers demonstrate that important correctness claims can be represented as preconditions, postconditions, invariants, proofs, or executable checks. | [Hoare - An Axiomatic Basis for Computer Programming](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/363235.363259) | 5 | 5 | Do not formalize vague preferences or fast-changing policy. |
| 263 | Put contracts where ownership changes. | Design-by-contract and verifier docs distinguish caller preconditions, callee postconditions, and state invariants. | [Eiffel - Design by Contract and Assertions](https://www.eiffel.org/doc/solutions/Design_by_Contract_and_Assertions) | 4 | 5 | Public contracts become compatibility obligations; avoid exposing unstable internals. |
| 264 | Use assertions only for internal impossibilities and invariants. | Java and Python assertion docs make assertions unsuitable for required runtime validation because assertion execution can be disabled or configured. | [Oracle Java Assertion Facility](https://docs.oracle.com/cd/E19683-01/806-7930/6jgp65ikl/index.html) | 5 | 5 | Never use assertions for auth, user input, I/O, or recoverable business failures. |
| 265 | Model-check high-risk concurrent, distributed, or protocol designs before they harden. | TLA+ and AWS formal-methods experience show model checking can expose design bugs in state machines and distributed protocols before implementation. | [Lamport - Specifying Systems / TLA+](https://lamport.azurewebsites.net/tla/book.html) | 5 | 5 | Models can omit real bugs; state explosion requires scoped abstractions. |
| 266 | Apply formal methods surgically to high-risk kernels and boundaries. | AWS, NASA, SPARK, Dafny, and Frama-C sources show formal methods are most practical when applied to critical algorithms, protocols, and safety boundaries. | [AWS - How AWS Uses Formal Methods](https://www.amazon.science/publications/how-amazon-web-services-uses-formal-methods) | 5 | 5 | Full-product proof is rarely pragmatic; combine proof, model checking, property tests, review, and monitoring. |
| 267 | Promote repeated review comments into automated rules. | Tricorder, ESLint, and CodeQL show repeated mechanical review feedback can become lint/static-analysis checks. | [Google Tricorder](https://research.google/pubs/tricorder-building-a-program-analysis-ecosystem/) | 5 | 4 | Automate correctness and architecture, not taste wars; every rule needs an owner. |
| 268 | Treat schemas, contracts, rules, and models as code. | JSON Schema, OpenAPI, Dafny/SPARK, ESLint, and CodeQL workflows require versioning, review, tests, and CI execution to stay trustworthy. | [CodeQL Queries](https://codeql.github.com/docs/writing-codeql-queries/about-codeql-queries/) | 4 | 4 | Drifting checks create false confidence. |
| 269 | State verification boundaries explicitly. | Gradual typing, API field annotations, and formal-methods guidance all depend on knowing what is checked, assumed, or unchecked. | [PEP 484 - Type Hints](https://peps.python.org/pep-0484/) | 5 | 5 | False certainty is worse than no checker; document disabled checks and trusted assumptions. |
| 270 | Do not mix unrelated formatting, refactors, dependency bumps, and behavior changes. | Git, Linux, Google, and just-in-time quality research all support separating logical changes so review and regression attribution stay clear. | [Git SubmittingPatches](https://git-scm.com/docs/SubmittingPatches) | 5 | 5 | Mechanical tree-wide changes may be large, but should be isolated and labeled. |
| 271 | Make code ownership explicit for sensitive paths. | CODEOWNERS-style systems route review to domain experts and protect critical code paths. | [GitHub CODEOWNERS](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-code-owners) | 5 | 5 | Single-owner areas need backups and break-glass rules. |
| 272 | Write commit messages for future diagnosis. | Git patch guidance and commit-message practice emphasize explaining what changed, why, and any relevant context. | [Linux kernel submitting patches](https://www.kernel.org/doc/html/v4.16/process/submitting-patches.html) | 5 | 4 | Conventional formats are most valuable when tooling consumes them. |
| 273 | Preserve traceability from work item to PR, commit, and release. | GitHub and Azure provide explicit mechanisms to link issues/work items to code changes and releases. | [GitHub issue and PR keywords](https://docs.github.com/en/get-started/writing-on-github/working-with-advanced-formatting/using-keywords-in-issues-and-pull-requests) | 5 | 4 | Auto-closing can be wrong when merge does not equal done; link manually when needed. |
| 274 | Automate dependency updates, but throttle, group, test, and review them. | Dependabot docs and empirical studies show automation improves update speed but can create fatigue, rollback, and compatibility risks. | [GitHub Dependabot version updates](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/concepts/supply-chain-security/dependabot-version-updates) | 5 | 5 | Auto-merge only low-risk updates with strong tests and rollback paths. |
| 275 | Prefer short-lived branches and frequent small integrations. | DORA trunk-based development guidance links short-lived branches and frequent mainline integration to stronger delivery and operational outcomes. | [DORA - Trunk-Based Development](https://dora.dev/capabilities/trunk-based-development/) | 5 | 5 | Long-lived release branches can be justified, but feature branches should not become private forks. |
| 276 | Make bypasses explicit, rare, and auditable. | Branch protection and owner-review tools can enforce normal flow; emergency exceptions need recorded accountability. | [GitHub branch protection rule docs](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-protected-branches/managing-a-branch-protection-rule) | 5 | 4 | Small teams still need a defined emergency route. |
| 277 | No license, no reuse. | GitHub and SFLC guidance warn that missing license information leaves default copyright restrictions in place. | [SFLC - Managing Copyright Information](https://softwarefreedom.org/resources/2012/ManagingCopyrightInformation.html) | 5 | 5 | This is not legal advice; private/internal use and exceptions vary by jurisdiction and context. |
| 278 | Review license compatibility before combining or distributing. | FSF compatibility guidance and OSPO policies treat compatibility as the ability to satisfy all applicable license terms together. | [FSF - Relicensing versus compatibility](https://www.fsf.org/blogs/licensing/relicensing-versus-compatibility) | 4 | 5 | Linking, derivative works, SaaS, and distribution questions are fact-specific. |
| 279 | Treat copied snippets, forum answers, and AI suggestions as third-party material until cleared. | Copilot code-referencing and Stack Overflow licensing guidance show generated or copied suggestions can carry provenance and licensing questions. | [GitHub Copilot Code Referencing](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/completions/code-referencing) | 4 | 5 | Trivial snippets may not be protectable, but teams should not rely on informal guesses. |
| 280 | Document assets, datasets, databases, models, and training lineage. | Open Data Commons, Hugging Face model cards, CycloneDX, and third-party policies extend provenance beyond source packages. | [CycloneDX Specification Overview](https://cyclonedx.org/specification/overview/) | 5 | 5 | Data may also carry privacy, contract, database-right, export-control, or model-use restrictions. |
| 281 | Ship an SBOM or equivalent inventory with release provenance when risk justifies it. | NTIA, SPDX, CycloneDX, and NIST SSDF describe SBOM/provenance records as formal component and supply-chain transparency artifacts. | [NTIA SBOM Minimum Elements](https://www.ntia.gov/sites/default/files/publications/sbom_minimum_elements_report_0.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Completeness and sharing level should match customer, threat, and confidentiality needs. |
| 282 | Require inbound contribution provenance. | The Developer Certificate of Origin lets contributors certify they have the right to submit work under the project license. | [Developer Certificate of Origin](https://developercertificate.org/) | 5 | 4 | DCO, CLA, and inbound-equals-outbound models have governance tradeoffs. |
| 283 | Escalate uncertain or high-risk license findings before release. | SFC enforcement principles and SPDX guidance emphasize verification, comprehensive analysis, and avoiding unsupported legal conclusions. | [SFC GPL Enforcement Principles](https://sfconservancy.org/copyleft-compliance/principles.html) | 4 | 4 | Not every finding needs counsel; unresolved copyleft, proprietary, AI, data, or customer-distribution risk should not be hand-waved. |
| 284 | Do not use binary floating point for exact decimal domains. | Python decimal and floating-point docs explain that decimal values like `1.1` are not exactly representable in binary floating point, while decimal types can represent decimal values exactly. | [Python decimal module](https://docs.python.org/3/library/decimal.html) | 5 | 5 | Money, billing, tax, inventory counts, and legal/accounting calculations need explicit decimal or integer-minor-unit policies. |
| 285 | Specify rounding mode and precision at domain boundaries. | IEEE 754 and BigDecimal documentation show that arithmetic systems expose rounding modes, scale, precision, and exceptional conditions that change results. | [IEEE 754-2019](https://standards.ieee.org/ieee/754/6210/) | 5 | 5 | Hidden default rounding can become a production bug when jurisdictions, currencies, or reports disagree. |
| 286 | Keep units with values. | Fowler's Quantity pattern and NASA's Mars Climate Orbiter failure both show that naked numbers invite unit-mismatch bugs. | [Martin Fowler - Quantity](https://martinfowler.com/eaaDev/Quantity.html) | 4 | 5 | Strongest for physical quantities, money/currency, rates, durations, sizes, and external interfaces. |
| 287 | Use monotonic clocks for elapsed time. | Monotonic-clock docs distinguish elapsed-time measurement from wall-clock time, which can jump due to synchronization or manual changes. | [Python time.monotonic](https://docs.python.org/3/library/time.html#time.monotonic) | 5 | 5 | Use wall clocks for timestamps and civil time; use monotonic clocks for durations, deadlines, and timeout calculations. |
| 288 | Treat time handling as domain logic, not simple arithmetic. | Time falsehood collections and date/time standards warn against assuming fixed day length, stable time zones, unique timestamps, or synchronized client/server clocks. | [Falsehoods Programmers Believe About Time](https://infiniteundo.com/post/25326999628/falsehoods-programmers-believe-about-time) | 4 | 5 | Prefer battle-tested date/time libraries and test DST, time-zone changes, clock skew, precision, and serialization. |
| 289 | Never change a stable API element in place when clients may depend on it. | Google, Microsoft, Stripe, Kubernetes, and SemVer guidance converge on additive evolution, new versions, or new elements for breaking changes. | [Google AIP-180 - Backwards compatibility](https://google.aip.dev/180) | 4 | 5 | Internal APIs can move faster only when all callers are known and migrated together. |
| 290 | Separate deprecation from sunset and removal. | RFC 9745 defines deprecation signals, while RFC 8594 defines sunset communication for expected unavailability. | [RFC 9745 - The Deprecation HTTP Header Field](https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc9745/) | 5 | 5 | Deprecated means "avoid new use"; sunset/removal needs exact dates, replacement paths, and runtime/doc signals. |
| 291 | Define deprecation windows by stability level before you need them. | Kubernetes, Google API, and Microsoft Graph policies set minimum support windows that vary by API maturity and client impact. | [Kubernetes deprecation policy](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/using-api/deprecation-policy/) | 5 | 5 | Exact windows are ecosystem-specific; the rule is to publish them before contentious removals. |
| 292 | Add only what old clients can safely ignore. | API compatibility guidance and schema evolution rules favor optional/defaulted request fields, nullable/default response fields, and no new required headers or semantics. | [Microsoft Azure REST API Guidelines](https://github.com/microsoft/api-guidelines/blob/vNext/azure/Guidelines.md) | 5 | 5 | New enum values, pagination changes, and required params often break clients even when they look additive. |
| 293 | Require unknown-field and unknown-enum tolerance in clients and SDKs. | Kubernetes API concepts and Protobuf guidance preserve forward compatibility by requiring clients to tolerate unknown fields or values where possible. | [Kubernetes API concepts](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/using-api/api-concepts/) | 5 | 5 | Some formats drop unknowns; document that loss explicitly. |
| 294 | Make API version and capability selection explicit. | Stripe, Azure, and API-versioning guidance use pinned versions, headers, query/path versions, preview channels, or feature gates to avoid ambiguous behavior. | [Stripe API versioning](https://docs.stripe.com/api/versioning) | 5 | 4 | The server should make it visible which version or capability it honored. |
| 295 | Keep old and new API versions usable side-by-side during migration. | Stripe upgrade and Kubernetes migration guidance support gradual migration with old and new surfaces concurrently available. | [Stripe API upgrades](https://docs.stripe.com/upgrades) | 5 | 4 | Side-by-side support has cost; remove after the published window and observed migration. |
| 296 | Ship migration tooling with every significant retirement. | Kubernetes API migration guides and cloud retirement notices show migrations need scanners, compatibility tests, SDK mappings, examples, and rollback guidance. | [Helm - Deprecated Kubernetes APIs](https://helm.sh/docs/topics/kubernetes_apis/) | 4 | 5 | Documentation alone is weak when users need inventory and automated detection. |
| 297 | Compatibility-test supported client/server version pairs. | API lifecycle guidance implies old clients against new servers, new clients against supported old servers, SDKs against pinned API versions, and events/webhooks across versions. | [Stripe SDK versioning policy](https://docs.stripe.com/sdks/versioning) | 5 | 5 | Define the supported matrix explicitly so tests do not grow unbounded. |
| 298 | Never retire an API by silence. | RFC Sunset, Kubernetes deprecation, Stripe upgrade, and Azure retirement examples emphasize clear dates, failure behavior, affected versions, and replacement paths. | [RFC 8594 - The Sunset HTTP Header Field](https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc8594/) | 5 | 5 | Silent retirement turns planned migration into an outage. |
| 299 | Do not invent cryptography. | OWASP and NIST cryptographic guidance favor reviewed libraries, standard protocols, and approved algorithms over custom cryptographic designs. | [OWASP Cryptographic Storage Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cryptographic_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Custom crypto needs specialist review; most product code should never need it. |
| 300 | Use authenticated encryption by default. | NIST GCM guidance and libsodium APIs support authenticated encryption so ciphertext is not decrypted or trusted without integrity protection. | [NIST SP 800-38D](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/38/d/final) | 5 | 5 | Never treat unauthenticated CBC/CTR-style encryption as enough for attacker-controlled data. |
| 301 | Never reuse a nonce or IV with the same key. | AEAD modes such as AES-GCM and ChaCha20-Poly1305 depend on nonce uniqueness for security. | [libsodium secret-key authenticated encryption](https://libsodium.gitbook.io/doc/secret-key_cryptography/secretbox) | 5 | 5 | Nonce misuse is a design bug, not a harmless implementation detail. |
| 302 | Generate secrets only with a CSPRNG. | Language security APIs distinguish cryptographic randomness from general-purpose PRNGs for keys, salts, tokens, reset links, and session IDs. | [Python secrets](https://docs.python.org/3/library/secrets.html) | 5 | 5 | Do not use `Math.random`, Python `random`, Go `math/rand`, or similar APIs for security values. |
| 303 | Hash passwords only with password-hashing algorithms. | OWASP and NIST guidance recommend password hashing/KDFs such as Argon2id, scrypt, bcrypt, or PBKDF2 rather than plain SHA/MD5. | [OWASP Password Storage Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Password_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Parameters need periodic review as hardware changes. |
| 304 | Store production secrets in purpose-built secret/key stores. | OWASP secrets guidance and cloud KMS/secret-manager docs provide lifecycle, access control, audit, and rotation support absent from raw config sprawl. | [OWASP Secrets Management Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Secrets_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Environment variables can be acceptable for low-risk delivery paths but are poor as uncontrolled secret sprawl. |
| 305 | Separate cryptographic keys by purpose, environment, tenant, and data class. | NIST key-management guidance treats key usage, lifetime, access, and separation as core controls. | [NIST SP 800-57 Part 1 Rev. 5](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/57/pt1/r5/final) | 5 | 5 | Do not reuse one key for encryption, signing, MACs, token signing, and peppering. |
| 306 | Build key rotation and revocation before compromise. | Cloud KMS and secrets-manager docs show rotation, audit, ownership, and emergency compromise handling as part of key lifecycle. | [AWS KMS key rotation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/kms/latest/developerguide/rotate-keys.html) | 5 | 5 | Rotation must account for old ciphertext, token validation windows, caches, and rollback. |
| 307 | Use modern TLS and never disable certificate or hostname validation. | OWASP TLS guidance and CISA bad-practice guidance reject weak protocols, ciphers, certificate hashes, and bypassed validation. | [OWASP Transport Layer Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Transport_Layer_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | TLS 1.3 should be default; TLS 1.2 may remain only when justified and hardened. |
| 308 | Scope tokens narrowly and make them short-lived where practical. | OAuth security guidance emphasizes audience, issuer, subject, resource/action scope, expiry, and sender constraints where available. | [RFC 9700 OAuth 2.0 Security Best Current Practice](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/rfc9700/) | 5 | 5 | Refresh tokens need rotation or sender constraints and a revocation story. |
| 309 | Validate token signatures and claims before trusting payloads. | OWASP JWT and OAuth guidance warn against algorithm confusion, missing expiry, wrong audience/issuer, and accepting unsigned tokens. | [OWASP JSON Web Token Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/JSON_Web_Token_for_Java_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Decoding is not validation; validation must happen before authorization decisions. |
| 310 | Deprecate weak cryptography deliberately. | NIST transition guidance and CISA bad-practice guidance flag obsolete algorithms and protocols such as MD5, SHA-1 signatures, DES/3DES, RC4, ECB, weak RSA, and legacy password hashes. | [NIST SP 800-131A Rev. 2](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/131/a/r2/final) | 5 | 5 | Migration needs compatibility windows and telemetry to find remaining usage. |
| 311 | Compare secret tokens with constant-time helpers. | Security-oriented standard libraries expose constant-time comparison for MACs, signatures, reset tokens, and API tokens to reduce timing leaks. | [Python secrets](https://docs.python.org/3/library/secrets.html) | 5 | 4 | Use platform helpers; do not hand-roll comparison loops unless reviewed. |
| 312 | Roll out code, config, and data changes progressively. | Cloudflare's 2019 incident and SRE canary guidance show fast global rollout can turn one defect into a global outage. | [Cloudflare 2019 outage postmortem](https://blog.cloudflare.com/details-of-the-cloudflare-outage-on-july-2-2019/) | 5 | 5 | Global-first rollout is rarely justified for high-impact systems. |
| 313 | Validate config and policy data like production code. | Google Cloud's 2025 policy incident and SRE guidance show config shape, nulls, size, performance, flags, and scope can break production as surely as code. | [Google Cloud June 2025 incident report](https://status.cloud.google.com/incidents/ow5i3PPK96RduMcb1SsW) | 4 | 5 | Config validators need tests, staged rollout, and ownership. |
| 314 | Put hard blast-radius limits into operational tools. | AWS S3 and Atlassian incidents show internal tools can remove too much capacity or data too quickly without automated guardrails. | [AWS S3 2017 message summary](https://aws.amazon.com/message/41926/) | 5 | 5 | Human review is not enough when a single command can affect global capacity or many tenants. |
| 315 | Make destructive operations soft-delete first and hard-delete later. | Atlassian's outage review and GitLab's data-loss incident show restore-tested soft deletion is safer than immediate irreversible deletion. | [Atlassian April 2022 outage PIR](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/atlassian-engineering/post-incident-review-april-2022-outage) | 5 | 5 | Soft delete needs retention, access controls, restore tests, and eventual purge. |
| 316 | Keep optional dependencies non-blocking. | AWS Kinesis/Cognito and SRE cascading-failure guidance show optional dependencies can turn local failure into broad outage when not isolated. | [AWS Kinesis 2020 event summary](https://aws.amazon.com/message/11201/) | 5 | 5 | Degrade non-core functionality rather than blocking critical paths. |
| 317 | Partition critical systems into cells or failure domains. | AWS Kinesis and Cloudflare postmortems show cellularization/failure-domain isolation limits unknown scaling and dependency failures. | [Cloudflare 2023 control-plane outage postmortem](https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-mortem-on-cloudflare-control-plane-and-analytics-outage/) | 5 | 5 | Cells add operational complexity but reduce correlated blast radius. |
| 318 | Test disaster recovery at the scale of the incident you fear. | Atlassian and Cloudflare postmortems show single-resource restore drills do not prove mass recovery or newer-service DR procedures. | [Atlassian April 2022 outage PIR](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/atlassian-engineering/post-incident-review-april-2022-outage) | 5 | 5 | DR tests must include scale, people, tooling, data, and dependencies. |
| 319 | Keep monitoring, status, and incident tooling independent of the failing platform. | AWS Kinesis and Google Cloud 2025 incidents show status/incident tools can be impaired by the same outage they need to report. | [AWS Kinesis 2020 event summary](https://aws.amazon.com/message/11201/) | 5 | 4 | Incident tooling needs separate dependencies, credentials, and communications paths. |
| 320 | Do not let control-plane changes propagate globally faster than validation can stop them. | Google and Cloudflare incidents show control-plane config/policy propagation can amplify a bad change before operators can intervene. | [Google 2019 service disruption update](https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/inside-google-cloud/an-update-on-sundays-service-disruption) | 5 | 5 | Control-plane rollout should have gates, regional staging, rate limits, and emergency stop. |
| 321 | Never let untrusted input directly choose a filesystem path. | CWE and OWASP path traversal guidance warn that externally controlled paths can escape intended directories or resources. | [CWE-22 Path Traversal](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html) | 5 | 5 | Map user intent to server-owned IDs or allowlisted basenames; reject absolute paths, traversal, UNC/drive prefixes, and separators. |
| 322 | Canonicalize paths with platform APIs, then enforce trusted-root boundaries. | Path traversal defenses need OS-aware normalization plus component-boundary checks under a trusted root. | [OWASP Path Traversal](https://owasp.org/www-community/attacks/Path_Traversal) | 4 | 5 | Canonicalization alone is not enough; operate through handles/descriptors where possible. |
| 323 | Never check a path and later use it by name. | CWE TOCTOU and CERT guidance warn that path checks can be invalidated before later open/use operations. | [CWE-367 TOCTOU Race Condition](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/367.html) | 5 | 5 | Combine check and use in one atomic operation or verify stable metadata such as device/inode. |
| 324 | Treat links and reparse points as hostile unless explicitly allowed. | CWE/CERT and Linux `openat2` guidance support link-aware operations that prevent escaping trusted directories. | [Linux openat2(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/openat2.2.html) | 5 | 5 | Symlinks, hard links, junctions, reparse points, and magic links all need policy. |
| 325 | Never invent temporary filenames. | CWE temp-file weaknesses and language temp APIs require atomic create/open behavior with restrictive permissions. | [Python tempfile](https://docs.python.org/3/library/tempfile.html) | 5 | 5 | Prefer private temp directories and remove only owned temp resources. |
| 326 | Publish file updates with a durable atomic-write protocol. | POSIX rename and `fsync` docs show atomic replace and durability require writing a temp file in the same directory, flushing, renaming, and syncing the parent directory. | [Linux fsync(2)](https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/fsync.2.html) | 5 | 5 | Platform and filesystem behavior differs; test the exact durability requirement. |
| 327 | Set permissions correctly at creation time. | CWE and CERT file-permission guidance warn against creating broad permissions and tightening later. | [CWE-732 Incorrect Permission Assignment](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html) | 5 | 5 | `umask`, ACLs, inherited defaults, containers, and platform models are variables to test. |
| 328 | Avoid shells for process execution. | OWASP command-injection guidance recommends avoiding shell command construction and passing fixed executable paths with argument arrays. | [OWASP OS Command Injection Defense Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/OS_Command_Injection_Defense_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Never concatenate command strings with untrusted input. |
| 329 | Treat child-process environment, cwd, PATH, and inherited descriptors as untrusted. | CERT environment guidance and process APIs expose environment, search path, working directory, and descriptor inheritance as execution inputs. | [CERT ENV03-C](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/recommendations/environment-env/env03-c/) | 5 | 5 | Pass a minimal environment, absolute executable path, safe cwd, and close-on-exec descriptors. |
| 330 | Extract archives into a private staging area before promotion. | Zip Slip and tar extraction guidance show archives can contain traversal paths, links, permissions, and unexpected file types. | [Python tarfile extraction filters](https://docs.python.org/3/library/tarfile.html#tarfile-extraction-filter) | 5 | 5 | Filter members, reject traversal and links unless intended, inspect results, then promote safely. |
| 331 | Do not assume filesystem semantics are portable. | OS, Unicode, Windows, APFS, and path libraries differ in case sensitivity, normalization, separators, reserved names, lengths, rename behavior, and network semantics. | [Microsoft - Naming Files, Paths, and Namespaces](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/fileio/naming-a-file) | 5 | 4 | Cross-platform code needs tests on the target filesystems, not only one developer machine. |
| 332 | Model money as amount, currency, and rounding policy. | Currency-code and payment docs show amounts depend on currency exponent/minor units, while decimal docs and Fowler's Money pattern separate amount from currency. | [ISO 4217 Currency Codes](https://www.iso.org/iso-4217-currency-codes.html) | 5 | 5 | Never use binary float for settled amounts; choose integer minor units or decimal deliberately. |
| 333 | Make integer overflow policy explicit. | CERT integer guidance, language checked-arithmetic APIs, and Ariane 501 show default overflow behavior can be catastrophic when assumptions are wrong. | [CERT INT32-C](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/rules/integers-int/int32-c/) | 5 | 5 | Choose checked, saturating, widening, bigint, or intentional wrapping arithmetic. |
| 334 | Use approximate floats only for approximate facts. | IEEE 754, Goldberg, and language docs show floating-point arithmetic is approximate and non-associative. | [Goldberg - What Every Computer Scientist Should Know About Floating-Point Arithmetic](https://docs.oracle.com/cd/E19957-01/806-3568/ncg_goldberg.html) | 5 | 5 | Use tolerances, stable algorithms, and error budgets; do not use exact equality unless values are exact by construction. |
| 335 | Fix calculation order and numeric context when reproducibility matters. | Floating-point reductions can change with order, precision, runtime, compiler options, and platform assumptions. | [Python math.fsum](https://docs.python.org/3/library/math.html#math.fsum) | 5 | 4 | Bitwise reproducibility is a requirement to design for, not something to assume. |
| 336 | Distinguish observed instants from future civil times. | RFC 3339, RFC 9557, IANA tzdb, and date/time APIs distinguish timestamps from local scheduled events that depend on calendar and time-zone rules. | [RFC 9557 Date and Time on the Internet: Timestamps with Additional Information](https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc9557/) | 5 | 5 | Future appointments need calendar, IANA zone, and recurrence rules, not only UTC. |
| 337 | Keep time-zone and leap-second behavior as explicit mutable data. | IANA tzdb, NIST leap-second guidance, and leap-smear docs show clock rules change and may be represented differently across systems. | [IANA Time Zone Database](https://data.iana.org/time-zones/tz-link.html) | 5 | 5 | Test skipped/repeated local times, leap behavior, clock skew, and tzdb updates. |
| 338 | Treat identifiers as tokens, not quantities. | JSON number interoperability and large platform IDs show numeric-looking identifiers can lose precision, leading zeroes, or formatting when treated as numbers. | [ECMAScript Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER](https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/numbers-and-dates.html#sec-number.max_safe_integer) | 5 | 5 | Preserve IDs, account numbers, and object IDs as strings unless arithmetic is part of the domain. |
| 339 | Make text sorting a locale policy. | Unicode collation, CLDR, ECMA-402, and W3C internationalization guidance show byte or code-point order is rarely user-expected order. | [Unicode UTS #10 Collation](https://www.unicode.org/reports/tr10/) | 5 | 4 | Specify locale, strength, numeric collation, normalization, and stable tie-breakers. |
| 340 | Store measurement provenance with measured values. | SI/NIST measurement guidance treats units, reference frame, precision, resolution, and uncertainty as part of measurement meaning. | [NIST SP 330](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.330-2019.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Formatted decimals do not prove real precision. |
| 341 | Authorize every object access server-side. | OWASP BOLA and authorization guidance require checking subject, action, object, and context on every object-level API path. | [OWASP API1:2023 Broken Object Level Authorization](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa1-broken-object-level-authorization/) | 5 | 5 | GUIDs reduce guessing, not authorization risk; batch, list, export, and admin paths need the same rule. |
| 342 | Default deny for authorization. | OWASP authorization guidance and IAM best practices favor explicit allowed actions/resources/conditions rather than implicit access. | [OWASP Authorization Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Public resources must be explicit policy cases, not missing-policy accidents. |
| 343 | Derive tenant and subject authority from trusted context, not request fields. | Tenant-isolation and BOLA guidance show client-supplied tenant/user/role/scope values cause cross-object and cross-tenant access flaws. | [AWS SaaS Tenant Isolation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/whitepapers/latest/saas-architecture-fundamentals/tenant-isolation.html) | 5 | 5 | Admin cross-tenant support needs explicit audited tenant switch permission. |
| 344 | Enforce tenant isolation below the controller where possible. | PostgreSQL row security and cloud SaaS guidance support database/IAM/resource scoping as backstops beneath application routing. | [PostgreSQL Row Security Policies](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/ddl-rowsecurity.html) | 5 | 5 | RLS owners/superusers and connection-context bugs can bypass protections; still test app-level authz. |
| 345 | Centralize and regression-test authorization decisions. | OWASP ASVS and authorization guidance frame authorization as verifiable requirements with safe failure, logging, and tests. | [OWASP ASVS 5.0](https://owasp.org/www-project-application-security-verification-standard/) | 5 | 4 | Centralization should not become an unreviewed god function; domain rules still need clear inputs. |
| 346 | Use short-lived credentials and real revocation. | ASVS, OAuth revocation, and IAM guidance require session/credential invalidation for logout, account changes, incident response, and app uninstall. | [RFC 7009 OAuth 2.0 Token Revocation](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7009) | 5 | 5 | Stateless JWTs need short TTL plus revocation/blocklist/per-user cutoff strategy. |
| 347 | Regenerate and harden session tokens at trust changes. | OWASP session guidance requires secure generation, backend validation, renewal after authentication, timeouts, and hardened cookie attributes. | [OWASP Session Management Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Session_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | `SameSite` is useful but not a full CSRF or XSS defense. |
| 348 | Prevent confused deputy in delegated access. | AWS confused-deputy guidance and OAuth BCP require delegated calls to bind requester, resource, audience, and source context. | [AWS Confused Deputy Problem](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/confused-deputy.html) | 5 | 5 | Sender-constrained tokens fail if attacker gets both token and key material. |
| 349 | Separate human, workload, service, admin, and break-glass privileges. | AWS, Google, and Microsoft IAM guidance emphasizes least privilege, temporary credentials, elevation, and scoped roles. | [Google Cloud Service Account Best Practices](https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/best-practices-service-accounts) | 5 | 5 | Too many roles can become unmanageable; review unused permissions and automate policy analysis. |
| 350 | Log authorization and tenant-boundary decisions without logging bearer secrets. | OWASP logging and IAM impersonation/audit guidance identify authn/authz, session, admin, and privileged events as important audit records. | [OWASP Logging Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Logging_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 4 | Include actor, tenant, object, action, decision, and correlation ID; never log tokens or session IDs. |
| 351 | Step up authentication before sensitive operations. | ASVS requires reauthentication or stronger assurance for sensitive account, recovery, MFA, privilege, billing, export, or destructive operations. | [OWASP ASVS 5.0](https://owasp.org/www-project-application-security-verification-standard/) | 5 | 4 | Excessive prompts train users to click through; reserve step-up for real risk boundaries. |
| 352 | Document federated identity and session boundaries. | NIST federation and ASVS guidance require clarity around IdP/RP lifetimes, logout, consent, token revocation, and reauthentication triggers. | [NIST SP 800-63C-4](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/63/c/4/final) | 5 | 4 | SSO providers may limit logout/revocation semantics; document limitations and compensating controls. |
| 353 | Treat AI-generated code as untrusted code until reviewed, tested, and understood. | OWASP LLM guidance identifies insecure output handling and misinformation risks; generated code can look plausible while being wrong or unsafe. | [OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications](https://owasp.org/www-project-top-10-for-large-language-model-applications/) | 5 | 5 | AI does not remove ownership; the committer owns correctness, security, licensing, and maintainability. |
| 354 | Do not paste secrets or sensitive code into AI tools without an approved data policy. | AI tool trust/privacy docs and OWASP LLM sensitive-information risks make prompt/input handling a data exposure concern. | [GitHub Copilot Trust Center FAQ](https://copilot.github.trust.page/faq) | 4 | 5 | Enterprise controls vary by tool and plan; follow the strictest applicable project policy. |
| 355 | Check public-code references and provenance for accepted AI suggestions. | GitHub Copilot code-referencing docs explain that suggestions may match public code and that code references are not universal for every accepted or edited suggestion. | [GitHub Copilot Code Referencing](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/completions/code-referencing) | 4 | 5 | Treat notable snippets like third-party material until license/provenance is cleared. |
| 356 | Verify generated APIs, dependencies, and commands against primary docs. | OWASP LLM misinformation and insecure-output risks apply directly to hallucinated APIs, wrong versions, unsafe flags, and invented behavior. | [OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications](https://owasp.org/www-project-top-10-for-large-language-model-applications/) | 5 | 5 | Do not trust model confidence; verify especially for security, data loss, money, migrations, and infrastructure commands. |
| 357 | Own AI-generated output as if you wrote it. | GitHub Copilot terms and coding-agent security guidance place responsibility for accepted suggestions, approvals, and commands on the user/team. | [GitHub Copilot Product Specific Terms](https://github.com/customer-terms/github-copilot-product-specific-terms) | 4 | 5 | Tool output can assist judgment; it cannot own consequences. |
| 358 | Review AI-generated code like an external contribution. | GitHub generated-code review guidance combines normal code review with checks for hallucinated logic, missed constraints, maintainability, and assumptions. | [GitHub - Review AI-generated code](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/tutorials/review-ai-generated-code) | 4 | 5 | Require a human reviewer who understands the code path, not only the prompt. |
| 359 | Require executable evidence for AI-assisted changes. | GitHub and Codex best practices emphasize compiling, relevant tests, static/type/security checks, and diff review before accepting generated changes. | [OpenAI Codex best practices](https://developers.openai.com/codex/learn/best-practices) | 5 | 5 | Generated code should not bypass the normal proof burden. |
| 360 | Bind coding agents with least privilege and explicit approvals. | Coding-agent security docs recommend sandboxing, command approvals, network limits, protected paths, managed settings, and audit logs. | [OpenAI - Running Codex safely](https://openai.com/index/running-codex-safely/) | 5 | 5 | Agents that can write files, run commands, or access networks are part of the trusted build/development surface. |
| 361 | Never let AI fix by weakening evidence or controls. | AI-generated-code review guidance specifically calls for scrutiny when code deletes tests, skips checks, relaxes validation, broadens permissions, or suppresses warnings. | [GitHub - Review AI-generated code](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/tutorials/review-ai-generated-code) | 4 | 5 | Such changes need explicit human justification and stronger regression evidence. |
| 362 | Scope AI context deliberately. | Copilot, Codex, and Claude guidance show that relevant context improves output and reduces unnecessary data exposure. | [OpenAI AGENTS.md guidance](https://developers.openai.com/codex/guides/agents-md) | 5 | 4 | Attach only files/connectors needed for the task; broad context increases exposure and distraction. |
| 363 | Treat untrusted repo content as adversarial input to AI tools. | Agent security and OWASP AI guidance warn that READMEs, issues, PR comments, web pages, dependency scripts, and tool responses can carry prompt-injection or execution risk. | [OWASP Secure Coding with AI Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Secure_Coding_with_AI_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Review generated commands and tool calls before approval. |
| 364 | Audit AI-agent workflow, not just final diffs. | Agent security guidance calls out logs and review of approvals, tool calls, network access, and code references. | [Claude Code security](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/security) | 4 | 4 | Records help teams update rules when repeated AI failure modes appear. |
| 365 | Commit the resolver's exact dependency output. | npm, pip, Cargo, Go, and Maven guidance all provide lockfiles, hashes, sums, or explicit resolved versions for repeatable installs. | [npm package-lock](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v11/configuring-npm/package-lock-json/) | 5 | 5 | Lockfiles and sums are release artifacts, not local cache. |
| 366 | Use frozen installs in CI and releases. | `npm ci`, pip secure/repeatable installs, and lockfile workflows prevent CI from silently resolving different dependency trees. | [npm ci](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v11/commands/npm-ci/) | 5 | 5 | The install should fail when manifests and lockfiles disagree. |
| 367 | Treat version ranges as compatibility promises, not deployment pins. | Package manager docs distinguish dependency constraints from resolved versions; applications deploy resolved trees, while libraries publish compatibility ranges. | [PyPA Dependency Specifiers](https://packaging.python.org/en/latest/specifications/dependency-specifiers/) | 5 | 5 | Broad/open-ended ranges need review because they move risk to consumers or CI time. |
| 368 | Review lockfile diffs like code diffs. | Transitive dependency incidents such as event-stream, ua-parser-js, and axios show the lockfile can contain the real behavioral/security change. | [GitHub Advisory - ua-parser-js malware](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-pjwm-rvh2-c87w) | 5 | 5 | Diffs should expose new packages, changed maintainers, new scripts, source changes, and license shifts. |
| 369 | Default-deny install-time and build-time execution. | Package manager docs and incidents show lifecycle scripts, source builds, and build hooks can execute attacker-controlled code during install. | [Cargo build scripts](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/build-scripts.html) | 5 | 5 | Allowlist scripts/hooks only when needed and reviewed. |
| 370 | Never let private package names compete with public registries. | pip and dependency-confusion guidance warn that mixed indexes can install attacker-controlled public packages with private names. | [pip --extra-index-url warning](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/cli/pip_install/#cmdoption-extra-index-url) | 5 | 5 | Use one authoritative routing policy and explicit private-module exceptions. |
| 371 | Verify package provenance, not just version numbers. | Trusted publishing, provenance attestations, checksum databases, and registry signatures bind packages to publishers/builds more strongly than names and versions alone. | [npm trusted publishing](https://docs.npmjs.com/trusted-publishers/) | 5 | 4 | Provenance support varies by ecosystem; use the strongest available signal. |
| 372 | Treat package namespaces as assets. | Package scopes, group IDs, module paths, and project names are part of the security boundary because dependency confusion and takeover attacks target naming. | [OpenSSF Scorecard checks](https://github.com/ossf/scorecard/blob/main/docs/checks.md) | 4 | 4 | Reserve and monitor names where practical. |
| 373 | Reject mutable or ambient dependency sources in hardened builds. | Git dependencies, branch heads, remote tarballs, source distributions, and ambient build tools need explicit pins, hashes, or approval. | [pip secure installs](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/topics/secure-installs/) | 5 | 4 | Some source builds are legitimate, but they should be a known and reviewed choice. |
| 374 | Vendor deliberately or do not vendor. | Cargo and Go vendoring docs treat vendoring as a managed mirror with provenance and refresh workflows, not arbitrary copied code. | [Cargo source replacement and vendoring](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/source-replacement.html) | 4 | 3 | Keep vendored sources read-only, document origin, and refresh on a schedule. |
| 375 | Define dependency abandonment as a failure condition. | Dependency update research and package incidents show stale, unmaintained, or unresponsive dependencies become latent operational and security risk. | [GitHub Dependabot version updates](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/concepts/supply-chain-security/dependabot-version-updates) | 5 | 4 | Freeze behind review, replace, or assume maintenance ownership. |
| 376 | Log structured events, not prose. | OWASP, OpenTelemetry, and NIST logging guidance support stable event schemas with timestamp, service, actor, resource, action, outcome, reason, severity, and correlation IDs. | [OWASP Logging Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Logging_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Log messages for humans are fine, but machine-readable fields are the durable contract. |
| 377 | Classify logs by sensitivity and retention at write time. | Privacy and logging guidance treats logs as data stores that need purpose, minimization, retention, deletion, and access rules. | [NIST SP 800-92](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/92/final) | 5 | 5 | No indefinite debug/audit hoarding. |
| 378 | Make audit events complete and non-optional. | NIST and OWASP logging guidance identify identity, security, privacy, admin, export, alteration, deletion, and audit-log access events as accountability records. | [NIST SP 800-53 Rev. 5](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/53/r5/upd1/final) | 5 | 5 | Audit events should survive ordinary sampling and debug-mode changes. |
| 379 | Correlate without over-identifying. | OpenTelemetry and privacy guidance support correlation IDs and trace IDs while minimizing direct identifiers such as emails, names, raw IPs, and session tokens. | [OpenTelemetry Logs](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/otel/logs/) | 5 | 4 | Prefer opaque or pseudonymous IDs unless direct identifiers are required. |
| 380 | Audit and restrict access to logs themselves. | Security controls and privacy guidance require least-privilege access, encryption, and audit trails for log datasets. | [CIS Control 8](https://www.cisecurity.org/controls/audit-log-management) | 4 | 5 | Raw logs often contain operational and personal data even after redaction. |
| 381 | Make logs tamper-evident and operationally reliable. | Logging and audit guidance call for centralized collection, deletion/modification protection, clock sync, and alerting on logging gaps. | [CSA CCM LOG-02](https://csf.tools/reference/cloud-controls-matrix/v4-0/log/log-02/) | 4 | 4 | Logging pipeline failure modes need tests just like service dependencies. |
| 382 | Do not sample away accountability. | OpenTelemetry sampling is useful for high-volume observability, but audit/security/deletion/compliance events require complete capture. | [OpenTelemetry Sampling](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/concepts/sampling/) | 5 | 5 | Sample traces and debug logs; do not sample required audit records. |
| 383 | Make debugging privacy-safe by design. | Okta's HAR/support incident and privacy guidance show temporary debug artifacts can expose tokens and personal data. | [Okta support-system incident](https://sec.okta.com/articles/2023/10/tracking-unauthorized-access-oktas-support-system/) | 4 | 5 | Debug elevation should have approval, scope, TTL, redaction, secure storage, and cleanup. |
| 384 | Make deletion requests traverse logs deliberately. | GDPR/ICO guidance requires erasure handling where personal data exists, including active logs, archives, exports, and processors when applicable. | [ICO - Right to erasure](https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/individual-rights/individual-rights/right-to-erasure/) | 5 | 4 | If security/legal retention overrides erasure, minimize direct identifiers and document the basis. |
| 385 | Treat browser state as untrusted input. | HTML, MDN, and OWASP guidance all warn that client-side validation, hidden fields, DOM state, and URL parameters can be modified. | [MDN - input type hidden](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/input/hidden) | 5 | 5 | The server remains the source of truth for policy, price, identity, permissions, and state transitions. |
| 386 | Client-side validation is UX; server-side validation is policy. | WHATWG and MDN constraint-validation guidance explicitly warn that client validation does not replace server validation. | [MDN Constraint Validation](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Guides/Constraint_validation) | 5 | 5 | Never rely on browser constraints for security or business rules. |
| 387 | Do not store session tokens or secrets in script-readable browser storage. | OWASP HTML5 security guidance warns against storing sensitive data in localStorage/sessionStorage where scripts can read it. | [OWASP HTML5 Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/HTML5_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Prefer hardened cookies for sessions where appropriate; XSS still remains dangerous. |
| 388 | Render untrusted data through safe sinks and context-aware encoding. | OWASP XSS prevention guidance distinguishes HTML, attribute, JavaScript, CSS, and URL contexts and recommends safe sinks. | [OWASP XSS Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross_Site_Scripting_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | CSP and Trusted Types are backup layers, not substitutes for correct output handling. |
| 389 | Build a semantic baseline before JavaScript enhancement. | Progressive-enhancement guidance expects core content and workflows to survive partial JavaScript/CSS failure. | [GOV.UK Progressive Enhancement](https://www.gov.uk/service-manual/technology/using-progressive-enhancement) | 4 | 4 | Rich clients are fine; core failure modes still need deliberate design. |
| 390 | Treat accessibility failures as correctness failures. | WCAG and ARIA guidance define testable behavior for names, roles, keyboard interaction, focus, states, and error handling. | [WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices Guide](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/) | 5 | 5 | Native controls first; ARIA that lies is worse than no ARIA. |
| 391 | Handle `fetch` as HTTP plus async failure, not just a promise. | MDN Fetch and AbortSignal docs distinguish HTTP statuses, network errors, aborts, timeouts, parsing errors, and body consumption failures. | [MDN Fetch API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Fetch_API) | 5 | 4 | Check status, parse deliberately, and propagate cancellation. |
| 392 | Browser cache/offline behavior must preserve freshness, privacy, and user intent. | Cache and service-worker guidance require explicit strategies for freshness, invalidation, and sensitive data. | [web.dev - Service worker caching and HTTP caching](https://web.dev/articles/service-worker-caching-and-http-caching) | 4 | 4 | Stale data is a product decision, not a default. |
| 393 | Treat principles as tools, not verdicts. | Practitioner debates around Clean Code, SOLID, DRY, and microservices converge on applying rules only after naming the local problem, payoff, and failure mode. | [A Philosophy of Software Design - Talks](https://web.stanford.edu/~ouster/cgi-bin/cs190-winter21/lecture.php?topic=talks) | 4 | 5 | A commandment without context becomes a ritual. |
| 394 | An abstraction must hide more complexity than it adds. | Ousterhout's deep-module framing and wrong-abstraction critiques warn that shallow wrappers and tiny-method scatter can raise cognitive load. | [Sandi Metz - The Wrong Abstraction](https://sandimetz.com/blog/2016/1/20/the-wrong-abstraction) | 4 | 5 | Prefer deep modules with small interfaces over layers that merely rename work. |
| 395 | DRY knowledge, not similar-looking code. | Pragmatic Programmer DRY framing treats duplication of knowledge/source-of-truth as the real problem, not every repeated shape of code. | [Pragmatic Programmer - DRY](https://media.pragprog.com/articles/may_04_oo1.pdf) | 4 | 5 | Do not merge coincidentally similar code that represents different business concepts. |
| 396 | Duplicate before you generalize when the domain is still moving. | Wrong-abstraction and AHA programming guidance argue early duplication can reveal the true pattern before abstraction couples cases incorrectly. | [Kent C. Dodds - AHA Programming](https://kentcdodds.com/blog/aha-programming) | 4 | 4 | Duplication is a learning tactic, not a license to let known facts diverge. |
| 397 | Delete a wrong abstraction instead of patching it forever. | Wrong-abstraction guidance treats growing parameters, conditionals, and special cases as evidence the abstraction has expired. | [Sandi Metz - The Wrong Abstraction](https://sandimetz.com/blog/2016/1/20/the-wrong-abstraction) | 4 | 5 | Removing an abstraction can be the cleanest refactor. |
| 398 | SOLID is a change-axis diagnostic, not a class-count target. | SOLID/SRP sources and critiques agree that principles help identify axes of change but can create ceremony when applied literally. | [Uncle Bob - Single Responsibility Principle](https://blog.cleancoder.com/uncle-bob/2014/05/08/SingleReponsibilityPrinciple.html) | 4 | 4 | Splitting every small behavior into a class can increase dependency clutter. |
| 399 | Make modules before services. | Fowler's microservices guidance warns that microservices require operational maturity and stable boundaries; otherwise distributed complexity arrives too early. | [Martin Fowler - Monolith First](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/MonolithFirst.html) | 5 | 5 | Service boundaries need independent deployment, scale, ownership, and operations readiness. |
| 400 | Measure performance, but do not design performance away. | Knuth-context and performance critiques warn against tiny unmeasured efficiencies while still respecting critical-path performance and abstraction costs. | [EmbeddedRelated - Premature Optimization](https://www.embeddedrelated.com/showarticle/141.php) | 3 | 5 | "Premature optimization" is not permission to ignore known performance constraints. |
| 401 | No ritual code. | Cargo-cult programming critiques warn against patterns, frameworks, and idioms the team cannot explain in local terms. | [NDepend - Cargo Cult Programming](https://blog.ndepend.com/cargo-cult-programming/) | 3 | 5 | If the team cannot explain why the pattern works here, it does not belong yet. |
| 402 | YAGNI means no speculative feature, not no design. | Fowler's YAGNI framing discourages imagined future features while still allowing design that keeps real future change cheap. | [Martin Fowler - YAGNI](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/Yagni.html) | 5 | 4 | Do not build imagined futures; keep code easy to refactor when real futures arrive. |
| 403 | Bound every resource-consuming path. | NASA/JPL requires fixed loop bounds in critical code, and performance guidance extends the same idea to queues, retries, fanout, concurrency, and budgets. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Unbounded work becomes latency spikes, outages, denial of service, memory pressure, or runaway cost. |
| 404 | Put explicit budgets on latency, memory, CPU, I/O, network, and spend. | Performance budgets and SRE monitoring turn resource goals into reviewable engineering constraints. | [web.dev - Performance budgets 101](https://web.dev/articles/performance-budgets-101) | 4 | 5 | A budget makes resource use a design requirement rather than an after-the-fact complaint. |
| 405 | Optimize percentiles and saturation, not averages. | SRE and Azure guidance emphasize that tail latency and saturation reveal user pain and overload that averages hide. | [Google SRE - Service Level Objectives](https://sre.google/sre-book/service-level-objectives/) | 5 | 5 | Users experience outliers, queues, and slow paths, not mean behavior. |
| 406 | Design overload behavior before overload happens. | Google SRE recommends graceful degradation, load shedding, and overload handling to prevent cascading failures. | [Google SRE - Addressing Cascading Failures](https://sre.google/sre-book/addressing-cascading-failures/) | 5 | 5 | Every finite system can be overloaded; undefined overload behavior usually becomes an outage. |
| 407 | Treat retries as load. | AWS warns that retries can worsen overload and recommends limiting retries with backoff, jitter, idempotency, and budgets. | [AWS Well-Architected - Control and limit retry calls](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/2025-02-25/framework/rel_mitigate_interaction_failure_limit_retries.html) | 5 | 5 | A failed request can still consume downstream capacity; retry storms are self-inflicted traffic spikes. |
| 408 | Capacity-plan from real demand and production-like load tests. | Azure and AWS guidance connect capacity planning and realistic load tests to both performance and cost control. | [Azure Well-Architected - Capacity planning](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/well-architected/performance-efficiency/capacity-planning) | 5 | 5 | Autoscaling is not a substitute for knowing peak demand, redundancy needs, bottlenecks, and limits. |
| 409 | Track cost per unit of value. | FinOps unit economics ties cloud spend to units such as users, requests, transactions, or workloads instead of total bill size alone. | [FinOps Foundation - Unit Economics](https://www.finops.org/framework/capabilities/unit-economics/) | 4 | 5 | Growth is only healthy if the cost curve remains understood and intentional. |
| 410 | Give every recurring resource cost an owner and purpose. | FinOps allocation guidance uses tags, labels, hierarchies, and accountability to make ongoing cost visible and governable. | [FinOps Foundation - Allocation](https://www.finops.org/framework/capabilities/allocation/) | 4 | 4 | Unowned resources, jobs, dashboards, environments, and data copies quietly compound. |
| 411 | Make data movement a first-class design cost. | AWS cost guidance and Android performance guidance show that bytes affect cost, latency, battery, quotas, and reliability. | [AWS Well-Architected - Plan for data transfer charges](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/framework/cost-08.html) | 5 | 5 | Moving data is never free; locality, batching, compression, and transfer paths are architecture decisions. |
| 412 | Collapse chatty remote calls into coarse, local, or batched operations. | Azure's chatty I/O antipattern warns against treating remote calls like local memory access. | [Azure Architecture Center - Chatty I/O antipattern](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/antipatterns/chatty-io/) | 5 | 5 | Round trips multiply latency, load, failure modes, and cost. |
