| 413 | Cache repeated expensive work intentionally and with bounds. | Azure's no-caching antipattern shows repeated fetching or computation can hurt latency, quotas, scalability, and data-access costs. | [Azure Architecture Center - No-Caching antipattern](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/antipatterns/no-caching/) | 5 | 4 | Caches need invalidation, size limits, freshness rules, and security boundaries. |
| 414 | Reuse expensive clients, connections, pools, and allocators. | Azure Functions guidance warns that connection limits can be exhausted and recommends reusing clients where appropriate. | [Azure Functions - Manage connections](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-functions/manage-connections) | 5 | 4 | Resource lifetimes often dominate behavior under concurrency and load. |
| 415 | Gate performance and cost regressions before rollout. | Performance budgets, load tests, and cost monitoring all point to catching resource regressions before they reach broad production. | [AWS Well-Architected - Load test your workload](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/performance-efficiency-pillar/perf_process_culture_load_test.html) | 5 | 5 | Small code or config changes can create large resource and bill changes. |
| 416 | Shed, defer, batch, downsample, or drop low-value work under pressure. | Green Software and mobile performance guidance emphasize energy, hardware, and network efficiency through doing less low-value work. | [Green Software Foundation - Introduction](https://learn.greensoftware.foundation/introduction/) | 4 | 4 | Not all work deserves immediate scarce capacity when user value or system health is at risk. |
| 417 | Break hidden shared-fate dependencies before they break you. | Cloudflare outage reports and SRE guidance show that hidden coupling can turn local failures or inefficient calls into global incidents. | [Cloudflare - September 12, 2025 dashboard and API outage](https://blog.cloudflare.com/deep-dive-into-cloudflares-sept-12-dashboard-and-api-outage/) | 5 | 5 | Shared fate should be deliberate, visible, and limited by failure domains. |
| 418 | Instrument the customer path before you ship it. | SRE monitoring centers latency, traffic, errors, and saturation as core signals for service health. | [Google SRE - Monitoring Distributed Systems](https://sre.google/sre-book/monitoring-distributed-systems/) | 5 | 5 | A feature that cannot be observed cannot be operated, defended, or improved safely. |
| 419 | Page only on actionable, user-impacting symptoms. | Google SRE on-call guidance ties paging to actionable, user-impacting conditions and warns against noise. | [Google SRE - Being On-Call](https://sre.google/sre-book/being-on-call/) | 5 | 5 | Noisy alerts train responders to ignore the system and burn out the people operating it. |
| 420 | Every alert must include ownership, context, and a next action. | Runbook and incident-response guidance expects alerts to point responders to owners, diagnostics, dashboards, and remediation paths. | [AWS Well-Architected - Use runbooks](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/framework/ops_ready_to_support_use_runbooks.html) | 5 | 5 | An alert that says only "something is wrong" is not operationally complete. |
| 421 | Treat runbooks as part of the feature. | AWS and incident-response guidance treat runbooks as operating artifacts needed for consistent response. | [AWS Well-Architected - Use runbooks](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/framework/ops_ready_to_support_use_runbooks.html) | 5 | 5 | If a feature can fail, the team needs triage steps, mitigations, permissions, and escalation paths. |
| 422 | Let SLOs and error budgets govern reliability tradeoffs. | Google SRE connects SLOs and error budgets to alerting, release policy, and reliability work. | [Google SRE Workbook - Implementing SLOs](https://sre.google/workbook/implementing-slos/) | 5 | 5 | Reliability decisions need explicit user-facing thresholds, not vibes. |
| 423 | Gate launches on operational readiness. | Google PRR, AWS ORR, and Azure checklists all treat owner, monitoring, rollback, capacity, support, and incident readiness as launch criteria. | [AWS Well-Architected - Operational Readiness Reviews](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/operational-readiness-reviews/wa-operational-readiness-reviews.html) | 5 | 5 | Shipping includes the ability to run, diagnose, support, and recover the code. |
| 424 | Test incident response as system behavior. | NIST and SRE guidance emphasize practicing incident management, disaster exercises, and failure-mode response. | [NIST SP 800-61 Rev. 3](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/61/r3/final) | 5 | 5 | Response paths fail like code paths; they need rehearsal, evidence, and improvement. |
| 425 | Mitigate first while preserving evidence. | Incident-response guidance prioritizes service restoration while retaining logs, timelines, and forensic context. | [Google SRE - Managing Incidents](https://sre.google/sre-book/managing-incidents/) | 5 | 5 | Destructive fixes can erase the data needed to prevent recurrence. |
| 426 | Close the postmortem loop with owned, tracked improvements. | SRE postmortem culture treats incidents as learning events that need concrete follow-up work. | [Google SRE - Postmortem Culture](https://sre.google/sre-book/postmortem-culture/) | 5 | 5 | A postmortem without fixes is documentation of repeated future failure. |
| 427 | Make operations humane by reducing toil, ambiguity, and wakeups. | SRE and on-call guidance connect alert quality, toil reduction, and clear action paths to sustainable operations. | [Google SRE - Being On-Call](https://sre.google/sre-book/being-on-call/) | 5 | 5 | Code quality includes the human cost of running it at bad hours. |
| 428 | Health checks must prove progress, not just process existence. | Watchdog guidance recommends proving each task is making progress before declaring a system healthy. | [Memfault - Watchdog best practices](https://interrupt.memfault.com/blog/firmware-watchdog-best-practices) | 4 | 5 | A process can be alive while workers, queues, dependencies, or schedulers are stuck. |
| 429 | Enter a known safe state on faults and preserve diagnostics. | NASA safety requirements call for safe initialization, safe transitions, off-nominal handling, and diagnostic preservation. | [NASA SWE-134](https://swehb.nasa.gov/spaces/7150/pages/16449641/SWE-134+-+Safety+Critical+Software+Requirements?macroName=div&desktop=true) | 5 | 5 | Failing safely beats continuing from unknown corrupted state. |
| 430 | Trace important requirements and risks to code, tests, and release evidence. | FDA software guidance and safety standards expect documented development, verification, validation, and risk evidence. | [FDA - Content of Premarket Submissions for Device Software Functions](https://www.fda.gov/media/153781/download) | 5 | 5 | Traceability shows what a change affects and what proof is missing. |
| 431 | Derive high-risk requirements from explicit threat, hazard, and failure analysis. | NASA software safety guidance centers hazard analysis, failure analysis, controls, and lifecycle safety assessment. | [NASA Software Safety Guidebook](https://standards.nasa.gov/sites/default/files/standards/NASA/Baseline/0/nasa-gb-871913.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Security, safety, privacy, and reliability work all start by naming what can go wrong. |
| 432 | Never allow a single software decision to be a catastrophic point of failure. | Therac-25 and aviation incident analyses show the danger of relying on one software path or assumption without independent control. | [NTSB 737 MAX safety report](https://www.ntsb.gov/investigations/accidentreports/reports/asr1901.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Redundancy, diversity, fail-safes, and graceful degradation are not only hardware ideas. |
| 433 | Revalidate reused, legacy, generated, and third-party code in the new context. | The Ariane 501 report shows reused software can fail when its old operating assumptions no longer hold. | [ESA - Ariane 501 Inquiry Board report](https://www.esa.int/Newsroom/Press_Releases/Ariane_501_-_Presentation_of_Inquiry_Board_report) | 5 | 5 | "Worked before" is not evidence that it works under today's inputs, timing, scale, or contracts. |
| 434 | Revalidate assumptions whenever design, environment, users, or requirements change. | FAA/JATR review findings around MCAS show how stale assumptions can survive as systems evolve. | [FAA/JATR 737 MAX report](https://www.faa.gov/sites/faa.gov/files/2021-08/Final_JATR_Submittal_to_FAA_Oct_2019.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Assumption drift is a root cause in mature systems. |
| 435 | Never rely on undefined, unspecified, or undocumented implementation behavior. | MISRA-style guidance and platform docs warn that dangerous implementation behavior must be avoided, documented, or deliberately constrained. | [MISRA C](https://misra.org.uk/) | 4 | 5 | Compilers, CPUs, runtimes, browsers, databases, and operating systems change underneath code. |
| 436 | Treat unsafe, FFI, raw-pointer, reflection, and unchecked code as contract boundaries. | Rust's reference makes clear that `unsafe` transfers proof obligations to the programmer and does not remove undefined-behavior rules. | [Rust Reference - Behavior considered undefined](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/behavior-considered-undefined.html) | 5 | 5 | Every escape hatch needs a small surface, documented invariants, and tests around the boundary. |
| 437 | Prove indexes, lengths, pointer math, and buffer accesses before use. | CERT ARR30-C catalogs out-of-bounds pointer and subscript errors and their security consequences. | [CERT ARR30-C](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/rules/arrays-arr/arr30-c/) | 5 | 5 | Bounds bugs appear in parsers, arrays, collections, pagination, batching, and protocol framing. |
| 438 | Write down the platform and runtime guarantees your code depends on. | Linux memory-barrier documentation stresses explicit minimum guarantees and architecture-specific assumptions. | [Linux kernel - Memory barriers](https://docs.kernel.org/core-api/wrappers/memory-barriers.html) | 5 | 4 | Hidden platform assumptions break under new CPUs, filesystems, runtimes, containers, and cloud services. |
| 439 | Limit build variants and hidden code paths unless each is testable. | Power of Ten warns that conditional compilation and hidden branches explode test combinations and defeat analysis. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Feature flags, plugins, generated branches, and environment forks create the same coverage problem. |
| 440 | Keep warning debt at zero. | Power of Ten recommends compiling critical code with strict warnings and running static analysis with no warnings. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Warning debt teaches teams and tools that warning signals are optional. |
| 441 | Handle or explicitly waive every return value, status, and error. | Power of Ten and safety standards forbid silently ignoring return values in critical code. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Silent failure is portable across languages, APIs, commands, jobs, and service boundaries. |
| 442 | Enforce dangerous-language rules mechanically. | MISRA and CERT show that language subsets, static analysis, and deviation records reduce reliance on memory and taste. | [SEI CERT Coding Standards](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/) | 5 | 4 | Every language has sharp edges; local rules should be executable where possible. |
| 443 | Every user-facing error should say what happened and what to do next. | Google technical writing, Microsoft, GOV.UK, and NN/g guidance treat errors as recovery aids, not just failure labels. | [Google - Write helpful error messages](https://developers.google.com/tech-writing/error-messages) | 5 | 5 | UI, CLI, API, SDK, and support errors should help the user or operator recover. |
| 444 | Put human language first and stable codes second. | GOV.UK and Apple guidance discourage opaque technical messages for users while API guidance supports stable codes for machines. | [GOV.UK Design System - Error message](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/error-message/) | 5 | 5 | Humans should not parse enum names, stack traces, or hex codes; machines need stable identifiers. |
| 445 | Attach the error to the thing that failed. | GOV.UK error-summary and field-error guidance requires both summary and local messages for fixable validation errors. | [GOV.UK Design System - Error summary](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/error-summary/) | 5 | 5 | Include field names, paths, row numbers, object IDs, request IDs, or operation names. |
| 446 | Preserve user work and failed input. | GOV.UK and Material guidance recommend preserving entered data when validation fails. | [Material Design - Errors](https://m1.material.io/patterns/errors.html) | 4 | 5 | Retry and repair should not force users or operators to recreate state from scratch. |
| 447 | Classify failures by cause and recoverability. | Google AIP-193 and Stripe error docs distinguish validation, auth, quota, not-found, conflict, transient, and internal failures. | [Google AIP-193 - Errors](https://google.aip.dev/193) | 5 | 5 | Correct handling depends on whether a failure is retryable, user-fixable, terminal, or internal. |
| 448 | Never report failure as success. | HTTP semantics and API guidance rely on status classes that truthfully distinguish success from client and server errors. | [RFC 9110 - HTTP Semantics](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9110) | 5 | 5 | Exit codes, booleans, statuses, promises, and events must honestly represent outcome. |
| 449 | Treat display text as mutable; branch only on stable error identifiers. | Google API error guidance separates stable machine-readable details from human-facing messages. | [Google AIP-193 - Errors](https://google.aip.dev/193) | 5 | 5 | English copy changes for clarity, localization, and policy; clients need stable contracts. |
| 450 | Document error codes, meanings, and fixes. | Twilio and Google API docs provide error catalogs that explain causes and remediation. | [Twilio Error Dictionary](https://www.twilio.com/docs/api/errors) | 4 | 4 | Public APIs, CLIs, SDKs, jobs, and internal tools all need diagnosis and remediation references. |
| 451 | Bulk work must be atomic or report structured per-item failures. | Google error guidance warns that partial errors add complexity and must be explicitly modeled when needed. | [Google AIP-193 - Errors](https://google.aip.dev/193) | 5 | 4 | Imports, queues, migrations, batch APIs, and background jobs must make partial success visible and replayable. |
| 452 | Keep error taxonomy consistent across screens, endpoints, versions, logs, and support channels. | GOV.UK, Microsoft API guidance, and API practitioners emphasize consistent structures and messages. | [Microsoft Azure REST API Guidelines](https://github.com/microsoft/api-guidelines/blob/vNext/azure/Guidelines.md) | 5 | 5 | One taxonomy should drive UI copy, API payloads, SDK exceptions, metrics, docs, and support playbooks. |
| 453 | Use the least disruptive error surface that still enables recovery. | Apple alert guidance reserves disruptive alerts for cases that truly require interruption. | [Apple - Writing Great Alerts](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2017/813/) | 5 | 4 | Inline, return-value, status, or background errors are better when local recovery is possible. |
| 454 | Centralize error boundaries, but recover locally only when recovery is real. | OWASP recommends global error handling while practitioners warn against catching errors that cannot be handled. | [OWASP Error Handling Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Error_Handling_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 4 | Libraries should expose typed failures; applications should map them consistently at process, API, and UI edges. |
| 455 | Put correlation IDs on every failure path. | W3C Trace Context and API support guidance make request or trace identifiers central to joining user reports, API responses, logs, and traces. | [W3C Trace Context](https://www.w3.org/TR/trace-context/) | 5 | 5 | Debugging should start from one identifier that crosses system boundaries. |
| 456 | Treat data as a production interface with contracts. | TFX data-validation work treats data quality as production-critical infrastructure, not a model-side detail. | [Data Validation for Machine Learning](https://mlsys.org/Conferences/2019/doc/2019/167.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Every data boundary needs schemas, invariants, ownership, and compatibility rules. |
| 457 | Version data, transformations, parameters, and artifacts together. | DVC pipelines use versioned pipeline definitions and lock files to make data workflows reproducible. | [DVC - Data Pipelines](https://doc.dvc.org/start/data-pipelines/data-pipelines) | 4 | 5 | Reproducibility requires immutable inputs plus executable steps, not just source code commits. |
| 458 | Never evaluate with information unavailable at decision time. | Data-leakage research and ML guidance show future or test-set contamination inflates results and fails in deployment. | [Leakage in Data Mining](https://www.cs.umb.edu/~ding/history/470_670_fall_2011/papers/cs670_Tran_PreferredPaper_LeakingInDataMining.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Tests, analytics, migrations, and simulations must respect the same time and authority boundaries as production. |
| 459 | Reuse production and validation code paths, or continuously test their divergence. | Google ML rules identify training-serving skew as a common failure from mismatched data handling. | [Google - Rules of Machine Learning](https://developers.google.com/machine-learning/guides/rules-of-ml) | 5 | 5 | Duplicate logic across environments drifts unless divergence is deliberate and tested. |
| 460 | Test the whole pipeline, not just the clever component. | The ML Test Score calls for integration tests spanning data sources, feature creation, training, and serving. | [The ML Test Score](https://research.google/pubs/the-ml-test-score-a-rubric-for-ml-production-readiness-and-technical-debt-reduction/) | 5 | 5 | System bugs usually live between components. |
| 461 | Make reproducibility a release gate. | The ML Test Score recommends retraining on identical data and comparing aggregate, sliced, and example-level outputs. | [The ML Test Score PDF](https://research.google.com/pubs/archive/45742.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Builds, migrations, analyses, model artifacts, and generated outputs must be replayable. |
| 462 | Evaluate on real usage slices, future data, and impact metrics. | ML Test Score and Google guidance warn aggregate metrics can hide slice failures and stale temporal assumptions. | [The ML Test Score PDF](https://research.google.com/pubs/archive/45742.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Quality claims need coverage across populations, edge cases, time, and user impact. |
| 463 | Own every upstream data dependency. | Hidden Technical Debt in ML Systems flags undeclared consumers, unstable upstream data, and missing producer SLOs as major risks. | [Hidden Technical Debt in ML Systems](https://papers.nips.cc/paper/5656-hidden-technical-debt-in-machine-learning-systems) | 5 | 5 | External data and service dependencies need owners, versions, contracts, SLOs, and change notice. |
| 464 | Carry provenance with every artifact and decision. | NIST AI RMF playbook asks teams to document data sources, transformations, labels, dependencies, constraints, and metadata. | [NIST AI RMF Manage Playbook](https://airc.nist.gov/airmf-resources/playbook/manage/) | 5 | 5 | Debugging, audits, rollback, and compliance require lineage. |
| 465 | Ship artifacts with intended use, limits, and evaluation context. | Model Cards propose standard documentation of intended use, evaluation conditions, and limitations. | [Model Cards for Model Reporting](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.03993) | 5 | 4 | Models, libraries, APIs, datasets, and tools all need usage contracts and caveats. |
| 466 | Monitor production for drift, staleness, data quality, and rollback triggers. | Google Cloud model-monitoring guidance emphasizes ongoing monitoring because quality shifts after deployment. | [Google Cloud - Model monitoring](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/machine-learning/model-monitoring/using-model-monitoring) | 4 | 5 | Deployment begins the operational lifecycle; assumptions decay in production. |
| 467 | Design feedback loops deliberately. | Hidden Technical Debt and incident analyses warn that deployed systems can change the data and behavior they later consume. | [The Parable of Google Flu](https://gking.harvard.edu/publication/the-parable-of-google-flu-traps-in-big-data-analysis/) | 5 | 5 | Recommendation, ranking, moderation, automation, and alerting systems all influence their own future inputs. |
| 468 | Treat labels, oracles, and ground truth as data products. | Human-label-variation and data-cascade research show that weak labeling and oracle assumptions create downstream failures. | [Human Label Variation](https://aclanthology.org/2022.emnlp-main.731/) | 5 | 4 | Test oracles, acceptance criteria, labels, and annotations need provenance, review, and maintenance. |
| 469 | Delete stale experiments, features, configs, and code paths. | Hidden Technical Debt ties pipeline jungles, dead experiments, and configuration debt to maintenance risk. | [Hidden Technical Debt in ML Systems](https://papers.nips.cc/paper/5656-hidden-technical-debt-in-machine-learning-systems) | 5 | 4 | Unused flags, branches, experiments, and configs are latent production risk. |
| 470 | Use the idioms of the runtime you are in. | Effective Go warns against transliterating designs from other languages; language conventions encode runtime-specific tradeoffs around errors, concurrency, ownership, and APIs. | [Effective Go](https://go.dev/doc/effective_go) | 5 | 4 | Cross-stack principles survive, but direct design translation often imports the wrong failure model. |
| 471 | Prefer local synchronous control flow unless concurrency is part of the feature. | Go review guidance notes synchronous functions are easier to reason about, test, and compose; callers can add concurrency more easily than remove hidden concurrency. | [Go Code Review Comments](https://go.dev/wiki/CodeReviewComments) | 4 | 4 | Hidden async work creates lifetime, cancellation, race, and testing complexity. |
| 472 | Test behavior and public contracts, not implementation details. | Google testing guidance warns that tests tied to implementation details become brittle and fail during behavior-preserving refactors. | [Google Testing Blog - Test behavior, not implementation](https://testing.googleblog.com/2013/08/testing-on-toilet-test-behavior-not.html) | 4 | 5 | Tests should protect user-visible guarantees, not private mechanics. |
| 473 | Preserve every generated counterexample as a replayable regression. | Hypothesis and fuzzing guidance rely on reproducing and shrinking generated failures, then keeping them as regression cases. | [Hypothesis documentation](https://hypothesis.readthedocs.io/en/latest/) | 5 | 5 | Randomized testing is useful only when failures can be reproduced, minimized, and retained. |
| 474 | Build fuzz targets as real test harnesses, not tool invocations. | libFuzzer and OSS-Fuzz guidance show fuzzing effectiveness depends on isolated, deterministic, fast, coverage-deep targets. | [libFuzzer documentation](https://llvm.org/docs/LibFuzzer.html) | 5 | 4 | Bad harnesses give false confidence; good harnesses explore meaningful input boundaries. |
| 475 | Run deep fuzzing continuously where risk justifies it, and short fuzzing in CI. | ClusterFuzzLite and OSS-Fuzz support both PR-oriented and long-running fuzzing models. | [ClusterFuzzLite](https://google.github.io/clusterfuzzlite/) | 5 | 4 | Match fuzzing duration to feedback loop: quick for changes, deep in the background. |
| 476 | Use mutation testing to audit assertion strength, not to chase perfect scores. | Mutation-testing tools and industrial research show surviving mutants reveal weak assertions, while full mutation adequacy can be impractical. | [Industrial Application of Mutation Testing](https://homes.cs.washington.edu/~rjust/publ/industrial_mutation_icst_2018.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Treat mutants as review prompts for critical logic, not universal busywork. |
| 477 | Put executable contracts at independently deployed boundaries. | Consumer-driven contract testing verifies provider behavior against consumer expectations without requiring every flow to be full end-to-end. | [Pact documentation](https://docs.pact.io/) | 5 | 5 | Service, SDK, event, schema, and API boundaries need consumer-owned expectations. |
| 478 | Do not let contract tests replace all integration or end-to-end tests. | Contract-testing guidance frames contracts as focused boundary checks, while full integration still catches emergent cross-system behavior. | [Microsoft - Pact contract testing](https://devblogs.microsoft.com/ise/pact-contract-testing-because-not-everything-needs-full-integration-tests/) | 4 | 4 | Contracts reduce E2E load; they do not prove whole workflows. |
| 479 | Root-cause flakiness by removing nondeterminism. | Google and Microsoft flaky-test research identify time, order, async waits, shared state, network, and external services as common flake sources. | [Google Testing Blog - Test flakiness](https://testing.googleblog.com/2020/12/test-flakiness-one-of-main-challenges.html) | 4 | 5 | Determinism is a design constraint for tests and testable code. |
| 480 | Test failure modes as first-class behavior. | AWS reliability guidance requires testing retries, overload, and injected failures because degraded behavior is part of correctness. | [AWS Well-Architected - Failure injection resiliency](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/reliability-pillar/rel_testing_resiliency_failure_injection_resiliency.html) | 5 | 5 | Correct code includes timeouts, cancellation, retries, partial failure, overload, and resource exhaustion behavior. |
| 481 | Run chaos and fault injection as controlled experiments. | Chaos Engineering principles and AWS FIS guidance emphasize steady state, hypotheses, guardrails, blast-radius limits, and stop conditions. | [Principles of Chaos Engineering](https://principlesofchaos.org/) | 4 | 4 | Failure injection is engineering only when measured and bounded. |
| 482 | Choose testing techniques by risk, feedback speed, and diagnosability. | Google testing and test-pyramid guidance frame test strategy as a tradeoff between speed, fidelity, and failure clarity. | [Software Engineering at Google - Larger Testing](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch14.html) | 5 | 5 | Advanced testing is valuable only when it buys confidence at acceptable cost. |
| 483 | Treat every published surface as a contract. | Google AIP-180 defines source, wire, and semantic compatibility for APIs; the same idea applies to CLIs, schemas, events, files, errors, and environment variables. | [Google AIP-180 - Backwards compatibility](https://google.aip.dev/180) | 4 | 5 | Consumers depend on more than code signatures. |
| 484 | Preserve round-trip compatibility across supported versions. | Kubernetes deprecation policy requires API objects to round-trip between versions without information loss. | [Kubernetes Deprecation Policy](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/using-api/deprecation-policy/) | 5 | 5 | Persistent state and distributed systems often run old and new code at the same time. |
| 485 | Tombstone retired identifiers. | Protocol Buffers guidance requires reserving removed field numbers and names to prevent accidental reuse. | [Protocol Buffers - Proto3 guide](https://protobuf.dev/programming-guides/proto3/) | 5 | 5 | Field IDs, enum values, database IDs, message types, and error codes become historical facts. |
| 486 | New inputs must be optional or defaulted. | API and schema-evolution guidance treats new required request fields as breaking because old producers and callers cannot supply them. | [Google AIP-180 - Backwards compatibility](https://google.aip.dev/180) | 4 | 5 | Required data breaks old writers, configs, clients, events, and forms. |
| 487 | Be strict in what you emit and deliberate in what you accept. | RFC 9413 updates Postel's Law by warning that excessive tolerance can create long-term interoperability and security problems. | [RFC 9413 - Maintaining Robust Protocols](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9413) | 5 | 4 | Parsers, services, CLIs, and importers should avoid both brittle rejection and silent ambiguity. |
| 488 | Consumers must tolerate unknown additive data. | Protobuf and Microsoft Graph guidance depend on old consumers safely ignoring unknown additive fields or derived types. | [Microsoft Graph versioning and support](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/versioning-and-support) | 5 | 5 | Additive evolution only works if consumers do not crash on unfamiliar data. |
| 489 | Treat events and webhooks as at-least-once, unordered, and replayable. | Stripe webhook docs describe retries, manual replays, duplicate handling, and no guaranteed ordering. | [Stripe Webhooks](https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks) | 5 | 5 | Async systems need idempotency, dedupe keys, reconciliation, and replay handling. |
| 490 | Enforce compatibility with machines, not memory. | Schema registries and contract tests automate backward, forward, and consumer/provider compatibility checks. | [Confluent Schema Evolution](https://docs.confluent.io/platform/current/schema-registry/fundamentals/schema-evolution.html) | 5 | 5 | CI catches boundary breakage humans miss. |
| 491 | Keep preview and stable contracts separate. | Microsoft Graph and Kubernetes give beta/alpha APIs weaker guarantees than stable APIs. | [Microsoft Graph versioning and support](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/versioning-and-support) | 5 | 5 | Experimental features need explicit blast-radius and compatibility boundaries. |
| 492 | Compatibility includes behavior, errors, latency, limits, and semantics, not just shape. | Microsoft Graph API guidance treats error-code, pagination, latency, rate-limit, and concurrency changes as potentially breaking. | [Microsoft Graph REST API Guidelines](https://github.com/microsoft/api-guidelines/blob/vNext/graph/GuidelinesGraph.md) | 5 | 5 | Consumers depend on timing, retries, ordering, errors, limits, and side effects. |
| 493 | Keep ownership current. | Kubernetes OWNERS files and CHAOSS project-health metrics treat active ownership and low bus factor as maintainability concerns. | [Kubernetes OWNERS files](https://www.kubernetes.dev/docs/guide/owners/) | 5 | 5 | Stale ownership is false governance; critical systems need active owners and backups. |
| 494 | Triage is maintenance work. | Kubernetes issue-triage guidance defines intake, categorization, priority, and ownership as ongoing project work. | [Kubernetes Issue Triage Guidelines](https://www.kubernetes.dev/docs/guide/issue-triage/) | 5 | 5 | Every issue needs type, priority, area, owner, next action, or closure. |
| 495 | Close work you will not do with a reason. | Open Source Guides recommends closing unwanted contributions promptly and clearly rather than leaving them to stall. | [Open Source Guides - Best Practices for Maintainers](https://opensource.guide/best-practices/) | 4 | 4 | "Won't fix" is healthier than silent backlog debt. |
| 496 | Do not merge code without its maintenance contract. | OpenSSF best-practices criteria require contribution process, docs, versioning, issue reporting, tests, CI, and vulnerability response. | [OpenSSF Best Practices Criteria](https://www.bestpractices.dev/en/criteria/0) | 5 | 5 | Definition of done should include owner, tests, docs, rollout, monitoring, migration notes, and support path. |
| 497 | Deprecation is a project, not an annotation. | Google's deprecation guidance frames deprecation around discovery, migration, backsliding prevention, and removal milestones. | [Software Engineering at Google - Deprecation](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch15.html) | 5 | 5 | Deprecations need owners, users, dates, migration docs, blocking checks, and final cleanup. |
| 498 | Block new uses of deprecated systems. | Google calls backsliding prevention a commonly overlooked part of deprecation, using warnings and static analysis to stop new dependencies. | [Software Engineering at Google - Deprecation](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch15.html) | 5 | 5 | Otherwise cleanup targets keep moving. |
| 499 | Design replacements with migration in mind from day one. | Google's large-scale-change guidance emphasizes successor systems that can be migrated automatically and safely. | [Software Engineering at Google - Large-Scale Changes](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch22.html) | 5 | 4 | New platforms need adapters, codemods, dual-read/write plans, and migration telemetry. |
| 500 | Archive or transfer unmaintained code; do not pretend it is supported. | GitHub archiving makes repositories read-only and signals they are no longer actively maintained. | [GitHub - Archiving repositories](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/archiving-a-github-repository/archiving-repositories) | 5 | 4 | Dormant repos, services, packages, and jobs need explicit support status. |
| 501 | Use automation as guardrails, not as a substitute for judgment. | OpenSSF Scorecard automates project-health checks, while maintainer discussions warn that mechanical closure can damage trust and signal. | [OpenSSF Scorecard](https://github.com/ossf/scorecard) | 5 | 4 | Automate detection and policy checks; keep human-owned decisions for closure, removal, and exceptions. |
| 502 | Tie every personal-data field to a specific purpose. | GDPR purpose limitation and Apple privacy disclosures require data use to be specific and transparent. | [GDPR Article 5](https://gdpr-info.eu/art-5-gdpr/) | 4 | 5 | Schemas, events, logs, analytics, and exports should not contain purposeless personal data. |
| 503 | Process data locally or ephemerally unless persistence is necessary. | Apple privacy guidance promotes on-device processing, and privacy frameworks reduce risk by minimizing collection and processing. | [Apple - Build privacy into your app](https://developer.apple.com/news/?id=ezrmhwjq) | 4 | 5 | Local processing and short-lived request data reduce breach, retention, and consent burden. |
| 504 | Ask for permissions at the point of need and in the smallest scope. | Android and Apple privacy guidance favor permission minimization and user-initiated access. | [Android privacy and security checklist](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/about) | 4 | 5 | Broad upfront permissions reduce agency and increase data exposure. |
| 505 | Make consent as easy to withdraw as to give. | GDPR Article 7 requires withdrawal to be as easy as giving consent. | [GDPR Article 7](https://gdpr-info.eu/art-7-gdpr/) | 4 | 5 | Consent paths need revocation paths in code, data state, and downstream processors. |
| 506 | Build data access, correction, export, and deletion into the data model. | GDPR rights and NIST privacy guidance require operational paths for access, portability, correction, and erasure. | [GDPR Article 20](https://gdpr-info.eu/art-20-gdpr/) | 4 | 5 | Data rights should not be manual support tickets against unindexed storage. |
| 507 | Retention must be executable policy. | GDPR storage limitation, NIST privacy controls, and logging guidance reject indefinite data keeping. | [NIST Privacy Framework Core](https://www.nist.gov/document/nist-privacy-framework-version-1-core-pdf) | 5 | 5 | Encode TTLs, purges, legal holds, backup expiry, and retention tests. |
| 508 | Treat third-party SDKs, vendors, and tracking pixels as part of your data system. | Apple privacy disclosures make developers responsible for third-party SDK data practices; NIST models privacy across the processing ecosystem. | [Apple App Privacy Details](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/app-privacy-details/) | 4 | 5 | Vendor code, outbound domains, and analytics tags need inventory, approval, monitoring, and contracts. |
| 509 | Tell users what the system actually does. | GDPR transparency rules require disclosure of purposes, recipients, retention, rights, and automated decisions. | [GDPR Article 13](https://gdpr-info.eu/art-13-gdpr/) | 4 | 5 | Privacy labels and notices should match actual code, sensors, events, vendors, and storage. |
| 510 | Avoid cross-context identity linking unless essential and explicit. | W3C privacy principles and Apple privacy guidance warn against unwanted tracking across apps, sites, contexts, and data brokers. | [W3C Privacy Principles](https://www.w3.org/TR/privacy-principles/) | 4 | 5 | Separate analytics IDs from account IDs, rotate identifiers, and avoid fingerprinting by default. |
| 511 | Apply heightened necessity tests for sensitive data and power-imbalanced users. | ICO and GDPR guidance treat health, employment, special-category, and automated-decision contexts as higher-risk processing. | [ICO worker health information guidance](https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/employment/information-about-workers-health/data-protection-and-workers-health-information/) | 5 | 5 | Health, biometric, child, location, financial, and employment data need stricter review and safer defaults. |
| 512 | Provide human appeal for consequential automated decisions. | GDPR Article 22 creates rights around solely automated decisions with significant effects. | [GDPR Article 22](https://gdpr-info.eu/art-22-gdpr/) | 4 | 4 | Bans, credit, hiring, pricing, and access decisions need reason codes, review queues, and override audit trails. |
| 513 | Keep point-in-time history for recoverability. | PostgreSQL and MySQL point-in-time recovery require logs in addition to snapshots; RDS supports restore to selected times. | [PostgreSQL - Continuous Archiving and PITR](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/continuous-archiving.html) | 5 | 5 | Recovery usually means "before the bad write", not merely "latest backup." |
| 514 | Every irreversible operation needs a pre-existing recovery path. | GitLab and Atlassian incidents show accidental deletion can outpace recovery when recovery was not proven beforehand. | [GitLab database outage postmortem](https://about.gitlab.com/blog/postmortem-of-database-outage-of-january-31/) | 5 | 5 | Deletion is fast; reconstruction is slow and often incomplete. |
| 515 | Make deletion a lifecycle, not an event. | Google SRE and S3 Object Lock/versioning guidance support soft deletion, delayed purge, and protected object versions. | [Google SRE - Data Integrity](https://sre.google/sre-book/data-integrity/) | 5 | 5 | Rows, files, accounts, buckets, and customer sites need recoverable deletion windows. |
| 516 | Backfills must be resumable, bounded, throttled, and safe to rerun. | Shopify maintenance tasks and GitLab batched migrations support batching, throttling, interruption, and rerun safety for large data work. | [GitLab Batched Background Migrations](https://docs.gitlab.com/development/database/batched_background_migrations/) | 5 | 5 | Long jobs fail midstream; rerun safety prevents duplicates, gaps, and partial state. |
| 517 | Verify migrated state independently before cleanup. | Stripe migration guidance compares old and new paths before switching and cleaning up. | [Stripe - Online migrations](https://stripe.com/blog/online-migrations) | 5 | 5 | Cleanup is where reversible migration becomes irreversible. |
| 518 | Do not acknowledge persistence until data and namespace metadata are durable. | `fsync(2)` notes file data and directory entries have separate durability requirements; SQLite documents atomic commit mechanics. | [man7 fsync(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/fsync.2.html) | 5 | 5 | File saves, WALs, databases, and object stores all depend on correct durability boundaries. |
| 519 | Test storage guarantees under real faults. | Jepsen documents lost writes and consistency anomalies in real systems under fault conditions. | [Jepsen - Lost Write](https://jepsen.io/consistency/phenomena/lost-write) | 5 | 4 | Defaults, guarantees, and failure modes are subtle and compose badly. |
| 520 | Write cross-system side effects through a durable intent log or outbox. | AWS transactional-outbox guidance addresses inconsistency between database updates and message/event publication. | [AWS - Transactional outbox pattern](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/transactional-outbox.html) | 5 | 4 | Database plus queue, email, payment, webhook, or external API is a universal partial-failure trap. |
| 521 | Keep recovery-critical metadata outside the destructive blast radius. | Atlassian incident analysis and SRE guidance show recovery depends on identifiers, manifests, contacts, owners, and audit trails surviving the failure. | [Atlassian April 2022 outage PIR](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/atlassian-engineering/post-incident-review-april-2022-outage) | 5 | 4 | You cannot recover what you can no longer identify, route, or prove. |
| 522 | Choose the right text unit before slicing, counting, or limiting text. | Unicode text segmentation distinguishes bytes, code units, code points, grapheme clusters, words, and sentences. | [Unicode UAX #29 - Text Segmentation](https://www.unicode.org/reports/tr29/) | 5 | 5 | User-visible length limits, cursors, truncation, and search must not split combining marks, emoji sequences, or script clusters. |
| 523 | Use standard locale identifiers; do not invent locale strings. | W3C language-tag guidance and BCP 47 separate language, script, region, variants, and extensions. | [W3C - Language Tags and Locale Identifiers](https://www.w3.org/TR/ltli/) | 5 | 5 | Preserve tags like `zh-Hant-TW`; do not infer country, script, currency, calendar, or direction from language alone. |
| 524 | Use maintained locale data instead of hand-coded cultural rules. | CLDR supplies locale data for formats, names, calendars, collation, plural rules, and numbering systems used by major platforms. | [Unicode CLDR](https://cldr.unicode.org/) | 5 | 5 | Pin and upgrade locale data deliberately; test behavior after CLDR, ICU, browser, runtime, or OS updates. |
| 525 | Distinguish instant, offset, local time, and time zone. | W3C time-zone guidance treats these as different concepts with different persistence and display needs. | [W3C - Working with Time and Timezones](https://www.w3.org/TR/timezone/) | 5 | 5 | Store IANA zone identifiers for future civil-time events; a UTC offset alone is not a time zone. |
| 526 | Treat calendars, weeks, weekends, and first-day-of-week as locale data. | Unicode LDML defines localized calendar, week, and date-field behavior beyond a single Gregorian or ISO assumption. | [Unicode LDML Part 4 - Dates](https://unicode.org/reports/tr35/tr35-dates.html) | 5 | 4 | Test non-Gregorian calendars, week-year boundaries, week 53, first-day rules, and local weekend definitions. |
| 527 | Separate locale-sensitive casing from locale-neutral matching. | Unicode and platform casing guidance shows that lowercasing and uppercasing are not universal operations across languages. | [ICU - Case Mappings](https://unicode-org.github.io/icu/userguide/transforms/casemappings.html) | 5 | 4 | Use invariant case folding for machine keys; use locale casing only for user-facing display text. |
| 528 | Use plural and select message formats for variable human text. | CLDR plural rules and ICU MessageFormat support language-specific categories beyond English singular and plural. | [ICU MessageFormat](https://unicode-org.github.io/icu/userguide/format_parse/messages/) | 5 | 5 | Keep variants in one translatable message, always provide `other`, and avoid string arithmetic like appending `s`. |
| 529 | Externalize complete translatable messages with context. | Localization guidance warns that concatenated fragments and reused source strings prevent correct grammar and word order. | [Microsoft - Message formatting](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/globalization/internationalization/message-formatting) | 4 | 5 | Give translators full sentences, named placeholders, descriptions, screenshots, and stable resource identifiers. |
| 530 | Design UI for translation expansion, wrapping, fonts, and localized assets. | Mozilla localization practices and platform guides treat layout flexibility as a prerequisite for real localization. | [Mozilla - Localization content best practices](https://mozilla-l10n.github.io/documentation/localization/dev_best_practices.html) | 4 | 4 | Avoid fixed text boxes and localizable text inside images; test long strings, font fallback, and asset variants. |
| 531 | Implement bidirectional text with metadata and logical layout. | W3C RTL guidance and Unicode bidi rules require direction metadata and isolation for mixed-direction content. | [W3C - Structural markup and right-to-left text](https://www.w3.org/International/questions/qa-html-dir) | 5 | 5 | Use `dir`, bidi isolation, and start/end layout properties; do not reorder text visually by hand. |
| 532 | Put internationalization cases in CI. | Pseudolocalization and international validation guidance find localization bugs before translation starts. | [Microsoft - Pseudolocalization](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/globalization/methodology/pseudolocalization) | 4 | 5 | Test pseudo-locales, RTL, DST, non-Gregorian calendars, plural-heavy locales, native digits, and long strings. |
| 533 | Authorize every request, object, action, and service boundary. | OWASP authorization guidance treats missing authorization checks as a high-impact access-control failure. | [OWASP Authorization Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Test role, owner, tenant, object state, and delegated authority at the data or action boundary. |
| 534 | Treat every server-side URL fetch as SSRF-sensitive. | OWASP SSRF guidance shows that server fetches can reach internal networks, metadata endpoints, and control planes. | [OWASP SSRF Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Server_Side_Request_Forgery_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Allowlist schemes and hosts, re-check resolved IPs, block private ranges, and revalidate redirects. |
| 535 | Keep data out of interpreters beyond SQL. | CWE command-injection guidance covers shells, commands, expressions, templates, LDAP, XPath, and other interpreter boundaries. | [CWE-78 - OS Command Injection](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/78.html) | 5 | 5 | Prefer structured APIs, argument arrays, allowlists, and sink-specific escaping; avoid `eval` and shell strings. |
| 536 | Do not use same-process language sandboxes as security boundaries. | Node.js documents that `vm` is not a security mechanism, and similar in-process sandboxes often fail under adversarial code. | [Node.js vm module](https://nodejs.org/api/vm.html) | 5 | 5 | Run untrusted code in isolated processes, containers, VMs, or sandboxes with CPU, memory, filesystem, syscall, and network limits. |
| 537 | Use approved cryptographic primitives and managed keys. | OWASP cryptographic-storage guidance warns against custom algorithms and weak key management. | [OWASP Cryptographic Storage Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cryptographic_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Use vetted libraries, AEAD modes, password-hashing standards, KMS or HSM-backed keys, and documented rotation. |
| 538 | Manage secrets through their full lifecycle. | OWASP secrets guidance covers creation, storage, access, rotation, revocation, monitoring, and incident response. | [OWASP Secrets Management Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Secrets_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Use short-lived scoped credentials, runtime injection, secret scanning, audit logs, and revocation playbooks. |
| 539 | Put abuse limits on valuable business flows. | OWASP API6:2023 covers abuse of legitimate business flows such as scalping, spam, scraping, and cost attacks. | [OWASP API6:2023 - Unrestricted Access to Sensitive Business Flows](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa6-unrestricted-access-to-sensitive-business-flows/) | 5 | 5 | Rate-limit by identity, tenant, device, action, object, and cost; add friction where automation creates harm. |
| 540 | Bound every user-controlled resource expansion. | OWASP API4:2023 covers resource exhaustion through payloads, pagination, queries, uploads, and expensive jobs. | [OWASP API4:2023 - Unrestricted Resource Consumption](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa4-unrestricted-resource-consumption/) | 5 | 5 | Cap size, depth, time, memory, queue length, fanout, decompression ratio, regex cost, and downstream spend. |
| 541 | Make tenant isolation mandatory across every shared surface. | OWASP multi-tenant guidance treats cross-tenant leakage as a central SaaS security risk. | [OWASP Multi-Tenant Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Multi_Tenant_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Include tenant context in data, caches, queues, files, logs, metrics, admin tools, and authorization policies. |
| 542 | Keep cloud metadata and control planes unreachable from application fetch paths. | AWS IMDSv2 guidance addresses attacks where application SSRF reaches ambient cloud credentials. | [AWS - Add defense in depth against open firewalls, reverse proxies, and SSRF vulnerabilities with enhancements to EC2 Instance Metadata Service](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/security/add-defense-in-depth-against-open-firewalls-reverse-proxies-and-ssrf-vulnerabilities-with-enhancements-to-the-ec2-instance-metadata-service/) | 5 | 5 | Require hardened metadata access, block metadata egress, avoid broad ambient credentials, and isolate control-plane routes. |
| 543 | Make builds hermetic. | Bazel hermeticity guidance defines builds that are insensitive to host-machine state and hidden inputs. | [Bazel - Hermeticity](https://bazel.build/basics/hermeticity) | 5 | 5 | Declare tools, dependencies, environment, network inputs, and generated sources explicitly; fail on undeclared access. |
| 544 | Make build outputs deterministic. | Reproducible-build guidance requires the same source and build environment to produce bit-for-bit equivalent artifacts. | [Reproducible Builds - Documentation](https://reproducible-builds.org/docs/) | 5 | 5 | Normalize timestamps, locale, paths, archive order, randomness, and filesystem traversal order. |
| 545 | Pin external build inputs by digest or immutable revision. | Secure-install and CI guidance warn that versions, tags, and remote action references can move or be compromised. | [pip - Secure installs](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/topics/secure-installs/) | 5 | 5 | Use hashes, image digests, checksums, lockfiles, and full commit SHAs for CI actions and remote downloads. |
| 546 | Verify provenance before deploy or consumption. | SLSA verification guidance turns provenance into an enforceable policy about source, builder, artifact, and parameters. | [SLSA - Verifying artifacts](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.0/verifying-artifacts) | 5 | 5 | Gate deploys on attestation checks for source repo, ref, builder identity, subjects, and signatures. |
| 547 | Sign artifacts and verify signatures. | Sigstore Cosign documents artifact signature verification tied to identity and transparency logs. | [Sigstore Cosign - Verify](https://docs.sigstore.dev/cosign/verifying/verify/) | 5 | 5 | Verify signatures, issuer, identity, certificate, transparency log, and digest before trusting an artifact. |
| 548 | Treat CI as production infrastructure. | OWASP CI/CD guidance treats pipelines as high-value systems with secrets, deploy authority, and write access. | [OWASP CI/CD Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/CI_CD_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Use least-privilege tokens, protected environments, OIDC, secret rotation, reviewed pipeline changes, and audit logs. |
| 549 | Isolate builders and runners, especially for untrusted work. | GitLab runner security guidance warns about shared runners, privileged containers, and secret leakage. | [GitLab Runner Security](https://docs.gitlab.com/runner/security/) | 5 | 5 | Prefer ephemeral runners, clean workspaces, restricted privileges, and separate runners for public or untrusted contributions. |
| 550 | Protect release sources with review, branch rules, and required checks. | OpenSSF Scorecard checks evaluate branch protection, reviews, token permissions, and CI controls. | [OpenSSF Scorecard Checks](https://github.com/ossf/scorecard/blob/main/docs/checks.md) | 5 | 5 | Require review, protected branches and tags, status checks, restricted release credentials, and audited exceptions. |
| 551 | Roll out releases assuming rollback may be needed. | Google SRE release guidance pairs releases with canaries, monitoring, and reliable rollback paths. | [Google Cloud - Reliable releases and rollbacks](https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/gcp/reliable-releases-and-rollbacks-cre-life-lessons) | 5 | 4 | Keep previous artifacts deployable, define rollback triggers, monitor SLOs, and limit blast radius through staged rollout. |
| 552 | Treat model output as untrusted data. | OWASP LLM guidance identifies insecure output handling as a core LLM application risk. | [OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications](https://owasp.org/www-project-top-10-for-large-language-model-applications/) | 5 | 5 | Validate, schema-check, escape, and sandbox output before feeding it to HTML, SQL, shell, code, workflows, or APIs. |
| 553 | Keep trusted instructions separate from untrusted content. | Anthropic and OpenAI prompt-injection guidance distinguish privileged instructions from data read from tools, documents, or users. | [Anthropic - Mitigate jailbreaks and prompt injections](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/test-and-evaluate/strengthen-guardrails/mitigate-jailbreaks) | 5 | 5 | Put web pages, emails, files, and retrieval results in typed data containers with source metadata, not in policy text. |
| 554 | Treat tool calls as proposals, not permissions. | OpenAI agent-security guidance routes side-effecting actions through system-level checks instead of raw model authority. | [OpenAI - Designing AI agents to resist prompt injection](https://openai.com/index/designing-agents-to-resist-prompt-injection/) | 5 | 5 | Deterministic policy code should authorize writes, sends, payments, deletes, and external calls before execution. |
| 555 | Give models only task-scoped authority. | Google SAIF controls emphasize limiting AI system access, privileges, data, and actions. | [Google SAIF Controls](https://saif.google/secure-ai-framework/controls) | 5 | 5 | Use per-request tool allowlists, scoped tokens, tenant isolation, expiring credentials, and disabled tools after untrusted reads. |
| 556 | Require human approval for high-impact AI-mediated actions. | Microsoft agent transparency guidance calls out human oversight for consequential autonomous actions. | [Microsoft - Agent Service transparency note](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/responsible-ai/agents/transparency-note) | 5 | 5 | Gate money movement, external messages, account changes, legal or medical actions, destructive writes, and safety-relevant decisions. |
| 557 | Put probabilistic model outputs behind deterministic schemas and policies. | Structured-output guidance constrains model responses so application logic can validate and decide. | [OpenAI Platform - Structured Outputs](https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/structured-outputs) | 5 | 5 | Use typed schemas, enums, validators, and policy engines; keep business decisions outside free-form prose. |
| 558 | Design for prompt-injection containment, not perfect detection. | NCSC guidance warns that prompt injection is an inherent challenge and should be managed with containment. | [NCSC - Prompt injection is not SQL injection](https://www.ncsc.gov.uk/blog-post/prompt-injection-is-not-sql-injection) | 5 | 5 | Assume detection fails; isolate tools, reduce privileges, separate context, and block direct bridges from untrusted text to privileged action. |
| 559 | Make evals first-class tests for AI behavior. | OpenAI evaluation guidance treats representative, adversarial, and regression examples as core engineering artifacts. | [OpenAI Platform - Evals](https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/evals) | 5 | 5 | Run golden tasks, prompt-injection cases, tool-call accuracy checks, refusal checks, and safety regressions in CI. |
| 560 | Monitor, trace, and audit every agent path. | Microsoft AI observability guidance treats prompts, outputs, tool calls, latency, cost, and evaluation signals as operational telemetry. | [Microsoft Foundry - Observability](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/concepts/observability) | 5 | 5 | Log prompts, retrieved sources, model outputs, tools, approvals, refusals, policy blocks, evaluator scores, and costs. |
| 561 | Bound loops, spend, and autonomy. | Microsoft LLM security planning recommends limits and controls for agentic application behavior. | [Microsoft - Security planning for LLM applications](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/playbook/technology-guidance/generative-ai/mlops-in-openai/security/security-plan-llm-application) | 5 | 4 | Set token, time, retry, queued-action, total-action, and cost budgets; fail closed on runaway behavior. |
| 562 | Treat currency scale and provider money rules as data. | Payment providers and ISO 4217 show that minor units and provider exceptions differ by currency and context. | [Stripe - Supported currencies](https://docs.stripe.com/currencies) | 5 | 5 | Do not assume cents or multiply by 100; version currency metadata and provider-specific exceptions. |
| 563 | Preserve value through rounding and allocation. | Fowler's Money pattern treats rounding and allocation as explicit logic because lost fractions create accounting discrepancies. | [Martin Fowler - Money](https://martinfowler.com/eaaCatalog/money.html) | 5 | 5 | Allocate remainders deterministically and assert that the sum of parts equals the original amount. |
| 564 | Model financial movement as balanced ledger entries. | Double-entry accounting patterns require every posting to be offset so money cannot appear or disappear silently. | [Martin Fowler - Accounting Transaction](https://martinfowler.com/eaaDev/AccountingTransaction.html) | 4 | 5 | Validate that ledger transactions sum to zero per currency before commit. |
| 565 | Keep ledgers append-only; correct with reversals. | Modern Treasury ledger guidance treats immutability as central to auditability and reconstruction. | [Modern Treasury - Enforcing immutability in your double-entry ledger](https://www.moderntreasury.com/journal/enforcing-immutability-in-your-double-entry-ledger) | 4 | 5 | Disable updates and deletes for posted entries; link reversal entries to originals. |
| 566 | Derive balances from immutable history or versioned snapshots. | Ledger engineering guides warn that mutable balances drift under retries, missed writes, and concurrency. | [Formance - Double-entry accounting for engineers](https://www.formance.com/blog/engineering/double-entry-accounting-for-engineers-building-financial-products) | 4 | 5 | Keep balances rebuildable from postings and make snapshots explicitly versioned or checkpointed. |
| 567 | Reconcile internal state to external systems. | Payment reconciliation guidance treats provider, payout, and bank records as independent checks on internal state. | [Stripe - Payment reconciliation](https://stripe.com/resources/more/payment-reconciliation-101) | 4 | 5 | Use clearing accounts, external references, exception queues, and explicit investigation instead of silent adjustments. |
| 568 | Model externally settled workflows as state machines. | Payment, refund, dispute, and settlement APIs expose multi-step asynchronous states rather than booleans. | [Square - Refund payments](https://developer.squareup.com/docs/payments-api/refund-payments) | 5 | 5 | Store transitions, timestamps, provider IDs, and terminal states; expect asynchronous updates and reversals. |
| 569 | Constrain reversals and refunds by the original transaction. | Refund APIs constrain reversal amount and identity against prior captures, payments, or settlements. | [PayPal - Refund captured payment](https://docs.paypal.ai/reference/api/rest/captures/refund-captured-payment) | 5 | 5 | Enforce cumulative refunded amount, original currency, original account, and idempotency per reversal intent. |
| 570 | Put concurrency control in the financial write path. | Ledger scaling guidance shows that concurrent valid operations can overspend or double-post without write-path controls. | [Modern Treasury - How to scale a ledger, part IV](https://www.moderntreasury.com/journal/how-to-scale-a-ledger-part-iv) | 4 | 5 | Use transactions, unique constraints, account versions, balance conditions, or serializable ledgers for critical writes. |
| 571 | Separate authorized, pending, posted, settled, available, disputed, and reversed funds. | Payment dispute and settlement lifecycles distinguish money states that are visible, available, final, or contested. | [PayPal - Dispute lifecycle stages](https://developer.paypal.com/docs/disputes/disputes-reference/) | 5 | 4 | Do not let pending or disputed funds behave like settled available funds. |
| 572 | Preserve audit metadata for financial and high-impact events. | Reconciliation and payout reports depend on stable provider IDs, batch IDs, actors, and timestamps. | [Stripe - Payout reconciliation report](https://docs.stripe.com/reports/payout-reconciliation) | 5 | 4 | Store actor, source, idempotency key, external ID, raw-event checksum, batch ID, and event time. |
| 573 | Treat network as synchronization, not the source of user-facing state. | Android offline-first guidance recommends observable local data with network updates feeding the local source. | [Android - Build an offline-first app](https://developer.android.com/topic/architecture/data-layer/offline-first) | 5 | 5 | Read from local state first; let network sync update durable local storage. |
| 574 | Persist user intent before attempting unreliable sync. | Offline storage guidance lets applications continue after disconnection by committing local writes and syncing later. | [Firebase Firestore - Access data offline](https://firebase.google.com/docs/firestore/manage-data/enable-offline) | 5 | 5 | Write domain state and outbox operation IDs atomically before network attempts. |
| 575 | Make conflict policy explicit product logic. | AWS AppSync conflict guidance requires choosing detection and resolution strategies for concurrent writes. | [AWS AppSync - Conflict detection and resolution](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/appsync/latest/devguide/conflict-detection-and-resolution.html) | 5 | 5 | Pick per model: reject, merge, CRDT, server authority, version retry, or human review. |
| 576 | Use convergence data structures when offline replicas must merge automatically. | Local-first research uses CRDTs where collaborative replicas need deterministic merges without a central coordinator. | [Ink and Switch - Local-first software](https://www.inkandswitch.com/essay/local-first/) | 5 | 4 | Use CRDTs for shared documents, counters, sets, and annotations when convergence beats central ordering. |
| 577 | Schedule durable background work with the platform scheduler. | Mobile platform docs require OS-managed background work for tasks that must survive UI closure and process death. | [Android - Persistent work](https://developer.android.com/develop/background-work/background-tasks/persistent) | 5 | 5 | Use WorkManager, background URL sessions, or platform equivalents with checkpoints and resumable chunks. |
| 578 | Scope UI async work to lifecycle and make cancellation safe. | Android lifecycle-aware coroutine guidance prevents stale UI writes, leaks, and duplicated work. | [Android - Lifecycle-aware coroutines](https://developer.android.com/topic/libraries/architecture/coroutines) | 5 | 4 | Keep durable sync outside UI scopes; cancellation should roll back, checkpoint, or be explicitly harmless. |
| 579 | Never order distributed writes by raw device clocks. | Firebase offline guidance exposes server time offsets because client clocks are unreliable for shared ordering. | [Firebase Realtime Database - Offline capabilities](https://firebase.google.com/docs/database/web/offline-capabilities) | 5 | 5 | Use server timestamps, versions, vector clocks, logical clocks, or hybrid logical clocks for conflict decisions. |
| 580 | Treat local storage as part of the consistency protocol. | SQLite transaction and WAL docs show that local durability and atomicity shape correctness under interruption. | [SQLite - Write-Ahead Logging](https://sqlite.org/wal.html) | 5 | 4 | Use local transactions, stable operation IDs, upserts, checkpoints, and migrations that preserve pending work. |
| 581 | Make degraded, pending, stale, and failed states visible. | Offline sync product guidance shows users and operators need explicit sync, conflict, and freshness states. | [Microsoft Field Service - Offline data sync](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dynamics365/field-service/mobile/offline-data-sync) | 5 | 4 | Show local, pending, failed, stale, and conflict states; log queue depth, retry count, and last successful sync. |
| 582 | Custom controls must expose name, role, value, and state. | WCAG and WAI-ARIA guidance require assistive technologies to receive the same operational model sighted users infer visually. | [W3C WCAG 2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/) | 5 | 5 | If native controls are impossible, explicitly implement accessible name, role, value, disabled, selected, expanded, and action semantics. |
| 583 | Manage focus deliberately across navigation, modals, menus, and errors. | GOV.UK and WAI-ARIA guidance treat focus position as the user's map for keyboard and assistive technology workflows. | [GOV.UK Design System - Focus states](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/get-started/focus-states/) | 5 | 5 | Keep focus visible, logical, restored after close, and not hidden behind overlays or sticky UI. |
| 584 | Announce dynamic interface changes intentionally. | WCAG status-message guidance covers async validation, loading, search results, autocomplete, and live updates that visual users notice automatically. | [W3C WCAG 2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/) | 5 | 4 | Use live regions or status roles sparingly, announce result counts and validation changes, and avoid stealing focus unnecessarily. |
| 585 | Respect reduced-motion preferences and user control over movement. | Platform accessibility guidance warns that animation, parallax, and auto-advancing content can cause nausea, dizziness, distraction, or task loss. | [Apple - Reduced Motion evaluation criteria](https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/manage-app-accessibility/reduced-motion-evaluation-criteria/) | 5 | 4 | Honor system reduced-motion settings, pause auto-updating content, and provide non-motion alternatives for essential state changes. |
| 586 | Text must scale and reflow without clipping, overlap, or horizontal scrolling. | WCAG and platform guidance treat zoom and large text as normal access paths. | [Apple - Larger Text evaluation criteria](https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/manage-app-accessibility/larger-text-evaluation-criteria/) | 5 | 5 | Test 200 percent zoom and platform large-text settings; avoid fixed-height text containers. |
| 587 | Pointer targets and gestures need forgiving alternatives. | WCAG target-size and gesture guidance addresses users who cannot perform precise taps, drag gestures, or multi-pointer actions. | [W3C WCAG 2.2 Quick Reference](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/quickref/) | 5 | 4 | Provide keyboard and single-pointer alternatives, adequate target size, and no drag-only critical workflows. |
| 588 | Accessibility belongs in acceptance criteria and test plans. | GOV.UK accessibility testing guidance says automated tools catch only part of the problem and must be paired with manual tests. | [GOV.UK Service Manual - Testing for accessibility](https://www.gov.uk/service-manual/helping-people-to-use-your-service/testing-for-accessibility) | 5 | 5 | Include accessibility AC in tickets, run automated checks, and manually test keyboard, screen reader, zoom, contrast, and error flows. |
| 589 | Put durable integrity rules in the database, not only in application code. | Framework validation docs warn uniqueness checks can race and require database constraints for correctness. | [Rails Active Record Validations API](https://api.rubyonrails.org/classes/ActiveRecord/Validations/ClassMethods.html) | 5 | 5 | Treat constraint violations as normal outcomes and map them to user or API errors. |
| 590 | Back business uniqueness with a unique constraint or unique index. | PostgreSQL unique-index guidance makes the index the concurrency-safe arbiter of identity. | [PostgreSQL - Unique Indexes](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/indexes-unique.html) | 5 | 5 | Encode tenant, case-folding, soft-delete, NULL, and partial-uniqueness semantics in the index definition. |
| 591 | Use foreign keys for real relationships. | Database foreign-key constraints prevent orphaned data and make delete/update semantics explicit. | [PostgreSQL - Constraints](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/ddl-constraints.html) | 5 | 5 | Choose restrict, cascade, set-null, and nullability deliberately; index child foreign-key columns when needed. |
| 592 | Match the constraint primitive to the invariant. | PostgreSQL documents that CHECK constraints are row-local and inappropriate for cross-row or cross-table invariants. | [PostgreSQL - Constraints](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/ddl-constraints.html) | 5 | 5 | Use unique, exclusion, foreign key, serializable transactions, or locks for invariants that span rows. |
| 593 | Choose transaction isolation by invariant, not by default. | Transaction-isolation docs and Jepsen analyses show weak isolation can permit lost updates, phantoms, stale decisions, and write skew. | [PostgreSQL - Transaction Isolation](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/transaction-iso.html) | 5 | 5 | Read-then-write business rules often need constraints, explicit locks, or serializable transactions. |
| 594 | Design for transaction retries. | PostgreSQL serialization and deadlock guidance treats some transaction failures as expected outcomes under concurrency. | [PostgreSQL - Serialization Failure Handling](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/mvcc-serialization-failure-handling.html) | 5 | 5 | Retry the whole transaction, including decision logic, with bounded backoff and idempotent external effects. |
| 595 | Keep one canonical state; make derived state rebuildable or transactionally maintained. | Data-systems guidance distinguishes canonical facts from indexes, caches, materialized views, and denormalized projections. | [Martin Kleppmann - Turning the database inside-out](https://martin.kleppmann.com/2015/03/04/turning-the-database-inside-out.html) | 5 | 4 | Mark derived fields explicitly, define owners and refresh rules, and reconcile projections from canonical facts. |
| 596 | Page with a unique, stable order. | PostgreSQL LIMIT/OFFSET guidance warns that query slices are unpredictable without deterministic ordering. | [PostgreSQL - LIMIT and OFFSET](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/queries-limit.html) | 5 | 4 | Sort by a business key plus unique tie-breaker and prefer cursor pagination for live or large result sets. |
| 597 | Treat indexes as correctness infrastructure, not only performance knobs. | PostgreSQL index docs show indexes enforce uniqueness, support constraints, and can fail or remain invalid during concurrent builds. | [PostgreSQL - CREATE INDEX](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createindex.html) | 5 | 4 | Verify index validity, clean invalid indexes, and pair lock-sensitive or foreign-key queries with supporting indexes. |
| 598 | Own schema changes through versioned migrations. | Migration tooling and framework docs treat schema changes as ordered, reviewable, repeatable artifacts. | [Flyway - Migrations](https://documentation.red-gate.com/fd/migrations-271585107.html) | 4 | 4 | Keep migrations in version control, run them once in order, test rollback or restore, and log progress. |
| 599 | Emit structured logs, not prose-only strings. | Structured logging guidance makes logs queryable, joinable, redaction-friendly, and alertable. | [AWS Well-Architected - Centralized and structured logging](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/serverless-applications-lens/opex-logging.html) | 5 | 5 | Use stable fields such as event, level, service, environment, request ID, trace ID, and safe actor ID. |
| 600 | Standardize telemetry identity and field names. | OpenTelemetry semantic conventions prevent every service from inventing incompatible telemetry schemas. | [OpenTelemetry Semantic Conventions](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/concepts/semantic-conventions/) | 5 | 5 | Require service name, service version, deployment environment, operation name, and shared attribute names. |
| 601 | Propagate trace and correlation context across every boundary. | W3C Trace Context standardizes request identity across services, queues, events, and outbound calls. | [W3C Trace Context](https://www.w3.org/TR/trace-context/) | 5 | 5 | Forward trace context through HTTP, RPC, queues, jobs, events, and scheduled work. |
| 602 | Correlate logs, traces, and errors by shared IDs. | OpenTelemetry log guidance links log records to traces and spans so responders can move from symptom to path. | [OpenTelemetry Logs](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/otel/logs/) | 5 | 5 | Inject trace ID and span ID into log records and verify they are searchable in production tooling. |
| 603 | Treat metric labels and log indexes as cardinality-constrained. | Grafana and metric-governance guidance warns that unbounded labels create cost explosions and unusable queries. | [Grafana Loki - Cardinality](https://grafana.com/docs/loki/latest/get-started/labels/cardinality/) | 5 | 5 | Do not use user IDs, order IDs, timestamps, raw URLs, or trace IDs as metric labels or index labels. |
| 604 | Page only on actionable, owned, user-impacting symptoms. | SRE monitoring guidance warns that noisy, non-actionable alerts train responders to ignore real signals. | [Google SRE - Monitoring Distributed Systems](https://sre.google/sre-book/monitoring-distributed-systems/) | 5 | 5 | Every paging alert needs owner, impact, severity, trigger, likely cause, action, and escalation path. |
| 605 | Make debug logging scoped, sampled, and temporary. | Runtime logging guidance warns that always-on verbose logs create performance, cost, and privacy risk. | [AWS Lambda Powertools - Logger](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/powertools/python/latest/core/logger/) | 4 | 4 | Support request-scoped debug, sampling, buffered logs flushed on error, and automatic expiry. |
| 606 | Test observability like product behavior. | OpenTelemetry testing guidance supports verifying emitted spans, attributes, logs, and metrics in tests. | [OpenTelemetry Erlang - Testing](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/languages/erlang/testing/) | 4 | 4 | Unit and contract tests should assert required spans, log fields, metrics, and alert rules for critical paths. |
| 607 | Feed incidents back into telemetry, alerts, and runbooks. | SRE postmortem guidance treats incidents as learning loops that should improve future diagnosis and response. | [Google SRE - Postmortem Culture](https://sre.google/sre-book/postmortem-culture/) | 5 | 4 | Each postmortem action should address a missing signal, bad alert, missing runbook step, or unsafe operational path. |
| 608 | Keep config keys orthogonal; avoid brittle environment bundles. | Twelve-Factor config guidance warns against grouped environment names that create hidden combinatorial drift. | [The Twelve-Factor App - Config](https://12factor.net/config) | 4 | 4 | Use independent typed keys and explicit deploy manifests instead of logic like `if environment is production`. |
| 609 | Keep development, staging, and production similar where behavior depends on it. | Twelve-Factor dev/prod parity guidance reduces bugs caused by different backing services, config shapes, and release paths. | [The Twelve-Factor App - Dev/prod parity](https://12factor.net/dev-prod-parity) | 4 | 5 | Match service types, config schema, flag SDK path, data shape, and rollout mechanics as closely as practical. |
| 610 | Validate config and flag payloads before deployment. | AWS AppConfig validators treat configuration as production input that needs schema and semantic checks. | [AWS AppConfig - Validators](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/appconfig/latest/userguide/appconfig-creating-configuration-and-profile-validators.html) | 5 | 5 | Enforce schema, type, range, enum, dependency, payload-size, and reserved-key checks before rollout. |
| 611 | Treat runtime config as untrusted input and keep last-known-good. | SRE configuration guidance treats configuration changes as production changes that can fail independently of code. | [Google SRE Workbook - Configuration Design](https://sre.google/workbook/configuration-design/) | 5 | 5 | Atomically load only validated config, reject bad updates without crashing, and retain the previous good version. |
| 612 | Version config and make rollback ordinary. | SRE and AppConfig guidance make config revisions and rollback part of safe operations. | [AWS AppConfig - Reverting a deployment](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/appconfig/latest/userguide/appconfig-deploying-reverting.html) | 5 | 5 | Attach immutable revision IDs, schema versions, changelogs, compatibility windows, and tested rollback commands. |
| 613 | Tie progressive delivery to health metrics and automatic rollback. | Guarded-rollout guidance gates rollout steps on live health signals rather than time alone. | [LaunchDarkly - Guarded rollouts](https://launchdarkly.com/docs/home/releases/guarded-rollouts) | 5 | 5 | Gate each step on error rate, latency, saturation, and domain metrics with bake time and rollback triggers. |
| 614 | Every flag evaluation needs a safe typed fallback. | Feature-flag evaluation docs require callers to define behavior when the flag service, key, context, or value fails. | [LaunchDarkly - Evaluating flags](https://launchdarkly.com/docs/sdk/features/evaluating) | 5 | 5 | Test unavailable provider, unknown key, wrong type, stale cache, and startup-not-ready paths. |
| 615 | Put risky features behind tested operator-grade kill switches. | Feature-toggle guidance describes operational toggles as a way to disable dangerous behavior without redeploying. | [Martin Fowler - Feature Toggles](https://martinfowler.com/articles/feature-toggles.html) | 5 | 5 | Wrap the full risky path, restrict write access, audit changes, document owner and runbook, and drill the switch. |
| 616 | Give every feature flag an owner, type, expiry, and cleanup path. | Feature-flag debt guidance warns that stale flags multiply code paths and can reactivate unsafe behavior. | [Unleash - Technical debt](https://docs.getunleash.io/concepts/technical-debt) | 5 | 5 | Require flag registry metadata, expiry alerts, cleanup PRs, and stale-flag limits. |
