| 617 | Centralize flag decision logic. | Fowler's feature-toggle guidance separates toggle decisions from domain behavior to keep branching understandable and testable. | [Martin Fowler - Feature Toggles](https://martinfowler.com/articles/feature-toggles.html) | 5 | 4 | Use typed decision objects and lint direct SDK calls outside the decision layer. |
| 618 | Expose current config and flag state to operators. | Incident writeups show response slows when teams cannot answer what config or flags are live. | [Slack Engineering - All Hands on Deck](https://slack.engineering/all-hands-on-deck/) | 5 | 4 | Provide redacted config revision, schema version, flag snapshot hash, and last refresh time in operator diagnostics. |
| 619 | Block unlicensed material before it enters the tree. | GitHub licensing guidance states that visible public code is not automatically licensed for reuse. | [GitHub Docs - Licensing a repository](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/licensing-a-repository) | 5 | 5 | Reject unlicensed snippets, assets, packages, and vendored code unless policy or counsel explicitly approves. |
| 620 | Express licenses with SPDX identifiers and expressions. | SPDX provides machine-readable license identifiers and expressions for package metadata, SBOMs, and audits. | [SPDX License List](https://spdx.org/licenses/) | 5 | 5 | Validate license identifiers in source headers, package metadata, and third-party manifests. |
| 621 | Preserve copyright, license, notices, and attribution. | REUSE and common license texts require retaining license texts, copyright notices, NOTICE files, and attribution in redistributions. | [REUSE Specification 3.3](https://reuse.software/spec-3.3/) | 5 | 5 | Maintain license files, third-party notices, and release artifact checks. |
| 622 | Track provenance at file and snippet level. | Compliance specs warn that package-level licensing can miss copied snippets, mixed-license files, examples, fonts, images, and generated code. | [REUSE Specification 3.3](https://reuse.software/spec-3.3/) | 5 | 5 | Record origin, version, hash, license, modification history, and generation tool for copied or generated material. |
| 623 | Check license compatibility before combining code. | Ecosystem package docs use SPDX expressions because AND, OR, and WITH have different legal meanings. | [Cargo Book - License and license-file](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/manifest.html#the-license-and-license-file-fields) | 5 | 5 | CI should fail unknown, incompatible, or policy-denied license expressions before merge. |
| 624 | Treat copyleft distribution obligations as release requirements. | GPL license text defines source and distribution obligations that must be satisfied when covered software is distributed. | [GNU GPLv3](https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-3.0.en.html) | 5 | 5 | Prepare source bundles, written offers, build scripts, and dependency source when obligations apply. |
| 625 | Vendored code remains third-party code. | OSPO guidance treats vendoring as increasing provenance and notice responsibilities, not removing them. | [TODO Group - Using open source code](https://todogroup.org/resources/guides/using-open-source-code/) | 4 | 5 | Keep vendored code isolated with upstream URL, version, hash, license, notices, and local patch history. |
| 626 | Put open-source policy gates before merge and release. | OpenChain license-compliance guidance treats review, approval, scanning, and exception handling as process requirements. | [OpenChain - ISO/IEC 5230 license compliance](https://openchainproject.org/license-compliance) | 5 | 5 | Gate PRs and releases on license allowlists, SCA, SBOM generation, and documented exceptions. |
| 627 | Only humans certify legal contribution rights. | Linux kernel AI-assistant policy treats signoff as a human legal assertion that tools cannot make. | [Linux kernel docs - AI Coding Assistants](https://docs.kernel.org/process/coding-assistants.html) | 5 | 5 | Require human DCO or CLA signoff; use assisted-by metadata only as supporting provenance. |
| 628 | Route patent, trademark, export, sanctions, and unusual license terms through policy. | Foundation and government guidance show copyright licenses do not settle all product compliance duties. | [Apache - Third Party License Policy](https://www.apache.org/legal/resolved.html) | 5 | 4 | Escalate unusual patent grants, trademarks, non-OSI terms, cryptography exports, and restricted distribution contexts. |
| 629 | Treat every accepted input as a formal language. | LangSec research frames parsers and protocols as languages whose grammars must be explicit and enforceable. | [LangSec](https://langsec.org/) | 5 | 5 | Use schemas, parser generators, parser combinators, or formal grammar docs instead of ad hoc recognizers. |
| 630 | Parse first, then validate semantics. | OWASP input-validation guidance distinguishes syntactic validity from semantic business constraints. | [OWASP Input Validation Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Input_Validation_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Validate type, range, length, enum, relationship, and authority after parsing has produced a known shape. |
| 631 | Reject ambiguous encodings instead of choosing an interpretation. | I-JSON and protocol guidance warn that duplicate keys and conflicting framing can split behavior across implementations. | [RFC 7493 - I-JSON](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7493) | 5 | 5 | Reject duplicate JSON keys after decoding escapes, conflicting length metadata, and ambiguous map encodings. |
| 632 | Canonicalize with a published algorithm. | JSON canonicalization and XML canonicalization standards prevent homegrown byte forms from breaking signatures, hashes, caches, and comparisons. | [RFC 8785 - JSON Canonicalization Scheme](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8785) | 5 | 5 | Document exactly which canonical form is used before signing, hashing, comparing, or deduplicating data. |
| 633 | Enforce a Unicode security policy for identifiers. | Unicode security mechanisms cover confusables, mixed scripts, and normalization issues that can bypass identity checks. | [Unicode UTS #39 - Unicode Security Mechanisms](https://www.unicode.org/reports/tr39/) | 5 | 5 | Choose normalization per field, restrict scripts where needed, and detect mixed-script confusables in identifiers. |
| 634 | Version formats and exercise extension points continuously. | IAB extensibility guidance warns unused version negotiation and extension points ossify and later fail. | [RFC 9170 - Long-Term Viability of Protocol Extension Mechanisms](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9170) | 5 | 5 | Include version or feature negotiation and test unknown extensions in CI. |
| 635 | Parser limits are security controls. | Parser and JSON library docs warn that malicious size, depth, tokens, entity expansion, and CPU-heavy input can cause denial of service. | [Python json documentation](https://docs.python.org/3/library/json.html) | 4 | 5 | Set byte, nesting, token, entity-expansion, regex-time, allocation, and parse-time budgets. |
| 636 | Parse data, not code. | YAML and XML parser guidance shows unsafe loader features can instantiate objects, fetch resources, or expand active content. | [PyYAML Documentation](https://pyyaml.org/wiki/PyYAMLDocumentation) | 4 | 5 | Use safe loaders, constructor allowlists, disabled DTDs, disabled external entities, and tag restrictions. |
| 637 | Frame streams explicitly. | Streaming parser docs distinguish incomplete input from invalid input and require protocols to define message boundaries. | [nom parser combinator documentation](https://docs.rs/nom/latest/nom/) | 4 | 4 | Use length prefixes, record separators, token streams, or envelope frames and return need-more separately from invalid. |
| 638 | Round-trip and conformance tests are part of a format. | Parser and protocol conformance docs treat parse trees, serialization, and cross-version fixtures as compatibility contracts. | [Tree-sitter - Writing tests](https://tree-sitter.github.io/tree-sitter/creating-parsers/5-writing-tests.html) | 4 | 4 | Test parse, AST, serialize, parse again, canonical bytes, and old/new-version fixtures. |
| 639 | Fuzz parsers with valid, invalid, and grammar-aware inputs. | Browser fuzzing practice shows parsers fail at edge cases humans do not imagine. | [Chrome Developers - Chromium Chronicle 9: ClusterFuzz](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/chromium-chronicle-9/) | 5 | 5 | Fuzz boundary APIs with sanitizers, seed corpora, grammar generators, and minimized repros. |
| 640 | Prefer machine-readable contracts over prose contracts. | OpenAPI and schema standards can validate, generate, diff, document, and test contracts that prose cannot enforce. | [OpenAPI Specification 3.2.0](https://spec.openapis.org/oas/v3.2.0.html) | 5 | 4 | Keep OpenAPI, JSON Schema, Protobuf, or equivalent contracts as source of truth and validate requests and responses. |
| 641 | Do not use complex regular expressions as parsers. | Cloudflare's regex outage shows nested or backtracking-heavy regex can become production-wide CPU exhaustion. | [Cloudflare - Details of the Cloudflare outage on July 2, 2019](https://blog.cloudflare.com/details-of-the-cloudflare-outage-on-july-2-2019/) | 4 | 4 | Use real parsers or linear-time regex engines for untrusted input; anchor validators and benchmark worst cases. |
| 642 | Treat binary floating point as approximation, not decimal truth. | Decimal arithmetic docs show human decimal values such as money and measurements often require decimal or fixed-point representation. | [Python decimal documentation](https://docs.python.org/3/library/decimal.html) | 5 | 5 | Use Decimal, BigDecimal, scaled integers, or domain decimal types for exact decimal quantities. |
| 643 | Make rounding policy explicit. | BigDecimal and decimal arithmetic guidance require callers to choose precision and rounding behavior deliberately. | [Java BigDecimal](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/math/BigDecimal.html) | 5 | 5 | Pick rounding mode and precision at domain boundaries, especially finance, accounting, reporting, and allocation. |
| 644 | Compare floating-point values with domain tolerances. | NumPy and language math docs warn that approximate floating values should not be compared with bare equality in most numeric domains. | [NumPy isclose](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/reference/generated/numpy.isclose.html) | 5 | 5 | Choose relative and absolute tolerance by domain scale; do not blindly reuse defaults. |
| 645 | Handle NaN, infinity, and signed zero deliberately. | Python math and IEEE-style floating-point APIs expose special values that break ordinary ordering and equality assumptions. | [Python math documentation](https://docs.python.org/3/library/math.html) | 5 | 5 | Validate with finite checks and define serialization, sorting, equality, and rejection behavior. |
| 646 | Make numerical exceptions visible. | NumPy floating-point error handling lets overflow, divide-by-zero, underflow, and invalid operations be raised, warned, logged, or ignored. | [NumPy floating point error handling](https://numpy.org/devdocs/reference/routines.err.html) | 5 | 5 | In tests and critical jobs, fail or log on numerical exceptions rather than silently propagating bad results. |
| 647 | Never narrow or cast range-sensitive values without checks. | The Ariane 501 failure report shows unprotected conversion and unrepresentative assumptions can destroy otherwise working systems. | [ESA - Ariane 501 Inquiry Board report](https://www.esa.int/Newsroom/Press_Releases/Ariane_501_-_Presentation_of_Inquiry_Board_report) | 5 | 5 | Add range guards, wider accumulators, checked conversions, and representative boundary tests. |
| 648 | Prefer numerically stable algorithms over algebraically equivalent formulas. | NumPy summation guidance and classic floating-point research show operation order changes accumulated error. | [NumPy sum](https://numpy.org/doc/2.3/reference/generated/numpy.sum.html) | 5 | 5 | Use stable library routines, pairwise or compensated summation, wider accumulators, and tested numeric tolerances. |
| 649 | Own random-number-generator state explicitly. | NumPy random-generator guidance separates generator objects from global RNG state to improve reproducibility and composition. | [NumPy random Generator](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/reference/random/generator.html) | 5 | 5 | Pass RNG objects through APIs; seed at construction; avoid hidden global randomness in libraries and tests. |
| 650 | Record enough stochastic provenance to rerun the workflow. | Reproducible-computational-research guidance says seeds alone are insufficient without code, data, parameters, and environment. | [PLOS - Ten Simple Rules for Reproducible Computational Research](https://journals.plos.org/ploscompbiol/article?id=10.1371/journal.pcbi.1003285) | 5 | 5 | Log seed, PRNG algorithm and version, code revision, input data, parameters, environment, and hardware-relevant settings. |
| 651 | Split parallel random streams deliberately. | NumPy parallel RNG guidance provides stream spawning to avoid correlation, shared-state races, and adjacent-seed mistakes. | [NumPy parallel random number generation](https://numpy.org/doc/2.2/reference/random/parallel.html) | 5 | 4 | Use seed sequences, jumpable streams, or counter/keyed generators for parallel jobs. |
| 652 | Test stochastic systems for reproducibility and robustness separately. | ML deployment testing guidance distinguishes deterministic reproduction from confidence across data slices, seeds, and time. | [Google ML Crash Course - Deployment testing](https://developers.google.com/machine-learning/crash-course/production-ml-systems/deployment-testing) | 4 | 4 | Fix seeds for reproduction, but assess behavior across multiple seeds or runs for stability. |
| 653 | Treat exactly-once claims as boundary-scoped. | SQS and Kafka delivery semantics provide dedupe or transactions inside specific boundaries, not universal prevention of repeated external side effects. | [Amazon SQS FIFO exactly-once processing](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSSimpleQueueService/latest/SQSDeveloperGuide/FIFO-queues-exactly-once-processing.html) | 5 | 5 | Assume replay outside the broker boundary and make consumers and side effects idempotent. |
| 654 | Acknowledge messages only after durable side effects. | RabbitMQ, Kafka, and SQS delivery docs show crashes between processing and acknowledgment cause replay. | [RabbitMQ Consumer Acknowledgements and Publisher Confirms](https://www.rabbitmq.com/docs/confirms) | 5 | 5 | Commit business change and idempotency marker before ack, delete, or offset commit. |
| 655 | Leases need fencing tokens for correctness. | Distributed-locking guidance warns a paused client can resume after lease expiry and corrupt a protected resource. | [Martin Kleppmann - How to do distributed locking](https://martin.kleppmann.com/2016/02/08/how-to-do-distributed-locking.html) | 5 | 5 | Issue monotonic fencing tokens and require the protected resource to reject stale tokens. |
| 656 | Do not use wall-clock time for authority. | Distributed-system clock patterns separate monotonic durations and logical ordering from wall-clock timestamps. | [Martin Fowler - Generation Clock](https://martinfowler.com/articles/patterns-of-distributed-systems/generation-clock.html) | 4 | 5 | Use monotonic clocks for elapsed time and epochs, terms, log offsets, or versions for authority and ordering. |
| 657 | Use consensus for critical shared state. | Google SRE consensus guidance treats leader election, membership, and critical metadata as needing a single agreed history. | [Google SRE - Managing Critical State](https://sre.google/sre-book/managing-critical-state/) | 5 | 5 | Prefer Raft, ZooKeeper, etcd, or managed coordination services over homegrown coordination. |
| 658 | Split-brain systems must fail closed. | Jepsen analyses and production postmortems show dual primaries create divergent truth that is hard to reconcile safely. | [Jepsen - Hazelcast 3.8.3](https://jepsen.io/analyses/hazelcast-3-8-3) | 5 | 5 | Minority partitions should reject writes, stale leaders should step down, and recovery should reconcile explicitly. |
| 659 | Treat distributed locks as efficiency hints unless fenced or consensus-backed. | Redis and distributed-locking discussions distinguish duplicate-work avoidance from correctness-critical exclusion. | [Redis - Distributed Locks with Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/clients/patterns/distributed-locks/) | 4 | 5 | Use simple locks for duplicate work; use fencing or consensus for money, inventory, uniqueness, and safety. |
| 660 | Poison messages need quarantine, not infinite retry. | RabbitMQ and SQS dead-letter guidance provide dead-letter routes for messages that cannot be processed successfully. | [RabbitMQ Dead Letter Exchanges](https://www.rabbitmq.com/docs/dlx) | 5 | 4 | Set max attempts, record failure reason, alert owners, and support controlled replay after fixes. |
| 661 | Cache entries are derived state and may lie. | AWS caching guidance and Redis eviction docs treat stale, evicted, missing, and stampeding cache entries as normal operating conditions. | [AWS Builders Library - Caching challenges and strategies](https://aws.amazon.com/builders-library/caching-challenges-and-strategies/) | 5 | 4 | Give every cache a freshness, invalidation, fallback, stampede, and privacy policy. |
| 662 | Read-modify-write must be conditional, serialized, or commutative. | Distributed-system versioning patterns and Jepsen analyses show weak consistency can lose updates. | [Martin Fowler - Versioned Value](https://martinfowler.com/articles/patterns-of-distributed-systems/versioned-value.html) | 4 | 5 | Use CAS, ETags, version columns, unique constraints, serializable transactions, or commutative operations. |
| 663 | Every durable doc needs an owner and canonical home. | Software Engineering at Google treats documentation as an engineering artifact that needs ownership and maintenance. | [Software Engineering at Google - Documentation](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch10.html) | 5 | 5 | Add owner and source-of-truth metadata; deprecate or delete duplicates. |
| 664 | Update docs in the same change as behavior. | Google documentation best practices make docs maintenance part of the development workflow, not a later cleanup task. | [Google Developer Documentation Style Guide - Best practices](https://google.github.io/styleguide/docguide/best_practices.html) | 5 | 5 | Add README, docstring, API reference, OpenAPI, runbook, and ADR checks to PR templates where relevant. |
| 665 | Separate tutorials, how-to guides, reference, and explanation. | Diataxis and Divio document different reader needs that fail when mixed into one page. | [Diataxis](https://diataxis.fr/) | 5 | 5 | Label docs by mode; keep reference factual, how-tos goal-oriented, tutorials learning-oriented, and explanations conceptual. |
| 666 | README is the onboarding front door. | GitHub README guidance frames repository READMEs as the first orientation point for users and contributors. | [GitHub Docs - About READMEs](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-readmes) | 5 | 5 | Include what, why, setup, run, test, help, maintainers, and links to deeper docs. |
| 667 | Examples must be real, task-oriented, and testable. | Microsoft, Rust, and Go documentation guidance emphasize examples that developers can copy, run, and verify. | [Microsoft Style Guide - Code examples](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/style-guide/developer-content/code-examples) | 5 | 5 | Include prerequisites, dependencies, expected output, tricky cases, and doctest or CI coverage. |
| 668 | Record architectural decisions as decision records. | ADR guidance preserves context, decision, status, and consequences so maintainers do not rely on PR archaeology. | [Cognitect - Documenting Architecture Decisions](https://cognitect.com/blog/2011/11/15/documenting-architecture-decisions) | 5 | 5 | Store ADRs with status, context, decision, consequences, and supersession links. |
| 669 | Enable strict type and nullability modes. | TypeScript, Kotlin, and Swift docs show null and unchecked optional behavior weaken type guarantees. | [TypeScript strict](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/strict.html) | 5 | 5 | Turn on strict mode and add stricter optional, indexed-access, and nullability checks where available. |
| 670 | Parse into stronger types instead of only validating raw values. | Parse-don't-validate guidance carries proof of validation into the type system instead of leaving raw values and comments behind. | [Parse, don't validate](https://lexi-lambda.github.io/blog/2019/11/05/parse-don-t-validate/) | 4 | 5 | Boundary code should return EmailAddress, NonEmptyList, Config, or other validated domain types. |
| 671 | Represent recoverable failure as typed results. | Rust Result and Kotlin Result expose expected failure to callers instead of hiding it in side effects or uncontrolled exceptions. | [Rust Book - Recoverable Errors with Result](https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/ch09-02-recoverable-errors-with-result.html) | 5 | 5 | Use Result, Either, or sealed error types for expected domain failure; reserve panics for bugs. |
| 672 | Use sum types for mutually exclusive states. | Domain-modeling guidance shows boolean and nullable field combinations create invalid states that the domain never intended. | [F# for Fun and Profit - Making illegal states unrepresentable](https://fsharpforfunandprofit.com/posts/designing-with-types-making-illegal-states-unrepresentable/) | 4 | 5 | Replace flag bundles such as loading plus optional data plus optional error with explicit variants. |
| 673 | Require exhaustive handling of variants. | TypeScript and Kotlin exhaustiveness guidance lets new variants break incomplete consumers at compile time. | [TypeScript Handbook - Union exhaustiveness checking](https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/unions-and-intersections.html) | 5 | 5 | Use assert-never checks, sealed classes, Rust match, or compiler exhaustiveness enforcement. |
| 674 | Wrap IDs and domain tokens in opaque types. | Newtype and opaque-type guidance prevents unrelated strings, numbers, IDs, and tokens from being mixed silently. | [Rust Book - Advanced Types](https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/ch20-03-advanced-types.html) | 5 | 4 | Define UserId, OrderId, SanitizedHtml, and similar domain types with explicit constructors. |
| 675 | Use immutable value objects for domain concepts. | Value-object guidance keeps equality, validity, and behavior with the concept instead of scattering primitive checks. | [Martin Fowler - Value Object](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/ValueObject.html) | 4 | 4 | Model Money, DateRange, EmailAddress, and similar concepts with validated constructors and no mutating setters. |
| 676 | Put explicit contracts where types cannot express the rule. | Design by Contract covers preconditions, postconditions, and invariants for ranges, temporal constraints, and side effects. | [Eiffel - Design by Contract](https://www.eiffel.com/values/design-by-contract/introduction/) | 5 | 4 | Assert public API preconditions, mutation postconditions, and class invariants where static types are insufficient. |
| 677 | Keep operational escape hatches explicit and audited. | Invalid-state modeling critiques warn that over-rigid models can hide rare real-world exceptions or block repair workflows. | [Sean Goedecke - Make invalid states unrepresentable considered harmful](https://www.seangoedecke.com/invalid-states/) | 3 | 3 | Add quarantine, repair, override, or migration states with audit trails instead of silently weakening the core model. |
| 678 | Do not mix behavior change with drive-by churn. | Code-review guidance warns unrelated formatting, renames, or opportunistic refactors obscure intent and complicate rollback. | [Google Engineering Practices - What to look for in a code review](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html) | 5 | 5 | Split formatting, migrations, renames, and behavior into separate changes unless inseparable. |
| 679 | Preserve author context in change descriptions. | Google and Microsoft review guidance treat the change description as the reviewer's map of problem, approach, tests, and risk. | [Google Engineering Practices - CL descriptions](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/developer/cl-descriptions.html) | 5 | 5 | Include problem, approach, tradeoffs, tests, risks, linked task, screenshots for UI, and rollout notes. |
| 680 | Review for shared understanding, not only defect detection. | Microsoft and Google code-review research found knowledge transfer and system understanding are core review outcomes. | [Microsoft Research - Expectations, Outcomes, and Challenges of Modern Code Review](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2016/02/ICSE202013-codereview.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Ask whether the change and system impact are understandable before line-level polish. |
| 681 | Preserve author agency during review. | Google review-comment guidance recommends identifying problems and rationale without taking unnecessary control of the solution. | [Google Engineering Practices - How to write code review comments](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/comments.html) | 5 | 4 | State the issue and why; suggest fixes when useful; let the author choose among valid solutions. |
| 682 | Label review feedback by intent. | Explicit review-comment conventions reduce confusion about blockers, questions, suggestions, nits, and praise. | [Conventional Comments](https://conventionalcomments.org/) | 3 | 4 | Mark feedback as issue, suggestion, nit, question, praise, or blocker and say whether it blocks merge. |
| 683 | Use engineering metrics for learning, not individual scoring. | DORA and SPACE warn that single metrics invite gaming and miss collaboration, well-being, context, and stability. | [ACM Queue - The SPACE of Developer Productivity](https://queue.acm.org/detail.cfm?id=3454124) | 5 | 5 | Track team-level speed, stability, satisfaction, collaboration, and flow together; avoid personal leaderboards. |
| 684 | Declare infrastructure as desired state. | Terraform IaC guidance treats declarative configuration as reviewable, repeatable, and automatable infrastructure management. | [Terraform - Infrastructure as Code](https://developer.hashicorp.com/terraform/tutorials/aws-get-started/infrastructure-as-code) | 5 | 5 | Require PRs, plans, and reviews for infrastructure instead of relying on console clicks. |
| 685 | Use one management path per resource. | Kubernetes object-management guidance warns that mixing imperative and declarative control creates ownership ambiguity. | [Kubernetes - Object Management](https://github.com/kubernetes/website/blob/main/content/en/docs/concepts/overview/working-with-objects/object-management.md) | 5 | 5 | Mark owner and tool per resource; reserve console or manual edits for audited break-glass only. |
| 686 | Lock infrastructure state and serialize applies. | Terraform and OpenTofu state-locking docs protect shared state from concurrent writers. | [Terraform - State Locking](https://developer.hashicorp.com/terraform/language/state/locking) | 5 | 5 | Use remote state with locking, ban lock bypasses, and run one apply pipeline per state. |
| 687 | Detect infrastructure drift and reconcile deliberately. | Terraform and Pulumi drift docs show actual resources can diverge from desired state and create surprise changes or security gaps. | [Terraform - Manage resource drift](https://developer.hashicorp.com/terraform/tutorials/state/resource-drift) | 5 | 5 | Schedule drift checks and either codify the real state or restore the declared state through review. |
| 688 | Prefer immutable infrastructure over in-place mutation. | AWS Well-Architected guidance treats immutable replacement as a way to reduce drift and improve reproducibility. | [AWS Well-Architected REL08-BP04](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/framework/rel_tracking_change_management_immutable_infrastructure.html) | 5 | 5 | Rebuild images, nodes, or resources through blue-green, canary, or replacement workflows instead of manual patching. |
| 689 | Gate infrastructure with policy as code before apply. | OPA and Conftest policy tooling can evaluate plans and manifests before unsafe resources exist. | [Open Policy Agent - Terraform](https://www.openpolicyagent.org/docs/terraform) | 5 | 5 | Block public exposure, missing tags, weak IAM, unencrypted storage, and budget violations from plan JSON or manifests. |
| 690 | Treat infrastructure state, plans, and outputs as sensitive. | Terraform and Pulumi docs warn secrets can appear in state and plans even when hidden from normal CLI output. | [Terraform - Manage sensitive data](https://developer.hashicorp.com/terraform/language/manage-sensitive-data) | 5 | 5 | Encrypt remote state, restrict access, use secret managers, and rotate credentials after exposure. |
| 691 | Review the exact infrastructure plan that will be applied. | Terraform apply docs support saved plans so approval applies to the same actions later executed. | [Terraform apply](https://developer.hashicorp.com/terraform/cli/commands/apply) | 5 | 4 | Store plan artifacts, require approval, and apply saved plans rather than re-planning after review. |
| 692 | Treat browser origin as a security boundary. | OWASP ASVS frontend guidance treats same-origin isolation as foundational browser security. | [OWASP ASVS 5.0 - Web Frontend Security](https://github.com/OWASP/ASVS/blob/master/5.0/en/0x12-V3-Web-Frontend-Security.md) | 5 | 5 | Put separate apps, tenants, admin panels, and user content on separate hostnames; avoid collapsing boundaries with shared origins. |
| 693 | CORS is not authentication. | MDN CORS guidance describes browser read-permission control, not identity or authorization. | [MDN - Cross-Origin Resource Sharing](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/CORS) | 5 | 5 | Use exact origin allowlists; never reflect arbitrary origins or use wildcard credentials. |
| 694 | Protect every sensitive browser state change against CSRF. | OWASP CSRF guidance warns cookies are sent automatically and forged state-changing requests can still reach servers. | [OWASP CSRF Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross-Site_Request_Forgery_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Use CSRF tokens, Origin or Referer checks, non-safelisted headers, and SameSite cookies as layered defenses. |
| 695 | Use strict CSP to reduce script injection blast radius. | Strict CSP guidance favors nonces or hashes over fragile host allowlists. | [web.dev - Strict CSP](https://web.dev/articles/strict-csp) | 5 | 5 | Start with nonce or hash based script policy, object-src none, base-uri none, and reporting before enforcement. |
| 696 | Keep long-lived tokens out of browser storage. | OWASP HTML5 guidance warns localStorage, sessionStorage, and IndexedDB are accessible to same-origin XSS. | [OWASP HTML5 Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/HTML5_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Prefer hardened server sessions or BFF patterns; otherwise use short-lived tokens, rotation, and in-memory storage. |
| 697 | Treat third-party scripts as privileged code. | OWASP third-party JavaScript guidance warns vendor scripts execute with your origin's privileges. | [OWASP Third Party JavaScript Management Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Third_Party_Javascript_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Minimize vendors, use SRI for static assets, sandbox widgets, and block third-party scripts on sensitive pages. |
| 698 | Isolate untrusted content from application origins. | OWASP ASVS origin-separation guidance treats uploaded HTML, previews, embeds, and rich content as same-origin execution risks. | [OWASP ASVS - Browser Origin Separation](https://cornucopia.owasp.org/taxonomy/asvs-5.0/03-web-frontend-security/05-browser-origin-separation) | 5 | 5 | Serve untrusted content from cookieless separate origins or sandboxed iframes with minimal permissions. |
| 699 | Lock down browser permissions. | Web permission guidance treats camera, mic, location, clipboard, and notifications as high-impact privacy surfaces. | [web.dev - Web permissions best practices](https://web.dev/articles/permissions-best-practices) | 5 | 4 | Ask at point of need, provide fallback UI, and deny unused features with Permissions-Policy. |
| 700 | Validate postMessage both ways. | MDN postMessage docs require exact target origins and origin checks because the API intentionally crosses origins. | [MDN - Window.postMessage](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/postMessage) | 5 | 4 | Set exact targetOrigin, verify event origin and source, and schema-validate messages before action. |
| 701 | One mutable UI fact gets one owner. | React state-sharing guidance keeps shared state in one owner to prevent contradictory UI. | [React - Sharing State Between Components](https://react.dev/learn/sharing-state-between-components) | 5 | 5 | Lift or centralize shared state; do not let separate components mutate independent copies of the same fact. |
| 702 | Store UI facts and derive UI views. | Redux style guidance recommends keeping state minimal and deriving computed values to avoid desynchronization. | [Redux Style Guide](https://redux.js.org/style-guide/) | 5 | 5 | Use selectors, computed values, or derived stores instead of storing duplicated derived values. |
| 703 | Normalize shared client entities. | Redux state-shape guidance reduces inconsistent nested copies and expensive updates. | [Redux - Normalizing State Shape](https://redux.js.org/usage/structuring-reducers/normalizing-state-shape) | 5 | 5 | Store entities by ID and keep ordering or grouping as separate ID arrays. |
| 704 | Treat server data as cached server state, not local truth. | TanStack Query defaults treat remote data as stale, refetchable, invalidatable, and shared by other clients. | [TanStack Query - Important Defaults](https://tanstack.com/query/v5/docs/framework/react/guides/important-defaults) | 5 | 5 | Set stale time, invalidation, refetch, and cache persistence policies intentionally. |
| 705 | Every UI mutation needs a cache plan. | Apollo mutation guidance requires updating or invalidating local cache so the UI does not lie after writes. | [Apollo Client - Mutations](https://www.apollographql.com/docs/react/data/mutations) | 5 | 5 | Return changed fields, update cache directly, invalidate affected queries, or refetch exact dependencies. |
| 706 | Optimistic UI must include rollback and reconciliation. | Apollo optimistic-update guidance treats optimistic state as temporary until server confirmation. | [Apollo Client - Optimistic mutation results](https://www.apollographql.com/docs/react/performance/optimistic-ui) | 5 | 5 | Snapshot before write, render optimistic state, rollback on error, and reconcile with the server result. |
| 707 | Cancel or ignore stale async UI work. | React effect guidance uses cleanup to prevent out-of-order async responses from mutating stale UI. | [React - useEffect](https://react.dev/reference/react/useEffect) | 5 | 5 | Use cleanup, request IDs, sequence numbers, or AbortController for async UI effects. |
| 708 | Model async UI as explicit states. | TanStack Query network-mode guidance distinguishes loading, paused, offline, error, retry, and success states. | [TanStack Query - Network Mode](https://tanstack.com/query/v4/docs/framework/react/guides/network-mode) | 5 | 5 | Represent offline, paused, empty, error, loading, success, and retrying as distinct UI states. |
| 709 | Client storage is a cache, not authority. | MDN Web Storage docs note browser storage is synchronous, limited, and subject to quota and eviction behavior. | [MDN - Web Storage API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Web_Storage_API) | 5 | 5 | Store small versioned data, handle quota and eviction, and use IndexedDB or server truth where appropriate. |
| 710 | Add API pagination before collections grow. | Google AIP pagination guidance treats pagination as part of the collection contract. | [Google AIP-158 - Pagination](https://google.aip.dev/158) | 5 | 5 | Use opaque cursors or stable IDs and document page size, next token, ordering, and filter stability. |
| 711 | Make rate limits observable and recoverable. | Stripe rate-limit docs document limiter reasons and client handling of 429 responses. | [Stripe - Rate limits](https://docs.stripe.com/rate-limits) | 5 | 5 | Return 429 with reason, retry guidance, headers where possible, and backoff expectations. |
| 712 | Treat webhooks as a first-class API. | Stripe webhook docs cover signatures, retries, duplicate events, ordering gaps, and local testing. | [Stripe Webhooks](https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks) | 5 | 5 | Verify signatures, acknowledge quickly, dedupe event IDs, document retries, and provide testing tools. |
| 713 | Provide safe sandbox environments with realistic fixtures. | Stripe testing docs let developers simulate success, failure, cards, accounts, and webhooks without production risk. | [Stripe - Testing](https://docs.stripe.com/testing) | 5 | 4 | Include test keys, fake accounts, error fixtures, webhook simulation, and documented sandbox-production differences. |
| 714 | SDKs need product policy, not only generated methods. | OpenAPI Generator can produce clients, but retry, pagination, idempotency, typed errors, and version behavior still need product decisions. | [OpenAPI Generator - Usage](https://openapi-generator.tech/docs/usage/) | 4 | 4 | Generate from the spec, then add tested policy layers for retries, auto-pagination, errors, and idempotency. |
| 715 | Write API docs for integration tasks, not just endpoints. | Google AIP documentation guidance expects APIs to document behavior, examples, errors, and operational expectations. | [Google AIP-192 - Documentation](https://google.aip.dev/192) | 5 | 5 | Document side effects, idempotency, units, ranges, defaults, errors, examples, and failure modes for every operation. |
| 716 | Standardize API resource naming and casing. | Google resource-name guidance lowers client cognitive load and improves generated SDK naming. | [Google AIP-122 - Resource names](https://google.aip.dev/122) | 5 | 4 | Standardize collection plurals, stable IDs or names, casing, path depth, and action naming. |
| 717 | Deprecate APIs with machine and human signals. | Zalando deprecation guidance recommends explicit metadata, headers, dates, and migration paths. | [Zalando RESTful API Guidelines - Deprecation](https://github.com/zalando/restful-api-guidelines/blob/main/chapters/deprecation.adoc) | 5 | 5 | Use deprecated markers, Deprecation and Sunset headers, migration docs, dates, telemetry, and customer notice. |
| 718 | Return request IDs everywhere. | Heroku API design guidance uses request IDs to correlate client failures with server logs and support workflows. | [Heroku HTTP API Design Guide](https://github.com/brandur/heroku-http-api-design) | 4 | 4 | Emit request or trace IDs in every response and require them in support and incident workflows. |
| 719 | Treat human error as a system-design signal. | Blameless postmortem guidance treats operator error as evidence of missing guardrails or confusing systems. | [Google SRE - Postmortem Culture](https://sre.google/sre-book/postmortem-culture/) | 5 | 5 | Replace blame with better constraints, safer defaults, clearer tools, drills, and reviewable workflows. |
| 720 | Make dangerous operations hard to run wrong. | Atlassian and GitLab incidents show accidental destructive operations can outpace recovery. | [Atlassian April 2022 outage PIR](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/atlassian-engineering/post-incident-review-april-2022-outage) | 5 | 5 | Add dry-runs, scoped credentials, typed IDs, allowlists, confirmation diffs, soft delete, and staged rollout. |
| 721 | Limit blast radius by architecture. | AWS fault-isolation guidance designs failures to affect cells, shards, tenants, or regions rather than whole fleets. | [AWS Well-Architected - Fault isolation](https://wa.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/2020-07-02T19-33-23/wat.question.REL_10.en.html) | 5 | 5 | Use cells, bulkheads, tenant quotas, shuffle sharding, and independent deploy pipelines. |
| 722 | Map and reduce hidden single points of failure. | Incident reports show shared databases, DNS, auth, deploy, support, and observability systems can amplify failures. | [GitLab database outage postmortem](https://about.gitlab.com/blog/postmortem-of-database-outage-of-january-31/) | 5 | 5 | Maintain dependency maps for critical paths, including recovery and operator tooling. |
| 723 | Postmortem actions need owners, due dates, and verification. | Google research on postmortem action items warns untracked or weak actions fail to prevent recurrence. | [Google Research - Postmortem Action Items](https://research.google/pubs/postmortem-action-items-plan-the-work-and-work-the-plan/) | 5 | 5 | Each action item needs owner, due date, fix class, verification signal, and closure review. |
| 724 | Make near-miss reporting normal. | NASA's aviation safety reporting model uses confidential reports to surface weak signals before major accidents. | [NASA Aviation Safety Reporting System](https://asrs.arc.nasa.gov/) | 5 | 4 | Capture scary deploys, confusing tools, recovery surprises, and near misses without punishment. |
| 725 | Every dataset needs a retention class and TTL. | ICO storage-limitation guidance warns against indefinite retention beyond purpose. | [ICO - Storage limitation](https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/data-protection-principles/a-guide-to-the-data-protection-principles/storage-limitation/) | 5 | 5 | Require purpose, class, owner, retention days, legal basis, and review date in schema or catalog. |
| 726 | Inventory data before governing it. | NIST privacy guidance requires identifying data, processors, systems, and flows before controls can work. | [NIST Privacy Framework Core](https://www.nist.gov/document/nist-privacy-framework-version-1-core-pdf) | 5 | 5 | Catalog tables, topics, buckets, regions, PII flags, processors, recipients, and downstream sinks. |
| 727 | Suppress future collection after deletion or objection. | Segment deletion guidance shows deleted users can be re-created by later ingestion unless suppression is enforced. | [Twilio Segment - User Deletion and Suppression](https://www.twilio.com/docs/segment/privacy/user-deletion-and-suppression) | 4 | 5 | Enforce suppression at ingestion and document downstream or device-mode gaps. |
| 728 | Backups need retention and deletion semantics. | Google Cloud deletion guidance covers how backup and replicated copies expire after deletion. | [Google Cloud - Data deletion](https://docs.cloud.google.com/docs/security/deletion) | 5 | 5 | Track backup expiry and replay deletion ledgers before exposing restored backups. |
| 729 | Legal holds override normal deletion until released. | AWS legal-hold guidance preserves recovery points beyond retention when legal obligations apply. | [AWS Backup - Legal holds](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/aws-backup/latest/devguide/legalhold.html) | 5 | 5 | Deletion executors must check matter, custodian, scope, approver, and release state. |
| 730 | WORM and archive locks must be version-aware. | S3 Object Lock applies retention and legal holds to object versions, with explicit governance or compliance modes. | [Amazon S3 Object Lock](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/object-lock.html) | 5 | 5 | Store retain-until, mode, legal-hold status, version ID, and release authority. |
| 731 | Audit trails must be tamper-evident. | CloudTrail integrity validation uses digest files to detect log modification or deletion. | [AWS CloudTrail - Log file integrity validation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/awscloudtrail/latest/userguide/cloudtrail-log-file-validation-intro.html) | 5 | 5 | Hash or sign logs, separate digest permissions, use WORM storage, and synchronize timestamps. |
| 732 | Archives need defined retrieval and deletion behavior. | MongoDB Atlas archive docs treat archived data as still queryable and manageable, not a forgotten dump. | [MongoDB Atlas - Manage Online Archives](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/atlas/online-archive/manage-online-archive/) | 4 | 4 | Define query path, access roles, retrieval SLA, purge behavior, and tests. |
| 733 | Age out high-volume data by partition or lifecycle policy. | BigQuery partition-management guidance supports table and partition expiration for scalable data cleanup. | [BigQuery - Managing partitioned tables](https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/managing-partitioned-tables) | 5 | 4 | Partition by event time, expire or drop partitions, and account for time travel or fail-safe windows. |
| 734 | Govern analytics events at ingestion. | Segment Protocols uses tracking plans and schema controls to prevent unknown or excessive event data. | [Twilio Segment - Protocols Overview](https://www.twilio.com/docs/segment/protocols) | 4 | 5 | Maintain tracking plans, reject or quarantine unknown events, minimize properties, and set analytics TTLs. |
| 735 | Data lineage must follow archives and deletions. | AWS Glue cataloging supports discovery of data sources so downstream copies can be governed. | [AWS Glue - Data discovery and cataloging](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glue/latest/dg/catalog-and-crawler.html) | 5 | 4 | Emit source-to-target lineage and make deletion jobs traverse derived copies. |
| 736 | Avoid runtime allocation in critical paths. | NASA/JPL Power of Ten limits dynamic allocation because latency, fragmentation, leaks, and failure modes are hard to bound. | [NASA/JPL Power of Ten](https://spinroot.com/gerard/pdf/P10.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Use static allocation, pools, arenas, or startup-only allocation on latency- or safety-critical paths. |
| 737 | Keep timing deterministic where deadlines matter. | Mars Pathfinder's priority inversion incident shows scheduler and blocking behavior can break real-time expectations. | [Mars Pathfinder priority inversion account](https://www.cs.unc.edu/~anderson/teach/comp790/papers/mars_pathfinder_short_version.html) | 4 | 5 | Bound blocking, measure worst-case latency, and avoid hidden GC, scheduler, I/O, or lock stalls in deadline paths. |
| 738 | Watchdogs must prove health, not just liveness. | Zephyr watchdog docs show watchdogs supervise system progress, but blind kicks only prove the kicker still runs. | [Zephyr Watchdog documentation](https://docs.zephyrproject.org/latest/hardware/peripherals/watchdog.html) | 4 | 4 | Feed watchdogs only after critical health checks and capture minimal fault state before reset. |
| 739 | Define fail-safe and degraded modes. | NASA safety-critical requirements guidance expects safe behavior under detected faults. | [NASA SWE-134 - Safety Critical Software Requirements](https://swehb.nasa.gov/display/7150/SWE-134%2B-%2BSafety%2BCritical%2BSoftware%2BRequirements?desktop=true&macroName=div) | 5 | 5 | For each fault class, define continue, degrade, isolate, restart, shut down, or require human intervention. |
| 740 | Assume hardware, clocks, sensors, and networks can lie. | NASA safety guidance treats boundary data as fallible and requiring validation. | [NASA - Putting Safety in the Software](https://ntrs.nasa.gov/api/citations/20020027083/downloads/20020027083.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Validate range, unit, freshness, checksum, sequence, source, plausibility, and cross-sensor consistency. |
| 741 | Initialize everything explicitly before use. | CERT EXP33-C warns that uninitialized memory or state creates nondeterministic behavior. | [SEI CERT EXP33-C](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/rules/expressions-exp/exp33-c/) | 5 | 5 | Require constructors, defaults, zeroing, or sentinel initialization before first read. |
| 742 | Check every meaningful return, status, and error. | CERT ERR33-C warns unchecked errors lead to corrupted state and missed failure handling. | [SEI CERT ERR33-C](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/rules/error-handling-err/err33-c/) | 5 | 5 | Make ignored results explicit with rationale and enforce checks through lint or CI. |
| 743 | Treat concurrency as a safety boundary. | FreeRTOS mutex guidance and safety incidents show races, priority inversion, and deadlocks can become rare catastrophic failures. | [FreeRTOS - Mutexes](https://www.freertos.org/Documentation/02-Kernel/02-Kernel-features/02-Queues-mutexes-and-semaphores/04-Mutexes) | 4 | 5 | Use lock ordering, priority inheritance where needed, time-bounded waits, race detectors, and stress tests. |
| 744 | Tie requirements, tests, and hazards together. | Aviation software guidance links system requirements, verification, and safety impact rather than treating tests as isolated checks. | [FAA AC 20-115D](https://www.faa.gov/regulations_policies/advisory_circulars/index.cfm/go/document.information/documentID/1032046) | 5 | 4 | Map hazards and requirements to tests, coverage, reviews, and change-impact analysis. |
| 745 | Identify people by issuer and subject, not email. | OpenID Connect identity providers expose stable subject identifiers; email can change, be absent, or be reassigned. | [Google OpenID Connect](https://developers.google.com/identity/openid-connect/openid-connect) | 5 | 5 | Use a unique issuer plus subject identity key; treat email as mutable contact data. |
| 746 | Validate tokens against the exact trust boundary. | JWT best-current-practice guidance warns that signature validation alone is insufficient without issuer, audience, type, algorithm, key, and time checks. | [RFC 8725 - JWT Best Current Practices](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8725) | 5 | 5 | Use a verifier configured per token use case; reject wrong issuer, audience, type, algorithm, key, and expiry. |
| 747 | Bind authentication callbacks to the expected issuer. | OAuth security guidance describes mix-up attacks when clients forget which authorization server started a flow. | [RFC 9700 - OAuth 2.0 Security Best Current Practice](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/rfc9700/) | 5 | 4 | Store expected issuer, provider, state, nonce, and PKCE verifier; consume the transaction once. |
| 748 | Read only from the verified assertion object. | SAML parser-differential attacks show systems can validate one representation but authenticate from another. | [GitHub Security Lab - SAML parser differentials](https://github.blog/security/sign-in-as-anyone-bypassing-saml-sso-authentication-with-parser-differentials/) | 4 | 5 | Let the security library return a canonical verified assertion; never reparse raw XML for identity data. |
| 749 | Never let client payloads set authority-bearing fields. | OWASP API guidance describes mass-assignment and object-property authorization flaws where hidden fields become privilege changes. | [OWASP API3:2023 Broken Object Property Level Authorization](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa3-broken-object-property-level-authorization/) | 5 | 5 | Allowlist editable request fields; ignore or reject role, tenant, owner, admin, approval, and state authority fields. |
| 750 | Scope roles to resources, tenants, and projects. | Azure RBAC models role assignments as principal, role, and scope because global labels like admin leak authority. | [Microsoft Azure RBAC overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/role-based-access-control/overview) | 5 | 4 | Store role grants with explicit scope and test that authority in one tenant or project does not carry to another. |
| 751 | Treat recovery and credential changes as authentication ceremonies. | OWASP authentication guidance treats account recovery and sensitive credential changes as flows that require renewed proof of identity. | [OWASP Authentication Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Require recent authentication or step-up for email, password, MFA, device, and recovery changes; rotate sessions afterward. |
| 752 | Test authorization with negative cross-user cases. | OWASP authorization testing guidance emphasizes automated checks for broken access control, not only happy-path role tests. | [OWASP Authorization Testing Automation Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Testing_Automation_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 4 | For protected endpoints, test wrong user, wrong role, wrong tenant, and cross-object access. |
| 753 | Authenticate bytes before interpreting them. | AEAD guidance separates authenticated plaintext release from unauthenticated ciphertext, preventing action on tampered bytes. | [RFC 5116 - Authenticated Encryption](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc5116) | 5 | 5 | Verify integrity before parsing, decompressing, deserializing, rendering, or executing attacker-controlled data. |
| 754 | Bind encrypted data to its context. | Encryption-context guidance makes ciphertext unusable outside its intended purpose, tenant, object, or protocol. | [AWS Encryption SDK Concepts](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/encryption-sdk/latest/developer-guide/concepts.html) | 5 | 5 | Put tenant, purpose, object ID, schema version, and protocol in AAD or encryption context and verify on decrypt. |
| 755 | The verifier chooses cryptographic algorithms. | JWT best-current-practice guidance warns against letting attacker-controlled headers select verification algorithms. | [RFC 8725 - JWT Best Current Practices](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8725) | 5 | 5 | Hardcode algorithm allowlists per verifier; map key IDs to expected key type and purpose. |
| 756 | Give every secret a lifecycle. | OWASP secrets guidance treats creation, ownership, scope, rotation, revocation, expiry, audit, and retirement as core secret-management duties. | [OWASP Secrets Management Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Secrets_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Maintain secret inventory with owner, environment, access, rotation cadence, expiry, and emergency revoke path. |
| 757 | Block secrets before they enter version control. | GitHub push-protection guidance catches supported secret patterns before they become repository history and incident response work. | [GitHub Push Protection](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/concepts/secret-security/push-protection) | 4 | 5 | Use push protection, pre-commit scanning, CI blockers, and clear bypass review for false positives. |
| 758 | Preserve forward secrecy for transport sessions. | TLS 1.3 removes static RSA key exchange so future long-term key compromise does not expose old captured traffic. | [RFC 8446 - TLS 1.3](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8446) | 5 | 5 | Prefer TLS 1.3 or ECDHE-only TLS 1.2; rotate session ticket keys and disable static RSA key exchange. |
| 759 | Use envelope encryption for high-volume protected data. | Cloud KMS envelope-encryption guidance limits root-key exposure and makes data-key rotation practical. | [Google Cloud KMS - Envelope encryption](https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/envelope-encryption) | 5 | 4 | Generate per-object or per-batch data keys; store key ID, version, wrapped key, and ciphertext together. |
| 760 | Survive mobile process death. | Android lifecycle guidance says cached background processes can be killed without normal teardown callbacks. | [Android - Processes and app lifecycle](https://developer.android.com/guide/components/activities/process-lifecycle) | 5 | 5 | Persist critical state before backgrounding; test forced process kill and restore flows. |
| 761 | Keep main-thread work below interaction budgets. | Android responsiveness guidance treats blocking I/O and heavy computation on the main thread as causes of hangs and ANRs. | [Android - Keep your app responsive](https://developer.android.com/topic/performance/anrs/keep-your-app-responsive) | 5 | 5 | Lint or trace disk, network, locks, and expensive computation on UI threads; define launch and input latency budgets. |
| 762 | Treat background execution as quotaed and revocable. | Android Doze and App Standby guidance shows background work can be deferred, limited, or batched for battery and system health. | [Android - Optimize for Doze and App Standby](https://developer.android.com/training/monitoring-device-state/doze-standby) | 5 | 5 | Use platform schedulers, constraints, idempotent checkpoints, and retry paths rather than assuming immediate background execution. |
| 763 | Treat permissions as contextual and revocable. | Android runtime-permission guidance expects users to grant, deny, or later revoke permissions during normal use. | [Android - Request runtime permissions](https://developer.android.com/training/permissions/requesting) | 5 | 5 | Ask at point of need; handle unknown, denied, granted, and revoked states with fallback behavior. |
| 764 | Treat push notifications as hints, not state. | Firebase Cloud Messaging TTL guidance shows messages can expire, collapse, delay, or be discarded. | [Firebase Cloud Messaging - Message lifespan](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/customize-messages/setting-message-lifespan) | 5 | 5 | Use push to invalidate or wake sync; reconcile authoritative state on launch, resume, and notification open. |
| 765 | Keep privacy declarations synchronized with code. | Google Play Data safety guidance requires declared data collection and sharing to match SDKs, features, and actual flows. | [Google Play - Data safety section](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10787469) | 5 | 5 | Map permissions, SDKs, analytics events, network calls, and data classes to store disclosures. |
| 766 | Use public platform APIs and version checks. | Android compatibility guidance warns that non-SDK interfaces can change without notice and may be restricted. | [Android - Restrictions on non-SDK interfaces](https://developer.android.com/guide/app-compatibility/restrictions-non-sdk-interfaces) | 5 | 4 | Ban hidden API reflection in CI; use documented APIs with availability checks and fallback paths. |
| 767 | Log every served decision, not only feedback. | ML engineering guidance requires data collection that explains the decision shown, not just later clicks or outcomes. | [Google - Rules of Machine Learning](https://developers.google.com/machine-learning/guides/rules-of-ml) | 5 | 5 | Emit decision ID, candidates, rank, score, feature versions, model version, policy version, experiment bucket, and feedback join key. |
| 768 | Exposure is not preference. | Learning-to-rank research shows clicks and dwell time are biased by what users were shown and where it appeared. | [Unbiased Learning-to-Rank with Biased Feedback](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/unbiased-learning-rank-biased-feedback/) | 5 | 5 | Store shown, unshown, dismissed, clicked, position, and propensity data before treating behavior as preference. |
| 769 | Validate experiments before believing lift. | Experimentation guidance warns assignment, instrumentation, sample-ratio mismatch, and metric assumptions can corrupt results. | [Trustworthy Online Controlled Experiments](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/trustworthy-online-controlled-experiments/D97B26382EB0EB2DC2019A7A7B518F59) | 4 | 5 | Require guardrails, data-quality checks, SRM checks, predeclared thresholds, and blocked ship decisions on failed checks. |
| 770 | Evaluate ranked lists as lists, not isolated scores. | Recommender-system research treats diversity, novelty, serendipity, and coverage as list-level quality dimensions. | [Diversity, Serendipity, Novelty, and Coverage](https://research.ucc.ie/en/publications/diversity-serendipity-novelty-and-coverage-a-survey-and-empirical/) | 4 | 4 | Track repeated-item rate, catalog coverage, freshness, long-tail exposure, and user coverage alongside accuracy. |
| 771 | Give users controls and reasons for personalization. | The Digital Services Act requires platform transparency and user control around recommender systems in covered contexts. | [EU Digital Services Act](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2022/2065/oj/eng) | 5 | 4 | Expose why-this, personalization reset, personalization off, feed mode, and preference controls where personalization affects outcomes. |
| 772 | Treat user input to adaptive systems as adversarial. | Microsoft's Tay postmortem shows systems that learn from open user input can be gamed, abused, and redirected. | [Microsoft - Learning from Tay's introduction](https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2016/03/25/learning-tays-introduction/) | 4 | 5 | Threat-model abuse cases; quarantine learning inputs; apply moderation, rate limits, and red-team tests. |
| 773 | Do not force users to re-enter data you already have. | WCAG redundant-entry guidance recognizes repeated input as a barrier and an error source. | [WCAG 2.2 - Redundant Entry](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/redundant-entry.html) | 5 | 4 | Preserve drafts, back/forward state, and prior answers; offer same-as and reuse choices where appropriate. |
| 774 | Make notifications user-owned interruption channels. | Android notification-channel guidance gives users category-level control over interruption and visibility. | [Android - Notification channels](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/notifications/channels) | 5 | 4 | Use stable channel categories, in-app opt-outs, and permission prompts tied to user-initiated value. |
| 775 | Test app migrations against old-version fixtures. | Room migration guidance treats schema migrations as executable code that must preserve existing user data across versions. | [Android - Migrate your Room database](https://developer.android.com/training/data-storage/room/migrating-db-versions) | 5 | 5 | Keep old schema fixtures and run migration tests from supported historical versions to current. |
| 776 | Protect workflow definitions as release code. | GitHub rulesets protect branches, tags, and repository rules that determine what can be built and released. | [GitHub - About rulesets](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-rulesets/about-rulesets) | 5 | 5 | Require review and ownership for CI, release, deployment, and signing workflow changes. |
| 777 | Give machines a stable parseable contract. | Git separates human status output from porcelain formats because scripts need output unaffected by color, locale, width, and user config. | [Git status documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-status) | 5 | 5 | Provide JSON, porcelain, or schema-versioned output for automation; keep human output free to improve. |
| 778 | Make empty scope explicit, never implicit all. | SRE production guidance and outage patterns show empty selectors, empty config, and accidental broad targeting can amplify operator mistakes. | [Google SRE - Production Services Best Practices](https://sre.google/sre-book/service-best-practices/) | 5 | 5 | Reject empty filters by default; require explicit all-mode flags and show the matched count before mutation. |
| 779 | Put dangerous commands behind preview and force ladders. | Git clean uses dry-run, interactive mode, and force gates to prevent irreversible deletion by surprise. | [Git clean documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clean) | 5 | 5 | Destructive commands show affected resources, support dry run, and require stronger confirmation as blast radius grows. |
| 780 | Make elevated privilege visible and constrained. | kubectl conventions expose context and methods because operator tools can cross cluster, namespace, and privilege boundaries. | [Kubernetes kubectl usage conventions](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubectl/conventions/) | 5 | 5 | Show environment, identity, scope, and privilege mode; default to read-only where possible. |
| 781 | Cache keys are part of the data model. | CDN Vary guidance shows cached representations must vary by the same dimensions that change the response. | [Cloudflare - Vary for images](https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/concepts/vary/) | 5 | 5 | Declare cache dimensions per route: encoding, locale, device, auth segment, experiment, and content version. |
| 782 | Collapse user cache state to safe segments or bypass. | Fastly cookie guidance shows raw cookies reduce cache efficiency and can leak personalized content if not handled deliberately. | [Fastly - Cookie header](https://www.fastly.com/documentation/reference/http/http-headers/Cookie/) | 5 | 5 | Strip irrelevant cookies for public routes; cache only segment keys such as locale or logged-in state. |
| 783 | Normalize cache and origin inputs symmetrically. | Cloudflare cache-key guidance warns that cache normalization must match what the origin interprets. | [Cloudflare - Cache keys](https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/how-to/cache-keys/) | 5 | 5 | If query params, paths, or headers are normalized for cache, send the same canonical request to origin or prove equivalence. |
| 784 | Static-looking URLs are not proof of static content. | Cache-deception defenses exist because dynamic routes can be disguised with asset-like suffixes. | [Cloudflare - Cache Deception Armor](https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/cache-security/cache-deception-armor/) | 5 | 5 | Match route class, content type, extension, and auth state before allowing public cache. |
| 785 | Validators belong to representations, not abstract resources. | HTTP semantics distinguish selected representations; negotiated variants can need distinct strong validators. | [RFC 9110 - HTTP Semantics](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9110) | 5 | 4 | Include encoding, language, media type, and variant dimensions in strong ETag generation. |
| 786 | Never compress secrets with attacker-controlled text. | BREACH shows HTTP compression can leak secrets when reflected input and secret bytes share a compressed response. | [BREACH attack](https://breachattack.com/) | 4 | 5 | Disable compression or separate/mask secrets on pages that reflect attacker-controlled input. |
| 787 | Bound stale cache behavior. | RFC 5861 makes stale-while-revalidate and stale-if-error explicit cache-control extensions, not unlimited freshness waivers. | [RFC 5861 - HTTP Cache-Control Extensions for Stale Content](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc5861) | 5 | 4 | Define max-age, stale-while-revalidate, stale-if-error, validators, and monitoring per route. |
| 788 | Invalidate by dependency tags, not only URLs. | Fastly surrogate-key guidance lets related pages, fragments, and variants be purged by content relationships. | [Fastly - Surrogate keys](https://www.fastly.com/documentation/guides/full-site-delivery/purging/working-with-surrogate-keys/) | 5 | 4 | Emit cache tags from application code and purge by object, collection, tenant, deploy, and route dependency. |
| 789 | Redirect and HSTS changes are cached program state. | HSTS and permanent redirects change future browser behavior beyond the current response. | [MDN - Strict-Transport-Security](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Strict-Transport-Security) | 4 | 4 | Audit subdomains before preload; keep canonical redirect chains short and reversible before permanent rollout. |
| 790 | Reject ambiguous HTTP framing at the edge. | HTTP/1.1 framing rules treat conflicting Content-Length and Transfer-Encoding as invalid because parser disagreement enables smuggling. | [RFC 9112 - HTTP/1.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9112) | 5 | 5 | Reject duplicate or conflicting length metadata and invalid chunking before proxying to upstreams. |
| 791 | Treat edge configuration as production code. | Fastly's 2021 outage summary shows a valid edge configuration path can create global failure quickly. | [Fastly - June 8 outage summary](https://www.fastly.com/blog/summary-of-june-8-outage) | 5 | 5 | Review, test, stage, probe, monitor, and roll back CDN and edge config like application releases. |
| 792 | State the decision before the metric. | Google HEART metrics connect measurements to goals and signals, not dashboards for their own sake. | [Google Research - HEART metrics](https://research.google/pubs/measuring-the-user-experience-on-a-large-scale-user-centered-metrics-for-web-applications/) | 5 | 5 | Metric specs include goal, signal, owner, decision threshold, and expected action. |
| 793 | Keep one objective and many guardrails. | Trustworthy experimentation guidance separates primary objectives from guardrails and data-quality diagnostics. | [Microsoft Research - Trustworthy experimentation during experiment stage](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/articles/patterns-of-trustworthy-experimentation-during-experiment-stage/) | 5 | 5 | Experiment configs declare primary metric, guardrails, diagnostics, and ship-blocking failure criteria. |
| 794 | Match assignment grain to analysis grain. | Online-experiment research shows randomization unit, storage grain, and analysis unit affect independence and variance assumptions. | [Deng et al. - Choice of Randomization Unit](https://alexdeng.github.io/public/files/jsm2011-deng.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Store assignment unit and analysis unit explicitly; block naive aggregation across mismatched grains. |
| 795 | Run the null path through measurement systems. | A/A tests reveal assignment, logging, scorecard, and analysis bugs before real experiment decisions depend on them. | [Trustworthy Online Controlled Experiments - A/A Test](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/abs/trustworthy-online-controlled-experiments/aa-test/B0C8E3BDBB7C9519F851A3F597E6715F) | 5 | 5 | Run A/A checks for exposure, event logging, denominators, and scorecard deltas before launch. |
| 796 | Never trust a broken denominator. | Sample-ratio mismatch guidance treats denominator drift as evidence of selection, logging, routing, or eligibility corruption. | [Microsoft Research - Diagnosing Sample Ratio Mismatch in A/B Testing](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/articles/diagnosing-sample-ratio-mismatch-in-a-b-testing/) | 5 | 5 | Block experiment reads when SRM or denominator checks fail. |
| 797 | Peek only with a stopping contract. | Sequential analysis work shows repeated looks at ordinary significance thresholds inflate false confidence. | [Always Valid Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/1512.04922) | 5 | 4 | Choose fixed horizon or sequential-valid inference before launch; do not call winners from ad hoc peeking. |
| 798 | Report magnitude and uncertainty, not only significance. | The ASA p-value statement warns p-values alone do not measure effect size or practical importance. | [ASA Statement on Statistical Significance and P-Values](https://www.amstat.org/asa/files/pdfs/P-ValueStatement.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Show effect estimate, confidence or credible interval, sample size, and practical threshold. |
| 799 | Treat missing data as telemetry. | Missing-data methods distinguish mechanisms that affect bias and validity rather than treating nulls as harmless blanks. | [NCBI - Types of Missing Data](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK493614/) | 5 | 5 | Track missingness rate, reason codes, source, and analysis assumptions for nullable events and fields. |
| 800 | Segment before averaging. | Netflix experimentation work shows treatment effects can differ across groups even when aggregate results look acceptable. | [Netflix - Heterogeneous Treatment Effects](https://netflixtechblog.medium.com/heterogeneous-treatment-effects-at-netflix-da5c3dd58833) | 4 | 5 | Scorecards include required cohort slices and block material regressions in protected or critical segments. |
| 801 | Revalidate proxy metrics over time. | Netflix surrogate-index research treats short-term proxies as models that need validation against long-term outcomes. | [Netflix - Evaluating the Surrogate Index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.11922) | 5 | 4 | Give proxy metrics target outcomes, validation date, error bounds, owner, and recalibration schedule. |
| 802 | Prove query plans before adding indexes. | PostgreSQL EXPLAIN guidance shows access paths, estimates, sort/hash work, and plan choices that determine whether an index helps. | [PostgreSQL - Using EXPLAIN](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/using-explain.html) | 5 | 5 | Hot-query changes include plans on production-like cardinality and a before/after regression threshold. |
| 803 | Treat index builds as capacity events. | MongoDB index-build guidance shows live index creation consumes resources and can affect writes and cluster behavior. | [MongoDB - Index builds on populated collections](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/core/index-creation/) | 5 | 5 | Plan index builds with duration, resource budget, lock/write impact, monitoring, and rollback. |
| 804 | Store data by access pattern, not only entity shape. | MongoDB data-modeling guidance favors embedding or referencing based on query and update patterns. | [MongoDB - Data Modeling](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/data-modeling/) | 5 | 5 | Document top reads and writes, cardinality, embedding/reference decisions, and denormalization ownership. |
| 805 | Budget dynamic fields. | Elasticsearch mapping-explosion guidance warns that uncontrolled fields can exhaust memory and break ingest/query behavior. | [Elastic - Mapping explosion](https://www.elastic.co/docs/troubleshoot/elasticsearch/mapping-explosion) | 5 | 5 | Use strict mappings, templates, field-count alerts, and rejected-document handling. |
| 806 | Design keys for heat, not just identity. | Shard-key guidance warns low-cardinality or monotonic keys create hot shards even with enough total capacity. | [MongoDB - Choose a shard key](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/core/sharding-choose-a-shard-key/) | 5 | 5 | Review partition-key cardinality, monotonicity, hot-key risk, and targeted-query behavior. |
| 807 | Do not sell async replication as zero-loss high availability. | Jepsen analyses show acknowledged writes can be lost when replication and failover guarantees are weaker than users assume. | [Jepsen - Redis-Raft 1b3fbf6](https://jepsen.io/analyses/redis-raft-1b3fbf6) | 5 | 5 | Document write-loss budget and test failover behavior; use stronger durability for critical writes. |
| 808 | Treat CDC offsets as production state. | Debezium stores connector offsets and schema history as state required for correct replay and continuity. | [Debezium - Storing state of a connector](https://debezium.io/documentation/reference/stable/configuration/storage.html) | 5 | 5 | Replicate, back up, monitor, and restore offset and schema-history storage; test consumer replay. |
| 809 | Remember that deletes can create read debt. | Cassandra tombstone guidance shows deletes and TTLs can continue taxing reads until compaction clears them. | [Apache Cassandra - Tombstones](https://cassandra.apache.org/doc/latest/cassandra/managing/operating/compaction/tombstones.html) | 5 | 4 | Monitor tombstones, review compaction strategy, and test deleted-heavy partitions. |
| 810 | Make search freshness explicit. | Elasticsearch near-real-time docs explain that indexed writes are not immediately searchable until refresh. | [Elastic - Near real-time search](https://www.elastic.co/docs/manage-data/data-store/near-real-time-search) | 5 | 4 | Declare stale-window tolerance and use wait-for-refresh or primary-store reads for strict flows. |
| 811 | Anchor and bound graph traversals. | Neo4j query-plan guidance shows broad starts and unbounded expansions can explode work. | [Neo4j - Understanding query plans](https://neo4j.com/docs/cypher-manual/current/planning-and-tuning/execution-plans/) | 5 | 4 | Require indexed anchors, bounded path lengths, and row/db-hit thresholds for graph queries. |
| 812 | Ship only route-critical JavaScript at startup. | The Pinterest PWA case study shows reducing JavaScript weight can materially improve load and interaction performance. | [Pinterest PWA performance case study](https://medium.com/dev-channel/a-pinterest-progressive-web-app-performance-case-study-3bd6ed2e6154) | 4 | 5 | Put size budgets on route entry chunks and require dynamic imports for noncritical features. |
| 813 | Hydrate only interactive leaves. | Next.js server/client component guidance shows client boundaries pull their module graph into browser JavaScript. | [Next.js - Server and Client Components](https://nextjs.org/docs/app/getting-started/server-and-client-components) | 5 | 5 | Keep client component boundaries near interactive widgets; review top-level client boundaries. |
| 814 | Make first render deterministic. | React hydration guidance treats mismatches as bugs and warns that client/server markup must match. | [React hydrateRoot](https://react.dev/reference/react-dom/client/hydrateRoot) | 5 | 5 | Avoid time, randomness, locale drift, browser-only state, and client-only data in server-rendered initial markup. |
| 815 | Split long tasks. | web.dev guidance defines long tasks as main-thread work over 50 ms that blocks responsiveness. | [web.dev - Optimize long tasks](https://web.dev/articles/optimize-long-tasks) | 5 | 5 | Yield heavy work, chunk loops, or move work to workers; track long tasks on load and interaction paths. |
| 816 | Batch layout reads before writes. | Layout-thrashing guidance shows reading layout after DOM/style writes forces synchronous layout and hurts responsiveness. | [web.dev - Avoid layout thrashing](https://web.dev/articles/avoid-large-complex-layouts-and-layout-thrashing) | 5 | 4 | Separate measurement and mutation phases; flag offset or bounding-box reads after writes in the same frame. |
| 817 | Reserve space for late-loading content. | CLS guidance shows ads, embeds, images, and injected widgets shift layout unless space is reserved. | [web.dev - Optimize Cumulative Layout Shift](https://web.dev/articles/optimize-cls) | 5 | 5 | Give images, ads, iframes, embeds, and skeletons stable width, height, aspect ratio, or min-height. |
| 818 | Make the LCP asset eager and discoverable. | LCP guidance warns likely LCP resources should not be hidden behind JavaScript, CSS discovery, or lazy loading. | [web.dev - Optimize Largest Contentful Paint](https://web.dev/articles/optimize-lcp) | 5 | 5 | Put the likely hero asset in initial HTML and avoid lazy loading it. |
| 819 | Serve media to its rendered slot. | Responsive-image guidance prevents oversized media from wasting bandwidth and delaying critical resources. | [web.dev - Serve responsive images](https://web.dev/articles/serve-responsive-images) | 5 | 4 | Use srcset, sizes, picture, and DPR caps so intrinsic asset size matches display needs. |
| 820 | Put third-party scripts on probation. | Third-party script guidance shows external scripts can block rendering and consume main-thread time. | [web.dev - Efficiently load third-party JavaScript](https://web.dev/articles/efficiently-load-third-party-javascript) | 5 | 4 | Maintain a script registry with owner, value, load strategy, budget, and removal path. |
| 821 | Window large DOM surfaces. | DOM-size guidance ties large DOMs to slower style, layout, rendering, memory, and interaction. | [web.dev - DOM size and interactivity](https://web.dev/articles/dom-size-and-interactivity) | 5 | 4 | Virtualize or paginate large lists and tables; monitor DOM node counts. |
