| 822 | Tear down every external handle. | React effect guidance requires cleanup for subscriptions and external resources when components re-render or unmount. | [React - useEffect](https://react.dev/reference/react/useEffect) | 5 | 4 | Clean up listeners, timers, subscriptions, observers, workers, and in-flight fetches. |
| 823 | Require stack evidence for hot paths. | Performance methodology guidance uses stack traces and profiling to connect resource cost to code paths. | [Brendan Gregg - Performance Analysis Methodology](https://www.brendangregg.com/methodology.html) | 5 | 5 | Perf investigations include CPU, wall, heap, lock, or I/O profiles tied to build and workload. |
| 824 | Treat data layout as algorithmic. | Processor optimization guidance shows memory locality, cache misses, and working set can dominate runtime. | [Intel Optimization Reference Manual](https://www.intel.com/content/dam/doc/manual/64-ia-32-architectures-optimization-manual.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Hot loops and data structures track cache misses, working-set size, and contiguous-access opportunities. |
| 825 | Make backpressure cross every async boundary. | Reactive Streams exists because asynchronous producers must not overwhelm consumers with unbounded buffering. | [Reactive Streams](https://www.reactive-streams.org/) | 5 | 5 | Use demand, credits, bounded queues, semaphores, or rate limits across streams, jobs, fanout, and queues. |
| 826 | Give queues expiry, fairness, and drain visibility. | AWS queue-backlog guidance shows backlogs can turn spikes into stale work and prolonged outages. | [AWS Builders Library - Avoiding insurmountable queue backlogs](https://aws.amazon.com/builders-library/avoiding-insurmountable-queue-backlogs/) | 5 | 5 | Track oldest age, arrival rate, service rate, drain time, per-tenant fairness, priority lanes, and TTLs. |
| 827 | Derive concurrency limits from arrival rate and latency. | Little's Law relates average in-flight work to arrival rate and time in system. | [Little - A Proof for the Queuing Formula](https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/10.1287/opre.9.3.383) | 5 | 5 | Set in-flight limits from target throughput and latency budget; shrink limits when downstream latency rises. |
| 828 | Load test past the cliff. | SRE reliability testing guidance includes stress and overload tests to find saturation and failure behavior. | [Google SRE - Testing for Reliability](https://sre.google/sre-book/testing-reliability/) | 5 | 5 | Run stress, spike, and soak tests; record first exhausted resource and recovery behavior. |
| 829 | Benchmark the benchmark. | Active benchmarking guidance warns that benchmarks can confidently measure the wrong bottleneck. | [Brendan Gregg - Active Benchmarking](https://www.brendangregg.com/activebenchmarking.html) | 5 | 4 | Benchmark reports include environment, warmup, variance, reproduction steps, and profiler evidence. |
| 830 | Make performance regressions owned release decisions. | Chromium performance waterfall docs route regressions to owners instead of letting drift accumulate silently. | [Chromium - Perf waterfall](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/lkgr/docs/speed/perf_waterfall.md) | 4 | 5 | Perf CI assigns owner and requires fix, revert, or explicit waiver when thresholds are exceeded. |
| 831 | Name the oracle before the test. | Testing-oracle research shows input generation is cheaper than deciding whether observed behavior is correct. | [The Oracle Problem in Software Testing](https://pure.kaist.ac.kr/en/publications/the-oracle-problem-in-software-testing-a-survey/) | 5 | 5 | Each advanced test declares its oracle: spec, model, metamorphic relation, differential check, contract, snapshot, or reviewed golden. |
| 832 | Fuzz narrow deterministic entrypoints. | libFuzzer guidance expects harnesses to be fast, deterministic, corpus-backed, and sanitizer-friendly. | [libFuzzer documentation](https://llvm.org/docs/LibFuzzer.html) | 5 | 5 | Give parsers, decoders, and protocol handlers fuzz targets with seed corpora, sanitizer builds, and saved crash repros. |
| 833 | Fuzz stateful protocols as sequences. | RESTler research shows API bugs often appear through invalid operation sequences, not isolated malformed requests. | [RESTler - Stateful REST API Fuzzing](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/restler-stateful-rest-api-fuzzing/) | 5 | 5 | Use API specs to generate request sequences, infer producer-consumer dependencies, and retain failing traces. |
| 834 | Mutation tests should attack changed semantics. | Google's mutation-testing experience shows surviving mutants expose weak assertions better than coverage alone. | [State of Mutation Testing at Google](https://research.google/pubs/state-of-mutation-testing-at-google/) | 5 | 5 | Report live mutants on changed lines and require stronger assertions or explicit dismissal. |
| 835 | Consumer contracts should assert only consumed behavior. | Pact guidance keeps consumer contracts focused on fields and semantics the consumer actually depends on. | [Pact - Writing Consumer Tests](https://docs.pact.io/consumer) | 4 | 4 | Provider verification runs in CI; consumer contracts avoid freezing unused provider fields or internal formatting. |
| 836 | Snapshot tests are reviewed artifacts, not magic assertions. | Jest snapshot guidance makes snapshots useful only when changes are intentionally reviewed and readable. | [Jest Snapshot Testing](https://jestjs.io/docs/snapshot-testing) | 4 | 4 | Keep snapshots small and stable; require review for updates and use visual tests for appearance-sensitive behavior. |
| 837 | Cover input interactions, not just individual values. | NIST combinatorial testing shows many failures come from small combinations of parameter values. | [NIST Combinatorial Testing](https://www.nist.gov/programs-projects/combinatorial-testing) | 5 | 4 | Generate constrained pairwise or t-way matrices and measure interaction coverage for important configuration spaces. |
| 838 | Chaos tests need a hypothesis and blast radius. | Chaos-engineering guidance defines experiments around steady-state expectations and controlled failure injection. | [Chaos Engineering](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1702.05843) | 5 | 4 | Define steady-state metric, treatment, abort condition, scope limit, and follow-up fix before injecting failure. |
| 839 | Test data needs lived state. | Meta rich-state simulation work shows empty synthetic users miss bugs that require accumulated history and relationships. | [Meta - Rich-State Simulated Populations](https://arxiv.org/html/2403.15374v1) | 4 | 4 | Maintain isolated personas with evolved state, relationships, renewal rules, privacy isolation, and coverage tracking. |
| 840 | Retire wire slots forever. | Protobuf best practices warn that removed field numbers and names remain part of the historical wire contract. | [Protocol Buffers - Best Practices](https://protobuf.dev/best-practices/dos-donts/) | 5 | 5 | Reserve removed field numbers and names; CI rejects reuse across messages and history. |
| 841 | Read durable data with the writer's schema. | Avro schema resolution relies on the writer schema plus reader schema, not only the current application model. | [Apache Avro Specification](https://avro.apache.org/docs/1.11.1/specification/) | 5 | 5 | Store writer schema or schema ID with durable data and test old-writer to new-reader pairs. |
| 842 | Defaults are migration code. | Avro defaults materialize missing reader fields and therefore become compatibility behavior. | [Apache Avro Specification](https://avro.apache.org/docs/1.11.1/specification/) | 5 | 4 | Schema diffs require explicit defaults for added reader fields and tests for default materialization. |
| 843 | Check schema compatibility against retained history. | Schema-registry compatibility with only the latest version can miss consumers that replay older retained data. | [Confluent - Schema Evolution and Compatibility](https://docs.confluent.io/platform/current/schema-registry/fundamentals/schema-evolution.html) | 5 | 5 | Use transitive compatibility modes for topics, logs, and state stores with replay or long retention. |
| 844 | Put type identity in the payload envelope. | Confluent serializers include schema identity so consumers can decode bytes under the correct schema. | [Confluent - Kafka SerDes](https://docs.confluent.io/platform/current/schema-registry/fundamentals/serdes-develop/index.html) | 5 | 5 | Include format version and schema ID in headers or envelopes; fail closed on unknown versions. |
| 845 | Persist logical types, not naked physical bytes. | Parquet logical types preserve semantics such as UTF-8, decimals, UUIDs, and timestamps beyond physical storage. | [Parquet Logical Types](https://parquet.apache.org/docs/file-format/types/logicaltypes/) | 5 | 5 | Emit logical-type annotations and cross-engine round-trip tests for decimals, timestamps, UUIDs, and strings. |
| 846 | Treat skipping metadata as correctness data. | Parquet predicate-pushdown bugs show min, max, null, and page-index metadata can change query results when wrong. | [SPARK-23852 Parquet bug](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/SPARK-23852) | 4 | 5 | Compare query results with predicate pushdown and skipping enabled and disabled on edge-case data. |
| 847 | Treat FFI declarations as unsafe contracts. | Rust 2024 unsafe extern guidance makes foreign declarations explicit because the compiler cannot verify ABI truth. | [Rust - Unsafe extern blocks](https://doc.rust-lang.org/edition-guide/rust-2024/unsafe-extern.html) | 5 | 5 | Generate bindings where possible, review extern modules, and add ABI layout and calling-convention tests. |
| 848 | Expose a stable ABI or require rebuilds. | Node-API exists because runtime-internal native addons otherwise break across Node versions. | [Node-API](https://nodejs.org/api/n-api.html) | 5 | 4 | Use stable ABI layers or publish rebuild requirements with ABI-diff checks and compatibility tags. |
| 849 | The allocator that allocates must deallocate. | Rust CString ownership APIs show foreign allocation ownership must be explicit across boundaries. | [Rust CString](https://doc.rust-lang.org/beta/std/ffi/struct.CString.html) | 5 | 5 | Export matching free functions, wrap ownership in RAII types, and ban freeing foreign pointers directly. |
| 850 | Managed pointers cross native boundaries only pinned, copied, or handled. | cgo pointer rules restrict how Go pointers may be passed to C because garbage collectors must track movable memory. | [Go cgo command](https://pkg.go.dev/cmd/cgo) | 5 | 5 | Use handles, pinners, or copies into foreign memory; enable pointer-checking CI where available. |
| 851 | Classify every foreign handle by lifetime. | JNI reference types have local, global, weak, thread-affine, borrowed, and owned lifetime rules. | [JNI Functions](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/22/docs/specs/jni/functions.html) | 5 | 5 | Use typed wrappers for local/global refs, release local refs in loops, and never cache thread-local environments as globals. |
| 852 | Keep critical GC regions tiny and nonblocking. | JNI critical-array guidance restricts blocking, callbacks, and arbitrary JNI work while memory is pinned. | [JNI GetPrimitiveArrayCritical](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/22/docs/specs/jni/functions.html) | 5 | 5 | Copy data out, release before I/O or callbacks, and avoid allocation or logging inside critical regions. |
| 853 | In-process native code shares the host failure domain. | Erlang NIF guidance warns native code can block schedulers or crash the VM. | [Erlang NIF documentation](https://www.erlang.org/doc/apps/erts/erl_nif.html) | 5 | 5 | Run untrusted or high-risk native code out of process, in workers, through ports, or in sandboxed runtimes. |
| 854 | Signal handlers and forked children are async-signal-safe islands. | POSIX signal-safety guidance limits what can run safely in handlers and after fork in multithreaded programs. | [signal-safety(7)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/signal-safety.7.html) | 5 | 5 | In handlers, write to pipes or atomics only; avoid malloc, logging, locks, and runtime calls. |
| 855 | Normalize errors before crossing native boundaries. | JNI exception rules show VM exceptions, errno, panics, and native exceptions do not compose automatically across ABIs. | [JNI Functions](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/22/docs/specs/jni/functions.html) | 5 | 4 | Pick one error channel per boundary, catch panics and exceptions, and translate failures before returning. |
| 856 | Binary packages must encode ABI and platform policy. | Python compatibility tags encode interpreter, ABI, platform, and libc expectations for binary wheels. | [Python platform compatibility tags](https://packaging.python.org/en/latest/specifications/platform-compatibility-tags/) | 5 | 4 | Build artifact matrices by interpreter, ABI, platform, and libc; import-test every published artifact. |
| 857 | Native binaries need provenance and rollback. | The XZ backdoor showed native libraries can become high-impact executable supply-chain boundaries. | [NVD CVE-2024-3094](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2024-3094) | 5 | 5 | Keep SBOMs, signed provenance, source-vs-archive checks, pinned native deps, and emergency blocklists. |
| 858 | Protect business flows, not just endpoints. | OWASP API6:2023 covers abuse of legitimate business flows such as purchasing, posting, or reservation hoarding. | [OWASP API6:2023 Unrestricted Access to Sensitive Business Flows](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa6-unrestricted-access-to-sensitive-business-flows/) | 5 | 5 | Attach abuse policies to flows and protect posting, purchasing, invites, payouts, exports, and reservations. |
| 859 | Quotas should count what hurts. | GitHub rate-limit docs use primary and secondary limits because different operations have different cost and abuse impact. | [GitHub REST API rate limits](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api) | 4 | 5 | Meter weighted route cost, mutation count, generated content, token issuance, concurrency, and expensive side effects. |
| 860 | Put kill switches around external-cost actions. | Twilio fraud guidance treats phone verification as a cost and abuse surface requiring controls. | [Twilio - Preventing Fraud in Verify](https://www.twilio.com/docs/verify/preventing-toll-fraud) | 4 | 5 | Add spend caps, destination controls, velocity limits, provider disable switches, and anomaly alerts. |
| 861 | Bind challenges to the exact action. | reCAPTCHA Enterprise guidance recommends verifying action names and scores before the protected action completes. | [Google Cloud - reCAPTCHA best practices for automated threats](https://docs.cloud.google.com/recaptcha/docs/best-practices-oat) | 4 | 5 | Verify token, score, TTL, and expected action server-side before login, checkout, account change, or card authorization. |
| 862 | New accounts earn reach. | OWASP automated-threat taxonomy identifies bulk account creation as a foundation for later abuse. | [OWASP OAT-019 Account Creation](https://owasp.org/www-project-automated-threats-to-web-applications/assets/oats/EN/OAT-019_Account_Creation) | 5 | 5 | Start accounts in probation with caps on posts, invites, messages, reviews, coupons, exports, and withdrawals. |
| 863 | A successful login is not trust. | The 23andMe investigation showed valid compromised sessions can still enable broad graph and data exposure. | [23andMe joint investigation](https://www.priv.gc.ca/en/opc-actions-and-decisions/investigations/investigations-into-businesses/2025/pipeda-2025-001/) | 5 | 5 | Apply post-login risk checks, export step-up, graph fanout caps, device drift detection, and session interruption. |
| 864 | Stop bad credentials before sessions exist. | NIST identity guidance requires checking prospective passwords against compromised-value lists. | [NIST SP 800-63B](https://pages.nist.gov/800-63-4/sp800-63b.html) | 5 | 5 | Block breached passwords at set and change time; add leaked-credential detection and adaptive MFA. |
| 865 | Recovery flows must resist enumeration and flooding. | OWASP forgot-password guidance recommends uniform responses, rate limits, expiring tokens, and session handling. | [OWASP Forgot Password Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Forgot_Password_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 4 | Use uniform timing and responses, per-account throttles, single-use tokens, and session invalidation after reset. |
| 866 | Crawler consent requires enforcement. | Bot-control guidance and crawler incident reports show robots.txt and user agents are preference signals, not guarantees. | [AWS WAF Bot Control use cases](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/waf/latest/developerguide/waf-bot-control-use-cases.html) | 4 | 4 | Use verified-bot registries, WAF labels, path policy, IP/ASN behavior signals, and purpose-specific allow or block rules. |
| 867 | Do not reveal abuse thresholds. | Automated-threat guidance warns attackers can tune around visible thresholds and exact classifier outputs. | [Google Cloud - reCAPTCHA best practices for automated threats](https://docs.cloud.google.com/recaptcha/docs/best-practices-oat) | 4 | 4 | Use generic user errors, internal reason codes, delayed enforcement where appropriate, and hidden risk scores. |
| 868 | Separate detection, labels, enforcement, and review. | Pinterest trust-and-safety engineering separates labels from enforcement mapping so policies can evolve safely. | [Pinterest - Label-based enforcement pipeline](https://medium.com/pinterest-engineering/building-a-label-based-enforcement-pipeline-for-trust-safety-4b05a409cb5d) | 4 | 4 | Store policy labels, map them to surface-specific actions, and feed appeals or reversals back into review. |
| 869 | Make abuse telemetry a product schema. | OWASP logging guidance supports structured security events that can be investigated and tuned. | [OWASP Logging Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Logging_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Log actor, subject, action, resource, outcome, device, IP/ASN, risk score, assessment ID, and request ID. |
| 870 | DNS absence is cached too. | RFC 2308 defines negative caching for NXDOMAIN and related responses, which affects later record creation. | [RFC 2308 - Negative Caching of DNS Queries](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc2308) | 5 | 4 | Pre-create names before rollout and wait out positive and negative TTLs before assuming deletion or creation is visible. |
| 871 | Certificates should renew before humans matter. | ACME and Let's Encrypt guidance make certificate management automatable, reducing manual expiry outages. | [Let's Encrypt Integration Guide](https://letsencrypt.org/docs/integration-guide/) | 5 | 5 | Use ACME automation, expiry SLOs, renewal staging tests, randomized renewal windows, and runway alerts. |
| 872 | Discovery membership means ready, not merely alive. | Kubernetes readiness probes decide whether a target should receive traffic, distinct from liveness. | [Kubernetes - Liveness, Readiness, and Startup Probes](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/probes/) | 5 | 5 | Register only warmed and dependency-ready targets; flip readiness before shutdown. |
| 873 | Drain before you kill. | Load balancer deregistration delay lets in-flight requests finish after a target stops receiving new work. | [AWS ELB - Deregistration delay](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/elasticloadbalancing/latest/application/edit-target-group-attributes.html#modify-target-group-health-settings) | 5 | 5 | Deregister targets, wait for draining or unused state, then terminate processes or instances. |
| 874 | SNAT ports are capacity. | AWS NAT gateway docs describe per-destination connection limits and port-allocation behavior. | [AWS VPC - NAT gateway basics](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/latest/userguide/nat-gateway-basics.html) | 5 | 4 | Monitor NAT errors, pool connections, use private endpoints, and budget per-destination outbound fanout. |
| 875 | Generate provider allowlists from metadata. | Webhook provider guidance warns IP ranges can change and should be synchronized from provider sources. | [GitHub - Webhook best practices](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/best-practices-for-using-webhooks) | 5 | 4 | Sync IP ranges on a schedule, review firewall diffs, handle IPv4 and IPv6, and avoid hardcoded CIDRs in app code. |
| 876 | Email identity must align with the visible sender. | Gmail sender requirements and DMARC depend on aligned SPF, DKIM, and domain policy for trustworthy delivery. | [Gmail Email Sender Guidelines](https://support.google.com/a/answer/81126) | 5 | 5 | Validate SPF, DKIM, DMARC, List-Unsubscribe, one-click unsubscribe, and stream-specific signing in CI. |
| 877 | Verify webhook bytes before parsing, then enqueue and dedupe. | Stripe and GitHub webhook signatures depend on the exact raw body, and events may be redelivered. | [GitHub - Validating webhook deliveries](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/validating-webhook-deliveries) | 5 | 5 | Verify HMAC and timestamp on raw bytes, persist an inbox by event ID, then acknowledge quickly. |
| 878 | Clock sync is an application dependency. | NTP and cloud time-sync guidance matter for certificates, signatures, leases, webhooks, and token validity. | [RFC 5905 - NTPv4](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc5905) | 5 | 5 | Monitor clock offset, use monotonic clocks for durations, and fail closed when signing or token checks exceed skew limits. |
| 879 | Endpoint rotation must pierce runtime caches. | AWS SDK guidance warns JVM DNS caching can retain stale addresses longer than service endpoint rotation expects. | [AWS SDK for Java - JVM DNS TTL](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/sdk-for-java/latest/developer-guide/jvm-ttl-dns.html) | 5 | 4 | Configure runtime DNS TTLs and connection-pool lifetimes; re-resolve after connection failures. |
| 880 | The recovery plane must not share fate with the broken plane. | Meta's 2021 outage showed network and DNS failures can also disable tools and access needed for recovery. | [Meta - October 4 outage details](https://engineering.fb.com/2021/10/05/networking-traffic/outage-details/) | 5 | 5 | Test break-glass access, out-of-band runbooks, recovery credentials, and control-plane independence. |
| 881 | Ask for extension permissions late and narrowly. | Chrome extension permission docs support optional permissions to reduce initial trust and blast radius. | [Chrome Permissions API](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/extensions/reference/api/permissions) | 5 | 5 | Manifest lint maps every host and API permission to a feature and requires runtime prompts where possible. |
| 882 | One extension gets one clear purpose. | Chrome Web Store quality rules require a narrow, easy-to-understand single purpose. | [Chrome Web Store quality guidelines](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/webstore/program-policies/quality-guidelines/) | 5 | 5 | Map permissions, data types, network destinations, and UI surfaces to the stated purpose. |
| 883 | Reviewed packages own executable code. | Chrome and Firefox extension guidance restrict remote hosted code because it can change after review and consent. | [Chrome - Deal with remote hosted code violations](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/extensions/develop/migrate/remote-hosted-code) | 5 | 5 | Block CDN scripts, remote JavaScript or WASM execution, eval, and unsafe dynamic imports. |
| 884 | Trust state gates code execution. | VS Code workspace trust guidance disables risky extension features when workspace content is not trusted. | [VS Code Workspace Trust Extension Guide](https://code.visualstudio.com/api/extension-guides/workspace-trust) | 5 | 5 | In untrusted mode, disable process execution, workspace-defined commands, task launches, and write-capable automation. |
| 885 | Sandbox extension UI and message boundaries. | VS Code webviews and Chrome extension messaging bridge untrusted UI content to privileged extension code. | [VS Code Webview API](https://code.visualstudio.com/api/extension-guides/webview) | 5 | 5 | Use restrictive CSP, no inline scripts, schema-validated messages, sender allowlists, and least-capability bridges. |
| 886 | Extension manifests are security contracts. | Extension manifests and plugin configuration files declare activation, capabilities, compatibility, and dependencies. | [VS Code extension manifest](https://code.visualstudio.com/api/references/extension-manifest) | 5 | 4 | Validate manifest schema, version ranges, activation events, optional dependencies, and capability declarations in CI. |
| 887 | Marketplace review is not a trust boundary. | Extension runtime-security guidance and marketplace incidents show reviews and badges reduce risk but do not prove safety. | [VS Code extension runtime security](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/configure/extensions/extension-runtime-security) | 5 | 5 | Maintain allowlists, blocklists, kill switches, report paths, and credential-rotation playbooks. |
| 888 | Separate stable, beta, and canary update channels. | JetBrains marketplace guidance supports custom release channels so experimental builds do not silently replace stable installs. | [JetBrains - Custom release channels](https://plugins.jetbrains.com/docs/marketplace/custom-release-channels.html) | 5 | 4 | Release automation requires explicit channel, changelog, version bump, compatibility range, and promotion approval. |
| 889 | Do not mutate the host ecosystem by accident. | WordPress plugin best practices warn plugins share namespaces and must avoid collisions or unintended changes to other plugins. | [WordPress Plugin Best Practices](https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/plugin-basics/best-practices/) | 5 | 4 | Use unique namespaces and prefixes; change other plugins only through declared dependencies and documented hooks. |
| 890 | Sign every shipped desktop artifact. | Apple notarization and MSIX signing guidance make signatures, timestamps, and package identity part of desktop install and trust behavior. | [Apple - Notarizing macOS software before distribution](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/notarizing-macos-software-before-distribution) | 5 | 5 | CI verifies app, helper, installer, archive, timestamp, and notarization status before release. |
| 891 | Treat auto-updates as remote privileged code. | Desktop updaters fetch and execute new code, so signed metadata, transport security, rollback checks, and atomic verification are security boundaries. | [Sparkle - Publishing an Update](https://sparkle-project.org/documentation/publishing/) | 5 | 5 | Require signed manifests, HTTPS, anti-downgrade checks, separate channels and keys, and verify-before-install flow. |
| 892 | Preserve package identity across releases. | MSIX persistent identity guidance shows publisher, package name, and certificate identity affect whether users remain on the update path. | [Microsoft - MSIX Persistent Identity](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/msix/package/persistent-identity) | 5 | 5 | Build fails on identity or certificate changes unless migration and rollback artifacts are present. |
| 893 | Store desktop credentials in OS secret services. | Platform credential stores bind secrets to user and application identity more safely than app-owned config files or local databases. | [Apple Keychain Services](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/keychain-services) | 5 | 5 | Tokens are absent from prefs, JSON, SQLite, logs, and crash reports; stable service names use Keychain, Credential Locker, libsecret, or safeStorage. |
| 894 | Treat desktop entitlements as reviewed security policy. | Apple sandbox, Windows capabilities, and Tauri capabilities declare what local resources code may access. | [Apple App Sandbox](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/app-sandbox) | 5 | 5 | Capability diffs require review; broad filesystem, device, network, automation, and host exceptions need justification. |
| 895 | Use OS-mediated access flows where possible. | Flatpak portals provide user-mediated access to files, screens, printers, and URIs without broad host grants. | [Flatpak - Desktop Integration](https://docs.flatpak.org/en/latest/desktop-integration.html) | 5 | 4 | Prefer pickers, portals, security-scoped bookmarks, and per-resource grants over blanket home-directory or host-filesystem access. |
| 896 | Make desktop permission prompts deliberate and revocable. | Apple protected-resource and Electron security guidance treat camera, mic, location, notifications, and similar access as explicit user decisions. | [Electron Security](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/security) | 5 | 5 | Usage strings exist; prompts follow user intent; denial and revocation paths work; Electron permissions default to deny. |
| 897 | Validate every desktop IPC caller. | Electron and macOS IPC boundaries can expose privileged helpers to untrusted renderers or clients unless caller identity and payloads are checked. | [Electron - Context Isolation](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/context-isolation) | 5 | 5 | Expose narrow APIs, validate sender or client identity, schema-check payloads, and avoid raw privileged bridge exposure. |
| 898 | Treat file associations and deep links as hostile input. | OS file associations, MIME handlers, custom protocols, and open-file events move external payloads directly into app code. | [Windows ProgIDs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/shell/fa-progids) | 5 | 4 | Register handlers declaratively, listen early, canonicalize paths, parse URLs strictly, and reject command injection or remote payloads. |
| 899 | Make crash reporting privacy-controlled. | Crash dumps and metadata can contain sensitive process state, so reporters need initialization, symbolication, rate limits, and user control. | [Electron crashReporter](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/crash-reporter) | 5 | 4 | Start early, whitelist metadata, preserve symbols, honor upload settings, and keep system crash handling intact. |
| 900 | Test installers and uninstallers as product workflows. | Native package systems control install, update, repair, rollback, cleanup, trust prompts, services, and startup entries. | [Microsoft - MSIX overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/msix/overview) | 5 | 5 | Smoke-test fresh install, upgrade, repair, uninstall, and reinstall in clean VMs without orphaned privileged state. |
| 901 | Ship platform-specific desktop release matrices. | macOS, Windows, and Linux package formats differ in signing, metadata, sandboxing, update rules, and store expectations. | [Tauri - Distribute](https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/) | 5 | 4 | CI validates each target format, icon set, MIME metadata, AppStream data, entitlements, signatures, and updater channel. |
| 902 | Separate simulation time from presentation cadence. | Godot distinguishes fixed physics processing from variable idle processing because simulation correctness and rendering smoothness have different clocks. | [Godot - Idle and Physics Processing](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/scripting/idle_and_physics_processing.html) | 5 | 5 | Fixed-step logic owns simulation state; rendering interpolates or presents without changing authoritative state. |
| 903 | Bound catch-up work after stalls. | Unity time guidance warns that frame stalls can trigger excessive fixed-step catch-up unless maximum work is bounded. | [Unity - Handling variation in time](https://docs.unity3d.com/Manual/time-handling-variations.html) | 5 | 5 | Set max fixed timestep or catch-up budgets and choose degrade, skip, or slow-motion behavior deliberately. |
| 904 | Measure frame time, not average FPS. | Unity profiling guidance favors frame-time measurements because averages hide spikes that users experience as jank. | [Unity - Collecting performance data](https://docs.unity3d.com/Manual/profiler-profiling-applications.html) | 5 | 5 | Track percentiles, worst frames, main-thread time, GPU time, stalls, and workload labels. |
| 905 | Make runtime authority explicit. | Multiplayer runtime docs define object authority because conflicting writers create divergence and cheating risk. | [Unity Netcode - Authority](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.netcode.gameobjects@2.6/manual/terms-concepts/authority.html) | 5 | 5 | Every replicated object or state field has an owner, writer policy, validation rule, and conflict behavior. |
| 906 | Speculate only with reconciliation state. | Unreal networked physics supports prediction and resimulation by keeping enough history to reconcile client guesses with authoritative outcomes. | [Unreal Engine - Networked Physics Overview](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/networked-physics-overview) | 5 | 5 | Store input history, state snapshots, sequence numbers, and correction paths for predicted actions. |
| 907 | Judge latency-sensitive events in the actor's observed timeline. | Lag-compensation designs evaluate actions against what the actor could see when the input was made, not only the server's present. | [Gabriel Gambetta - Lag Compensation](https://www.gabrielgambetta.com/lag-compensation.html) | 4 | 4 | Keep bounded history, timestamps, anti-abuse limits, and clear rules for rewinding or rejecting events. |
| 908 | Interpolate presentation, not truth. | Godot physics interpolation smooths rendering between physics ticks while preserving the fixed simulation timeline. | [Godot - Physics Interpolation](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/physics/interpolation/physics_interpolation_introduction.html) | 5 | 4 | Presentation buffers and smoothing code do not mutate authoritative simulation state or persisted facts. |
| 909 | Send intent when replicated state fan-out explodes. | The Age of Empires networking article shows that deterministic commands can scale better than broadcasting every state change. | [1500 Archers on a 28.8](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/programming/1500-archers-on-a-28-8-network-programming-in-age-of-empires-and-beyond) | 5 | 4 | Prefer compact commands or intents when deterministic simulation and validation make state replication unnecessary. |
| 910 | Rollback needs cheap snapshots and quarantined side effects. | GGPO-style rollback depends on restoring past state and replaying inputs without duplicating irreversible effects. | [GGPO](https://www.ggpo.net/) | 4 | 4 | Snapshot deterministic state cheaply; delay, dedupe, or isolate audio, particles, telemetry, and external writes. |
| 911 | Pick one master clock per media pipeline. | Audio-video synchronization guidance depends on one clock being authoritative for playback pacing and drift correction. | [Amazon Fire TV - Audio Video Synchronization](https://developer.amazon.com/docs/fire-tv/audio-video-synchronization.html) | 4 | 5 | Declare whether audio, video, wall clock, or stream timestamps drive playback and correction. |
| 912 | Treat latency buffers as explicit contracts. | GStreamer's jitter buffer exposes latency, packet loss, retransmission, and timestamp behavior as configurable pipeline semantics. | [GStreamer rtpjitterbuffer](https://gstreamer.freedesktop.org/documentation/rtpmanager/rtpjitterbuffer.html) | 5 | 4 | Set buffer size, late-packet policy, retransmission behavior, and user-visible latency budgets deliberately. |
| 913 | Version assets and save data as migratable protocols. | Unreal asset and package versioning treats serialized content as durable data that must be interpreted across engine and application versions. | [Unreal Engine - Versioning Assets and Packages](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/versioning-of-assets-and-packages-in-unreal-engine) | 5 | 5 | Persist format IDs, migration code, compatibility tests, and old fixture saves or assets. |
| 914 | Coordinates need CRS, frame, and epoch. | OGC referencing guidance treats coordinates as meaningful only within a coordinate reference system and related metadata. | [OGC Abstract Specification Topic 2](https://docs.ogc.org/as/18-005r8/18-005r8.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Store CRS, axis order, datum, frame, epoch when relevant, and transformation path with coordinate data. |
| 915 | Axis order is data, not taste. | GeoJSON fixes positions as longitude then latitude, while other coordinate systems may define different axis order. | [RFC 7946 - GeoJSON](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7946) | 5 | 5 | Name axes explicitly and test boundary cases so lat/lon swaps cannot silently pass. |
| 916 | SRID assignment is not coordinate transformation. | PostGIS distinguishes setting an SRID label from transforming coordinates into a different spatial reference system. | [PostGIS ST_Transform](https://postgis.net/docs/ST_Transform.html) | 5 | 5 | Use SetSRID only for known unlabeled data and Transform only when coordinates must change CRS. |
| 917 | Never measure longitude and latitude with flat math. | PostGIS distance docs distinguish planar geometry calculations from spheroidal geography calculations. | [PostGIS ST_Distance](https://postgis.net/docs/ST_Distance.html) | 5 | 5 | Pick geodesic or projected calculations based on distance, area, accuracy, and region requirements. |
| 918 | Separate display projection from analysis projection. | EPSG:3857 is useful for web maps but explicitly has distortion that makes it unsuitable for many measurements. | [EPSG:3857](https://epsg.io/3857) | 4 | 5 | Render in map projection if needed, but compute distance, area, and buffering in an appropriate CRS. |
| 919 | Choose transformations by area and accuracy. | PROJ exposes candidate coordinate operations, areas of use, and accuracy metadata because transformations are not interchangeable. | [PROJ - Functions](https://proj.org/en/stable/development/reference/functions.html) | 5 | 5 | Select transformations by CRS pair, area of use, available grids, epoch, and required accuracy. |
| 920 | Treat location as an estimate. | Android geofencing guidance includes radius and behavior constraints because device location is uncertain and delayed. | [Android - Geofencing](https://developer.android.com/develop/sensors-and-location/location/geofencing) | 4 | 5 | Store accuracy, freshness, source, confidence, and threshold behavior instead of pretending there is an exact point. |
| 921 | Spatial indexes produce candidates, not proof. | PostGIS spatial-index guidance uses indexes to reduce candidate rows before exact spatial predicates confirm truth. | [PostGIS - Spatial Indexes](https://postgis.net/docs/using_postgis_dbmanagement.html) | 5 | 4 | Pair bounding-box index filters with exact geometry predicates and regression tests for false positives. |
| 922 | Antimeridian and poles are normal inputs. | GeoJSON includes antimeridian cutting guidance because valid geometries can cross longitude wrap boundaries. | [RFC 7946 - Antimeridian Cutting](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7946) | 5 | 4 | Test wraparound, pole proximity, world-spanning boxes, and shortest-path assumptions. |
| 923 | Own coordinate frames instead of guessing them. | ROS REP 105 defines standard frames and relationships because robotics code fails when frame meaning is implicit. | [REP 105 - Coordinate Frames](https://reps.openrobotics.org/rep-0105/) | 4 | 4 | Name frame owners, transforms, lifetimes, and authority for every sensor, actuator, map, and body frame. |
| 924 | Routes live on graphs, not lines. | OSRM APIs operate on road-network graphs with waypoints, bearings, restrictions, and alternatives rather than raw geometric straight lines. | [OSRM API](https://project-osrm.org/docs/v5.23.0/api/) | 4 | 4 | Use graph routing for travel paths, turn restrictions, modes, and costs; use geometry only for display or spatial filtering. |
| 925 | Reruns must be deterministic transactions. | Airflow best practices expect tasks and DAGs to be idempotent and partitioned so retries and backfills do not corrupt results. | [Airflow - Best Practices](https://airflow.apache.org/docs/apache-airflow/stable/best-practices.html) | 5 | 5 | Write outputs by partition or run ID, avoid hidden now-values, and make retries replace or verify the same target. |
| 926 | Declare event time before aggregation logic. | Beam and the Dataflow model treat event time, processing time, windows, watermarks, and triggers as separate concepts. | [Apache Beam Programming Guide](https://beam.apache.org/documentation/programming-guide/) | 5 | 5 | Aggregations define event-time source, windowing, allowed lateness, trigger, and accumulation behavior. |
| 927 | Late data needs a correction path. | Flink streaming analytics guidance treats out-of-order and late events as expected in real streams. | [Flink - Streaming Analytics](https://nightlies.apache.org/flink/flink-docs-stable/docs/learn-flink/streaming_analytics/) | 5 | 5 | Decide whether late data updates results, emits retractions, goes to quarantine, or is rejected with evidence. |
| 928 | Prove exactly-once across source, state, and sink. | Structured-streaming and Delta guidance scope exactly-once claims to the combination of input replay, checkpointed state, and idempotent or transactional output. | [Spark Structured Streaming Programming Guide](https://spark.apache.org/docs/3.5.6/structured-streaming-programming-guide.html) | 5 | 5 | Document the boundary and failure cases; external side effects need independent idempotency. |
| 929 | Use checkpoints for failure and savepoints for evolution. | Flink distinguishes automatic checkpoints for recovery from savepoints used for controlled upgrades and state evolution. | [Flink - Checkpointing](https://nightlies.apache.org/flink/flink-docs-stable/docs/dev/datastream/fault-tolerance/checkpointing/) | 5 | 4 | Automate checkpoint health separately from upgrade savepoint creation, validation, and rollback. |
| 930 | Freshness is a gate, not a dashboard. | dbt source freshness turns stale upstream data into explicit deploy or job evidence instead of passive monitoring. | [dbt - Source Freshness](https://docs.getdbt.com/docs/deploy/source-freshness) | 5 | 4 | Block or quarantine dependent jobs when source age exceeds declared thresholds. |
| 931 | Write, audit, then publish. | Iceberg branching and dbt tests support validating written data before exposing it to consumers. | [AWS - Write-Audit-Publish with Apache Iceberg](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/big-data/build-write-audit-publish-pattern-with-apache-iceberg-branching-and-aws-glue-data-quality/) | 4 | 5 | Land data in staging or branch, run quality checks, then promote atomically with audit evidence. |
| 932 | Schema drift must choose reject, evolve, or quarantine. | Delta Lake and Iceberg guidance make schema enforcement and schema evolution explicit choices. | [Delta Lake - Schema Enforcement](https://delta.io/blog/2022-11-16-delta-lake-schema-enforcement/) | 4 | 5 | Unknown fields, type changes, and missing columns follow a declared policy with alerts and tests. |
| 933 | Backfills use the production code path. | Airflow backfill and data-engineering guidance treat backfills as reruns of known logic, not one-off manual transformations. | [Airflow - Backfill](https://airflow.apache.org/docs/apache-airflow/stable/core-concepts/backfill.html) | 5 | 5 | Run backfills through versioned jobs with throttles, dry runs, progress, ownership, and validation. |
| 934 | Pin replay to immutable data versions. | Table history and Iceberg branches or tags allow replay against exact snapshots instead of whatever data exists today. | [Databricks - Table History](https://docs.databricks.com/gcp/en/tables/history) | 5 | 4 | Store table version, snapshot ID, code revision, and parameter set for reproducible replay. |
| 935 | Partitioning is physical, not business logic. | Iceberg partitioning supports hidden partition transforms so consumers do not depend on storage layout. | [Apache Iceberg - Partitioning](https://iceberg.apache.org/docs/latest/partitioning/) | 5 | 4 | Keep query semantics independent of partition columns and let layout evolve without breaking consumers. |
| 936 | Prefer changed ranges over fixed lookbacks. | Incremental-processing guidance favors processing known changed data instead of rereading arbitrary windows that miss late corrections or waste work. | [Netflix - Incremental Processing Using Maestro and Iceberg](https://netflixtechblog.com/incremental-processing-using-netflix-maestro-and-apache-iceberg-b8ba072ddeeb) | 4 | 4 | Track changed partitions, watermarks, manifests, and invalidation ranges rather than hardcoded lookback days. |
| 937 | Keep data resident on the accelerator. | CUDA best practices emphasize minimizing host-device transfer overhead because movement can dominate computation. | [NVIDIA CUDA C++ Best Practices Guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html) | 5 | 5 | Avoid repeated `.cpu()`, `.numpy()`, `.item()`, and tiny copies in hot paths; batch transfers and compute near data. |
| 938 | Synchronize deliberately. | PyTorch and JAX docs note GPU execution is asynchronous, so timing, error surfacing, and dependencies require explicit synchronization. | [PyTorch - CUDA semantics](https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/notes/cuda.html) | 5 | 5 | Benchmarks use events or blocking; stream dependencies are explicit; accidental sync points are profiled. |
| 939 | Check kernel launches and deferred failures. | CUDA runtime errors may surface at launch or later synchronization boundaries, so both sites need handling. | [CUDA Runtime API - Error Handling](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__ERROR.html) | 5 | 5 | Check launch status, synchronize at defined boundaries, and attach operation context to failures. |
| 940 | Determinism is a runtime mode, not a seed. | PyTorch reproducibility guidance requires deterministic algorithms and environment controls beyond setting random seeds. | [PyTorch - Reproducibility](https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/notes/randomness.html) | 5 | 5 | Capture library versions, device, flags, kernels, seeds, and known nondeterministic operations. |
| 941 | Do not demand bitwise floating equality from unordered parallel math. | NVIDIA floating-point guidance shows parallel operation order changes rounding and therefore exact results. | [NVIDIA - Floating Point and IEEE 754](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/floating-point/index.html) | 5 | 5 | Use tolerances or fixed reduction order when reproducibility is required. |
| 942 | Make accelerator precision policy explicit. | PyTorch CUDA settings expose TF32 and precision controls that can change speed and numerical results. | [PyTorch - CUDA semantics](https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/notes/cuda.html) | 5 | 4 | Declare FP32, TF32, BF16, FP16, accumulation precision, and acceptable error budgets by workload. |
| 943 | Use mixed-precision policy, not manual half-casting. | PyTorch AMP and TensorFlow mixed-precision guides manage autocast, scaling, and numerically sensitive operations as a policy. | [PyTorch - Automatic Mixed Precision](https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/amp.html) | 5 | 4 | Centralize autocast and scaling; keep denylisted operations in safer precision; test overflow and underflow. |
| 944 | Collectives must match across ranks. | NCCL and MPI correctness guidance require participating ranks to call compatible collectives in compatible order. | [NCCL - Collective Operations](https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/nccl/user-guide/docs/usage/collectives.html) | 5 | 5 | Assert rank groups, operation order, tensor shapes, dtypes, counts, and timeouts before entering distributed collectives. |
| 945 | Treat distributed progress failure as fatal until rebuilt. | NCCL communicator guidance and collective-deadlock research show one rank failure can poison collective progress for the group. | [NCCL - Communicators](https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/nccl/user-guide/docs/usage/communicators.html) | 5 | 5 | Abort, tear down, and reconstruct communicators or jobs after timeout, hang, or async error. |
| 946 | Checkpoint distributed state as distributed state. | PyTorch distributed checkpointing handles model, optimizer, and sharded state that cannot be recovered from a single local file. | [PyTorch - Distributed Checkpoint](https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/distributed.checkpoint.html) | 5 | 5 | Save model shards, optimizer state, scheduler state, RNG state, rank mapping, and code version together. |
| 947 | Budget accelerator memory before runtime allocation. | JAX and TensorFlow GPU docs expose preallocation and memory-growth behavior that can starve other processes or fail late. | [JAX - GPU memory allocation](https://docs.jax.dev/en/latest/gpu_memory_allocation.html) | 5 | 4 | Configure memory fraction or growth, monitor fragmentation, and define batch-size fallback before OOM. |
| 948 | Detect hardware capabilities and fall back loudly. | JAX configuration and CUDA compute-capability docs show that backend, device, and architecture features vary by environment. | [CUDA - Compute Capabilities](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/05-appendices/compute-capabilities.html) | 5 | 4 | Probe devices, drivers, kernels, precision support, and backend selection; fail with actionable messages or explicit fallback. |
| 949 | Append-correct regulated records instead of overwriting. | 21 CFR Part 11 requires record changes not to obscure previously recorded information. | [21 CFR Part 11](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-21/chapter-I/subchapter-A/part-11) | 5 | 5 | Corrections create new entries with actor, time, reason, and link to the original record. |
| 950 | Bind signatures and approvals to exact record and meaning. | 21 CFR Part 11 requires electronic signatures to be linked to their records and indicate signer, date, and meaning. | [21 CFR Part 11](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-21/chapter-I/subchapter-A/part-11) | 5 | 5 | Approval records include artifact digest, version, signer identity, timestamp, role, reason, and intent. |
| 951 | Model consent and authorization as scoped data. | HIPAA authorization rules distinguish who may disclose, what may be disclosed, to whom, purpose, expiry, and revocation. | [45 CFR 164.508](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-45/subtitle-A/subchapter-C/part-164/subpart-E/section-164.508) | 5 | 5 | Store scope, subject, recipient, purpose, expiry, revocation, and policy version; avoid plain consent booleans. |
| 952 | Every regulated action needs a unique accountable actor. | HIPAA technical safeguards require unique user identification for tracking system activity. | [45 CFR 164.312](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-45/subtitle-A/subchapter-C/part-164/subpart-C/section-164.312) | 5 | 5 | Avoid shared accounts; record human, service, delegation, and session identity for high-impact actions. |
| 953 | Enforce workflow sequence and authority in code. | 21 CFR Part 11 requires authority checks and operational-system checks to enforce permitted sequencing of steps and events. | [21 CFR Part 11](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-21/chapter-I/subchapter-A/part-11) | 5 | 5 | State machines enforce draft, review, approval, release, correction, and retirement transitions. |
| 954 | Separate maker, checker, deployer, and evidence roles. | NIST SP 800-53 separation-of-duties controls reduce conflicts where one actor can create, approve, deploy, and preserve evidence alone. | [NIST SP 800-53 Rev. 5](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/53/r5/upd1/final) | 5 | 5 | Define role conflicts, reviewer independence, break-glass rules, and exception evidence. |
| 955 | Ship change evidence with the change. | NIST configuration-management controls tie changes to analysis, approval, implementation, testing, and documentation evidence. | [NIST SP 800-53 Rev. 5](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/53/r5/upd1/final) | 5 | 5 | Release records include ticket, risk analysis, approvals, tests, deployment plan, rollback plan, and artifact identity. |
| 956 | Protect audit evidence from the system it audits. | NIST audit controls require protecting audit information from unauthorized access, modification, and deletion. | [NIST SP 800-53 Rev. 5 derived OSCAL](https://csrc.nist.gov/CSRC/media/Projects/risk-management/800-53%20Downloads/800-53r5/SP_800-53_v5_1-derived-OSCAL.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Send audit events to append-only or separately controlled stores with integrity checks and independent access control. |
| 957 | Monitor authorized-user misuse. | FTC Safeguards Rule monitoring requirements include authorized-user activity, not only outsider attacks. | [16 CFR 314.4](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-16/chapter-I/subchapter-C/part-314/section-314.4) | 5 | 5 | Alert on unusual exports, privilege changes, access patterns, bulk reads, and policy bypasses by legitimate accounts. |
| 958 | Make evidence self-contained enough for outsider review. | PCAOB audit-documentation standards require experienced reviewers to understand work performed, evidence obtained, and conclusions reached. | [PCAOB AS 1215](https://pcaobus.org/oversight/standards/auditing-standards/details/AS1215) | 5 | 4 | Evidence records include objective, scope, procedure, inputs, result, reviewer, date, and unresolved exceptions. |
| 959 | Continuously test controls after change. | FTC Safeguards Rule and the Equifax report show controls must be assessed and adjusted when operations, threats, or systems change. | [16 CFR 314.4](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-16/chapter-I/subchapter-C/part-314/section-314.4) | 5 | 5 | Re-run control tests after releases, dependency changes, policy changes, incidents, and architecture changes. |
| 960 | Treat SDK compatibility as its own contract. | SDK source compatibility, generated types, runtime support, and pinned service API versions can break clients independently of wire compatibility. | [Stripe - SDK Versioning and Support Policy](https://docs.stripe.com/sdks/versioning) | 5 | 5 | Public SDK API diffs gate releases; SDK manifests declare service API version, language runtime support, and migration path. |
| 961 | Keep stdout for data and stderr for diagnostics. | CLI pipelines and command substitution depend on stdout being clean machine-consumable output while diagnostics, progress, prompts, and logs stay separate. | [Command Line Interface Guidelines](https://clig.dev/) | 4 | 5 | Stream tests capture stdout and stderr separately; JSON mode writes only JSON to stdout. |
| 962 | Treat exit status as a public API. | Shell automation branches on process status, so success, usage errors, runtime failures, cancellation, and no-op outcomes need stable meanings. | [Bash Reference Manual - Exit Status](https://www.gnu.org/software/bash/manual/html_node/Exit-Status.html) | 5 | 5 | Contract tests cover success, usage error, runtime error, cancellation, and meaningful no-change states. |
| 963 | Honor end-of-options grammar. | POSIX and GNU-style command lines rely on `--` so operands beginning with dashes can be passed safely. | [POSIX Utility Conventions](https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9699919799/basedefs/V1_chap12.html) | 5 | 5 | Parser tests cover `cmd -- -file`, grouped short flags, unknown flags, and missing option arguments. |
| 964 | Use NUL-delimited path interfaces for filenames. | Filenames can contain whitespace, newlines, glob characters, and leading dashes, so line-oriented human listings are unsafe as path protocols. | [GNU Findutils xargs options](https://www.gnu.org/software/findutils/manual/html_node/find_html/xargs-options.html) | 5 | 5 | Fixtures include newline, tab, space, and leading-dash paths; tooling supports `-z` or equivalent. |
| 965 | Pass subprocess arguments as argv arrays. | Shell command strings introduce quoting, globbing, injection, and platform parsing bugs when shell semantics are not required. | [Python subprocess](https://docs.python.org/3/library/subprocess.html) | 5 | 5 | Linters ban shell-string execution by default; metacharacter tests prove no unintended execution. |
| 966 | Make terminal decoration conditional on the actual output device. | Color, pagers, prompts, spinners, and progress bars make logs and pipes nondeterministic when emitted outside an interactive TTY. | [Command Line Interface Guidelines](https://clig.dev/) | 4 | 4 | PTY and non-PTY tests verify color, pager, prompt, and progress behavior; `NO_COLOR` is honored. |
| 967 | Preserve signal and cancellation semantics. | Ctrl-C and termination signals should stop work promptly, clean up boundedly, and not report success. | [Command Line Interface Guidelines](https://clig.dev/) | 4 | 4 | SIGINT and SIGTERM tests ensure children are terminated or reaped and cancellation exits nonzero. |
| 968 | Make configuration precedence explicit and inspectable. | Hidden interactions among flags, environment variables, project files, user config, and system config make tools unpredictable. | [Command Line Interface Guidelines - Configuration](https://clig.dev/) | 4 | 4 | Precedence is tested and documented; `--show-config` reports effective values and their sources. |
| 969 | Keep help and version commands side-effect-free. | Discovery commands should be safe in scripts, diagnostics, package probes, and support workflows. | [GNU Coding Standards - Command-Line Interfaces](https://www.gnu.org/prep/standards/html_node/Command_002dLine-Interfaces.html) | 5 | 4 | `--help` and `--version` exit 0, write only to stdout, and perform no network, mutation, or expensive initialization. |
| 970 | Treat internally generated config as hostile input. | Cloudflare's 2025 outage showed that trusted config generation can still produce invalid or overlarge runtime data after upstream permission and query changes. | [Cloudflare outage on November 18, 2025](https://blog.cloudflare.com/18-november-2025-outage/) | 5 | 5 | Generated config has schema, cardinality, size, and semantic validators plus last-known-good rollback. |
| 971 | Include valid customer config in the failure matrix. | Fastly's 2021 outage was triggered by a valid customer configuration that activated a latent software bug. | [Fastly - Summary of June 8 outage](https://www.fastly.com/blog/summary-of-june-8-outage) | 5 | 4 | Config fuzzing, representative tenant fixtures, per-tenant canaries, and rollback cover customer-controlled config paths. |
| 972 | Encode data-topology invariants in failover automation. | GitHub's 2018 incident showed that partition-driven database failover can preserve data only when topology and reconciliation rules are explicit. | [GitHub - October 21 post-incident analysis](https://github.blog/news-insights/company-news/oct21-post-incident-analysis/) | 5 | 5 | Failover tests cover fencing, topology policy, partition simulation, write reconciliation, and data-integrity-first modes. |
| 973 | Fail closed on operational command validators. | Meta's 2021 outage passed a dangerous backbone command through a buggy audit system, then lost normal DNS and access paths. | [Meta - October 4 outage details](https://engineering.fb.com/2021/10/05/networking-traffic/outage-details/) | 5 | 5 | Command validators have independent preflight simulation, fail-closed behavior, bad-command drills, and out-of-band recovery. |
| 974 | Maintenance automation must preserve control-plane quorum. | Google Cloud's 2019 networking incident involved maintenance and software bugs taking too many critical control-plane jobs out of service. | [Google Cloud Status - Networking Incident 19009](https://status.cloud.google.com/incident/cloud-networking/19009) | 5 | 5 | Critical controllers have anti-affinity, max-eviction budgets, eligibility validators, and regional drain tests. |
| 975 | Budget observability agents as production clients. | OpenAI's 2025 incident showed telemetry agents can overload Kubernetes control planes when rollout scale exceeds staging assumptions. | [OpenAI Status - API, ChatGPT and Sora facing issues](https://status.openai.com/incidents/ctrsv3lwd797) | 5 | 5 | Observability agents have API QPS budgets, large-cluster load tests, rollout gates, and control-plane health limits. |
| 976 | Block scale-in on latency, errors, and backlog. | Slack's 2021 outage showed low CPU during dependency latency can be a saturation signal, not spare capacity. | [Slack - Outage on January 4th 2021](https://slack.engineering/slacks-outage-on-january-4th-2021/) | 5 | 4 | Autoscaling scale-in considers latency, errors, queue depth, in-flight work, dependency health, and incident pinning. |
| 977 | Property-test calendar and certificate edge dates. | Azure's 2012 leap-day outage generated invalid certificate expiry dates and automated recovery reproduced the bad state. | [Microsoft Azure - Feb 29 2012 service disruption summary](https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/summary-of-windows-azure-service-disruption-on-feb-29th-2012/) | 5 | 4 | Tests cover leap days, year boundaries, DST, clock skew, certificate validity windows, and auto-healing cascades. |
| 978 | Test recovery paths in recovery mode. | Tarsnap outage reports show reconstruction and recovery-throughput paths can fail differently from normal service paths. | [Tarsnap 2023 outage post-mortem](https://mail.tarsnap.com/tarsnap-announce/msg00050.html) | 5 | 4 | Disaster drills exercise full reconstruction, read-only validation, recovery flags, historical edge cases, and throughput budgets. |
| 979 | Enable SQLite foreign keys on every connection. | SQLite documents that foreign-key enforcement must be enabled per connection, so schema declarations alone do not prove relational integrity. | [SQLite - Foreign Key Support](https://sqlite.org/foreignkeys.html) | 5 | 5 | Connection setup asserts `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`; migrations and tests run `PRAGMA foreign_key_check`. |
| 980 | Treat isolation-level names as engine-specific claims. | ANSI isolation names do not fully capture real database behavior, and Jepsen analyses show vendor and topology details can violate assumptions. | [A Critique of ANSI SQL Isolation Levels](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2016/02/tr-95-51.pdf?from_theconsensus=1) | 5 | 5 | Critical invariants document required anomalies to prevent, engine behavior, lock strategy, and whole-transaction retry policy. |
| 981 | Declare DDL transaction and lock behavior in migrations. | Database and migration-tool docs show DDL can be transactional, non-transactional, implicit-commit, blocking, or partially recoverable depending on engine. | [Flyway - Migration Transaction Handling](https://documentation.red-gate.com/fd/migration-transaction-handling-273973399.html) | 5 | 5 | Each migration declares transactional behavior, lock impact, repair plan, rollback or restore plan, and large-table strategy. |
| 982 | Carry explicit bounds with every raw buffer. | CERT and CWE guidance tie out-of-bounds pointer arithmetic, reads, and writes to undefined behavior and exploitable memory corruption. | [SEI CERT ARR30-C](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/rules/arrays-arr/arr30-c/) | 5 | 5 | Raw pointers, slices, offsets, and memcpy-like calls have length, lower-bound, and upper-bound proofs at the same boundary. |
| 983 | Compute allocation sizes from the destination type with checked arithmetic. | CERT integer and memory rules warn that overflow, truncation, and `sizeof(pointer)` mistakes create undersized allocations and later overflows. | [SEI CERT MEM35-C](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-c-coding-standard/rules/memory-management-mem/mem35-c/) | 5 | 5 | Allocation reviews flag `n * size`, narrowing casts, `sizeof(ptr)`, and copy sizes not derived from destination element type. |
| 984 | Treat protocol length fields as attacker claims. | Heartbleed and curl CVE-2023-38545 both show trusted length or state crossing a buffer boundary can expose memory or overflow buffers. | [OpenSSL Security Advisory 2014-04-07](https://openssl-library.org/news/secadv/20140407.txt) | 5 | 5 | Parsers verify claimed length against remaining input, destination capacity, maximum frame size, and state-machine constraints before copying. |
| 985 | Use stable ABI and layout types at FFI boundaries. | Rust FFI and layout docs make clear that calling convention, representation, alignment, and ownership are not inferred safely across languages. | [Rustonomicon - FFI](https://doc.rust-lang.org/nomicon/ffi.html) | 5 | 5 | FFI signatures use `repr(C)` or generated bindings, stable ABI types, explicit string conventions, and layout tests. |
| 986 | Do not unwind across FFI boundaries by accident. | Language references warn that panics, exceptions, and long jumps crossing unsupported ABIs can bypass destructors or cause undefined behavior. | [Rust Reference - Behavior considered undefined](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/behavior-considered-undefined.html) | 5 | 4 | Boundary wrappers catch panics or exceptions, translate to the declared error channel, and document any unwind-capable ABI. |
| 987 | Keep managed-runtime pointers inside their pin or borrow scope. | Go cgo and Swift unsafe-pointer docs restrict storing managed pointers in native code because runtimes may move, reclaim, or invalidate objects. | [Go cmd/cgo - Passing pointers](https://pkg.go.dev/cmd/cgo) | 5 | 5 | Native callbacks use handles, copies, or pinned scopes; tests and review reject stored Go, Swift, or managed-runtime pointers. |
| 988 | Prove raw-memory alignment, initialization, validity, and aliasing before typed access. | Rust and CERT guidance treat misaligned access, invalid values, incompatible pointer casts, and aliasing violations as undefined behavior. | [Rust Reference - Behavior considered undefined](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/behavior-considered-undefined.html) | 5 | 5 | `transmute`, `reinterpret_cast`, packed-field reads, byte-to-struct casts, and type punning require local proof and tests. |
| 989 | Replace "clean" with concrete engineering claims. | Practitioner and community critiques of Clean Code argue that subjective labels hide the actual issue: correctness, understandability, testability, performance, or change cost. | [qntm - Clean Code](https://qntm.org/clean) | 5 | 5 | Review comments state the specific failure mode or tradeoff instead of relying on "clean", "SOLID", or taste labels alone. |
| 990 | Choose boring technology unless novelty earns its cost. | Repeated practitioner guidance favors mature tools and explicit code because novelty creates operational unknowns and team cognitive load. | [Choose Boring Technology](https://mcfunley.com/choose-boring-technology) | 5 | 5 | New dependencies, languages, frameworks, or patterns require a payoff, owner, maturity check, and failure-mode story. |
| 991 | Make code easy to delete. | Deletion-friendly code lowers maintenance cost by isolating feature logic, vendor boundaries, and experiments instead of generalizing them too early. | [Write code that is easy to delete](https://programmingisterrible.com/post/139222674273/write-code-that-is-easy-to-delete-not-easy-to) | 5 | 4 | Feature code has few callers, limited shared abstractions, no unused extension hooks, and clear ownership. |
| 992 | Debug by reproducing and minimizing before theorizing. | Debugging guidance from practitioners and Stack Overflow emphasizes actual reproduction, smallest failing cases, and checked assumptions over speculation. | [Eric Lippert - How to debug small programs](https://ericlippert.com/2014/03/05/how-to-debug-small-programs/) | 5 | 5 | Bug reports include reproduction steps, expected and actual behavior, minimized case, and last verified assumption. |
| 993 | Treat hard-to-name code as a design signal. | Practitioner and community naming guidance repeatedly treats vague names as evidence that responsibility or domain concepts are unclear. | [Carl Alexander - The importance of naming in programming](https://carlalexander.ca/importance-naming-programming/) | 4 | 5 | Names like `helper`, `manager`, `data`, `utils`, and `thing` trigger responsibility or boundary review. |
| 994 | Label review feedback by severity and intent. | Code-review guidance argues that teams should distinguish blocking defects from suggestions, nits, questions, and style preferences. | [Stack Overflow Blog - How to Make Good Code Reviews Better](https://stackoverflow.blog/2019/09/30/how-to-make-good-code-reviews-better/) | 4 | 5 | Review comments use labels such as blocking, suggestion, nit, question, and non-blocking; only blocking items hold merge. |
| 995 | Treat compilers, SDKs, and generators as declared toolchains. | Build correctness should not depend on whatever compiler, SDK, or generator happens to be installed on a developer machine or CI image. | [Bazel - Toolchains](https://bazel.build/extending/toolchains) | 5 | 5 | Toolchain definitions and resolvers exist; CI does not install ad hoc compilers before build steps. |
| 996 | Build caches must never change correctness. | SLSA build requirements treat caches as safe only when they cannot make a build output differ from an uncached build or cross trust boundaries. | [SLSA v1.2 Build Requirements](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/build-requirements) | 5 | 5 | Cache keys include source, config, and toolchain; untrusted PRs cannot write trusted caches; cache-off CI passes. |
| 997 | Drive monorepo CI from the dependency graph. | Build graph queries capture reverse dependencies and generated relationships that path globs often miss. | [Bazel Query Reference](https://bazel.build/query/language) | 5 | 5 | CI selects build and test targets using dependency queries or affected-target logic, not hand-maintained path lists alone. |
| 998 | Enforce module visibility at the build layer. | Build-system visibility rules prevent implementation reach-ins and protect public interfaces as repositories grow. | [Bazel - Visibility](https://bazel.build/concepts/visibility) | 5 | 5 | Targets are private by default, visibility groups are limited, and blanket public visibility requires justification. |
| 999 | Avoid disconnected recursive builds. | Recursive build structures hide dependencies, force manual ordering, and rebuild too much or too little. | [Recursive Make Considered Harmful](https://accu.org/journals/overload/14/71/miller_2004/) | 4 | 4 | The top-level build owns dependency edges; shell loops over subdirectories are not the primary build graph. |
| 1000 | Ship minimal runtime containers. | Docker multi-stage builds separate build dependencies from runtime artifacts so production images do not carry compilers, caches, tests, or intermediates. | [Docker - Multi-stage builds](https://docs.docker.com/build/building/multi-stage/) | 5 | 5 | Final images copy only runtime artifacts, use `.dockerignore`, and omit build toolchains and secret-bearing caches. |
| 1001 | Keep secrets out of build definitions and artifacts. | Docker build-secret guidance warns that build arguments and environment variables can persist in layers, logs, or metadata. | [Docker - Build secrets](https://docs.docker.com/build/building/secrets/) | 5 | 5 | Use secret mounts or CI secret primitives; no token-bearing `ARG`, committed config, image layer, or artifact metadata. |
| 1002 | Prefer language idiom over imported style habits. | Official language guides optimize for ecosystem readability and call-site clarity, not generic style transplanted from another stack. | [Swift API Design Guidelines](https://www.swift.org/documentation/api-design-guidelines/) | 5 | 5 | Formatter and style config match the language ecosystem; reviews reject alien getter, factory, naming, or error idioms. |
| 1003 | Construct values fully before exposure. | Language initialization guidance favors complete valid values over mandatory follow-up setup and sentinel defaults. | [Swift Book - Initialization](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/swiftlang/swift-book/main/TSPL.docc/LanguageGuide/Initialization.md) | 5 | 5 | Objects are not usable before `init` calls, late field assignment, or required setter sequences complete. |
| 1004 | Do not hide fallible cleanup in destructors or finalizers. | Rust and C++ guidance warn that destructors, drops, and finalizers are poor places for important recoverable errors or blocking work. | [Rust API Guidelines - Dependability](https://rust-lang.github.io/api-guidelines/dependability.html) | 5 | 4 | Provide explicit close, flush, commit, or shutdown APIs where cleanup can fail or block. |
| 1005 | Keep async work structured, cancellable, and nonblocking. | Official async guidance warns against blocking async paths, swallowing cancellation, and leaking background work outside its owner. | [C# async scenarios](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/asynchronous-programming/async-scenarios) | 5 | 5 | Async work returns a handle/task, observes cancellation, avoids blocking waits, and has an owner lifecycle. |
| 1006 | Keep atomic replacement on one filesystem and one logical object. | POSIX rename is atomic for name replacement, but cross-filesystem or multi-file updates require journals, manifests, or transactions. | [rename(3p)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/rename.3p.html) | 5 | 5 | Code handles `EXDEV`; temp files live beside targets; multi-file updates use a manifest, journal, or database transaction. |
| 1007 | Claim names atomically instead of check-then-create. | Existence checks race with attackers and concurrent workers; creation and precondition primitives combine the check with the write. | [open(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/open.2.html) | 5 | 5 | Use `O_CREAT` with `O_EXCL`, `mkstemp`, `O_NOFOLLOW`, object-store preconditions, or equivalent atomic create APIs. |
| 1008 | Resolve untrusted paths under a root capability. | String cleanup alone cannot handle `..`, symlinks, device paths, drive-relative paths, and concurrent renames safely. | [Go - Traversal-resistant file APIs](https://go.dev/blog/osroot) | 4 | 5 | Central path helpers use root or dirfd-style APIs, canonicalize-and-contain checks, and symlink-swap tests. |
| 1009 | Treat symlinks and hardlinks as hostile file boundaries. | Link tricks can redirect reads or writes after validation or expose files through alternate names in public writable directories. | [Apple - Race Conditions and Secure File Operations](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Security/Conceptual/SecureCodingGuide/Articles/RaceConditions.html) | 4 | 5 | Open first then `fstat`; verify type, owner, mode, and link count; pass file descriptors rather than paths. |
| 1010 | Use file locks only for cooperative concurrency. | Unix file locks are advisory and differ across APIs and filesystems, so they are not security or permission boundaries. | [flock(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/flock.2.html) | 5 | 4 | Lock helpers document advisory scope; all writers participate; security does not depend on locks. |
| 1011 | Do not assume object stores have POSIX directories or rename. | S3 and cloud object-store moves commonly mean copy and delete, while atomic rename exists only for specific APIs or configurations. | [Amazon S3 - Copying, moving, and renaming objects](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/copy-object.html) | 5 | 5 | Correctness uses manifests, versioned keys, staging prefixes, and idempotent cleanup rather than folder rename assumptions. |
| 1012 | Use object-store preconditions for concurrent writes. | Strong read-after-write consistency does not make read-modify-write safe without ETags, generations, or conditional headers. | [Google Cloud Storage - Request preconditions](https://docs.cloud.google.com/storage/docs/request-preconditions) | 5 | 5 | Creates, updates, and deletes include `If-Match`, `If-None-Match`, generation-match, or equivalent checks and handle precondition failures. |
| 1013 | Set embedded-database durability modes deliberately. | SQLite durability pragmas trade performance for crash and power-loss guarantees, so defaults must match the acknowledged-write contract. | [SQLite PRAGMA synchronous](https://sqlite.org/pragma.html) | 5 | 5 | Applications set and test `journal_mode` and `synchronous` rather than inheriting ambient defaults. |
| 1014 | Define portable filename and object-key policy. | Windows, POSIX, and object stores differ on reserved names, path forms, length limits, case behavior, slashes, and trailing dots. | [Microsoft - File path formats on Windows systems](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/standard/io/file-path-formats) | 5 | 4 | Validators cover reserved names, drive-relative paths, device paths, byte and segment limits, case, and trailing dot or slash behavior. |
| 1015 | Encode stable laws as property tests. | Property-based testing checks invariants across broad input spaces and shrinks failing examples into debuggable counterexamples. | [QuickCheck](https://www.cs.tufts.edu/~nr/cs257/archive/john-hughes/quick.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Tests assert roundtrip, idempotence, ordering, conservation, serialization, or algebraic laws over generated inputs. |
| 1016 | Build valid domain generators for property tests. | Mostly-filtered random data wastes runs and misses domain edge cases, while custom generators exercise meaningful valid states. | [Hypothesis settings](https://hypothesis.readthedocs.io/en/latest/settings.html) | 5 | 5 | Strategies create valid objects directly, include boundaries, and keep reject or filter rates low. |
| 1017 | Use metamorphic tests when no exact oracle exists. | Search, ML, ranking, numerical, and optimization systems often have relational correctness properties rather than exact expected outputs. | [Metamorphic Testing - A Review of Challenges and Opportunities](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/296477118_Metamorphic_Testing_A_Review_of_Challenges_and_Opportunities) | 5 | 4 | Tests transform inputs and assert permutation, scaling, monotonicity, subset, or roundtrip relations. |
| 1018 | Tier CI by risk, speed, and fault signal. | Test prioritization research and large-test guidance favor fast relevant feedback first and broader expensive suites later with clear ownership. | [Software Engineering at Google - Larger Testing](https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch14.html) | 5 | 4 | Presubmit runs fast impacted tests; postsubmit or release gates run broad slow suites with failure triage. |
| 1019 | Acknowledge durable writes only after the advertised commit point is recoverable. | Queue and log users treat acknowledgements as a durability contract, and Jepsen has found acknowledged writes lost when persistence lagged. | [Jepsen - NATS 2.12.1](https://jepsen.io/analyses/nats-2.12.1) | 5 | 5 | Publish or commit ack waits for required quorum and disk durability; crash tests measure acknowledged-message loss. |
| 1020 | Let quorum, not reachability, define write availability. | Raft-backed and quorum systems can only make safe progress where a majority can elect and serve a leader. | [RabbitMQ - Clustering and Network Partitions](https://www.rabbitmq.com/docs/partitions) | 5 | 5 | Minority partitions reject or time out writes; clients handle leader election pauses, nacks, and retryable failures. |
| 1021 | Treat consumer offsets as processing state. | Kafka offset commits determine replay and loss behavior; auto-commit can mark work complete before durable processing succeeds. | [Confluent - Kafka Consumer](https://docs.confluent.io/platform/current/clients/consumer.html) | 4 | 5 | Commit the next offset only after durable success; drain and revoke on rebalance; avoid auto-commit for nontrivial handlers. |
