| 1022 | Put broker-level backpressure on unacknowledged work. | Pending acknowledgements are the real in-flight work queue, and unbounded pending work causes overload and redelivery churn. | [NATS JetStream Consumers](https://docs.nats.io/nats-concepts/jetstream/consumers) | 5 | 5 | Configure prefetch, visibility, `MaxAckPending`, or equivalents; alert on ack timeout, redelivery, and oldest pending age. |
| 1023 | Do not substitute a broker for an event store. | Event sourcing requires per-entity streams, optimistic concurrency, immutable domain events, and replayable projections beyond ordinary distribution queues. | [Azure - Event Sourcing pattern](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/event-sourcing) | 5 | 5 | Aggregate streams append with expected revision checks; projections are rebuildable and snapshots are cache. |
| 1024 | Use CRDTs only when merge laws are part of the type. | CRDT convergence depends on deterministic merge and update laws, not merely labeling a replicated system eventually consistent. | [Conflict-free Replicated Data Types](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.06358) | 5 | 4 | Merge APIs include causal metadata where needed and property tests prove convergence under shuffled, duplicated, and replayed delivery. |
| 1025 | Link traces across asynchronous message boundaries. | Queue hops and batch consumption break simple parent-child traces; OpenTelemetry messaging semantics use propagated context and span links. | [OpenTelemetry - Messaging Spans](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/semconv/messaging/messaging-spans/) | 5 | 4 | Publishers inject trace context; consumers link processing spans to message creation context and settlement events. |
| 1026 | Use causal metadata for order-sensitive decisions. | Lamport clocks show distributed happened-before is partial and cannot be inferred from local receive time or wall-clock timestamp alone. | [Time, Clocks, and the Ordering of Events](https://lamport.azurewebsites.net/pubs/time-clocks.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Events carry version, causation ID, Lamport counter, vector clock, or expected revision for conflict checks. |
| 1027 | Preserve native form submission semantics. | HTML forms are a name/value protocol, and `requestSubmit()` preserves validation and submitter behavior that direct `submit()` bypasses. | [HTML Standard - Forms](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/forms.html) | 5 | 5 | Submitted controls have stable names; JS-triggered submit uses `requestSubmit`; Enter-key and no-JS paths are tested. |
| 1028 | Do not depend on unload. | Browser `unload` is unreliable and can make pages ineligible for the back-forward cache, hurting correctness and performance. | [web.dev - Back/forward cache](https://web.dev/articles/bfcache) | 5 | 5 | Avoid `unload`; use `visibilitychange` or `pagehide`; test BFCache restore with `pageshow.persisted`. |
| 1029 | Treat service workers as versioned network proxies. | Service workers intercept network requests and can preserve stale or broken cache behavior without explicit lifecycle and fallback discipline. | [web.dev - Serving](https://web.dev/learn/pwa/serving) | 5 | 5 | Cache names are versioned; `respondWith` is synchronous; activation cleans old caches; navigation fallback is tested. |
| 1030 | Treat Fetch and Cache bodies as one-shot streams. | Fetch response bodies are consumed by reads and `Cache.put`, so fan-out to cache, logs, and parsers requires cloning before consumption. | [MDN - Using Fetch](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Fetch_API/Using_Fetch) | 5 | 4 | Code clones responses before cache writes or multiple reads; tests cover cached, non-2xx, and error paths. |
| 1031 | Treat browser storage as evictable unless persistence is granted. | Browser storage is best-effort by default and may be evicted under pressure without prompting the user. | [MDN - Storage quotas and eviction criteria](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria) | 5 | 5 | Code handles quota and missing data, checks persistence status, and can resync or recover client-side state. |
| 1032 | Declare cookie delivery semantics per flow. | SameSite, Secure, Domain, Path, and cookie prefixes determine whether browsers attach cookies across OAuth, embeds, payments, and normal navigation. | [web.dev - SameSite cookies explained](https://web.dev/articles/samesite-cookies-explained) | 5 | 5 | CI asserts `Set-Cookie` attributes and browser tests cover same-site, cross-site, OAuth, embed, and payment callbacks. |
| 1033 | Roll CSP changes through headers and Report-Only. | CSP report-only mode is header-only, meta CSP lacks important directives, and `connect-src` controls fetch, WebSocket, EventSource, and beacons. | [W3C CSP Level 3](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSP3/) | 5 | 4 | Staging uses report endpoints before enforcement; tests cover APIs, WebSockets, SSE, and beacon under policy. |
| 1034 | Feature-detect web APIs before use. | Browser and UA strings are brittle; feature detection and Baseline-style compatibility gates are safer ways to ship Web API usage. | [MDN - Feature detection](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Learn_web_development/Extensions/Testing/Feature_detection) | 5 | 4 | Code uses API existence checks, CSS `@supports`, browser matrices, and polyfill or fallback paths instead of UA branches. |
| 1035 | Enforce Core Web Vitals at p75. | Core Web Vitals thresholds are judged at the 75th percentile, and performance budgets catch regressions before they become user-visible drift. | [web.dev - Defining Core Web Vitals thresholds](https://web.dev/articles/defining-core-web-vitals-thresholds) | 5 | 5 | CI and RUM track p75 LCP, INP, and CLS by device class; releases block significant regressions. |
| 1036 | Do not let untrusted metadata choose validation policy. | JWT algorithm-confusion failures show that validation rules must come from trusted configuration, not from the artifact being validated. | [RFC 8725 - JSON Web Token Best Current Practices](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8725.html) | 5 | 5 | Validators require caller-supplied allowed algorithms, key types, issuers, and policies; tests mutate `alg`, `kid`, and declared token type. |
| 1037 | Bind tokens, assertions, and proofs to their intended context. | A valid artifact can still be wrong if accepted by the wrong issuer, audience, tenant, client, resource, or protocol path. | [RFC 9700 - OAuth 2.0 Security Best Current Practice](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9700) | 5 | 5 | Token validation checks issuer, audience, subject, tenant, client, resource, token type, and intended use before authorization. |
| 1038 | Make freshness and replay resistance explicit state. | Authentication artifacts, one-time codes, and proof messages are vulnerable when lifetimes, nonces, state, counters, or replay caches are informal. | [NIST SP 800-63B-4](https://pages.nist.gov/800-63-4/sp800-63b.html) | 5 | 5 | Flows bind nonce or state, expire artifacts, reject replay, and test stolen-code, repeated-request, and delayed-message cases. |
| 1039 | Treat sessions as bounded evidence of prior authentication. | A session should not outlive or exceed the assurance of the authentication event that created it. | [NIST SP 800-63B-4](https://pages.nist.gov/800-63-4/sp800-63b.html) | 5 | 5 | Sessions have idle and absolute expiry, rotation after reauth or privilege change, server-side invalidation, and logout semantics. |
| 1040 | Authenticate data before use. | AEAD and signature schemes are only protective when plaintext, claims, or parsed objects are unavailable until integrity checks succeed. | [RFC 5116 - Authenticated Encryption](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc5116) | 5 | 5 | Crypto APIs return plaintext only after tag or signature success; associated data includes version, tenant, resource, and record type. |
| 1041 | Derive purpose-labeled keys instead of reusing master material. | Domain-separated key derivation prevents one use of a secret from undermining another use. | [RFC 5869 - HKDF](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc5869) | 5 | 4 | KDF context includes purpose, protocol version, algorithm, environment, tenant, and app scope; typed key handles prevent cross-use. |
| 1042 | Consume only the object you verified. | Signature-wrapping failures show that validating one representation and then reading another can bypass otherwise sound cryptography. | [OWASP - SAML Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/SAML_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 4 | 5 | Code parses once, verifies referenced IDs, rejects duplicates, and passes the verified object rather than reparsing or searching the document later. |
| 1043 | Treat downgrade and fallback paths as security-sensitive code. | Compatibility fallback can silently route users onto deprecated protocols, weak algorithms, or old validation behavior. | [RFC 9325 - Recommendations for Secure Use of TLS and DTLS](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9325) | 5 | 4 | Minimum versions and algorithms are policy; fallback needs explicit operator approval, telemetry, expiry, and tests. |
| 1044 | Make authentication failures externally uniform. | Different messages, status codes, timing, or branches can leak account existence or secret validity. | [OWASP - Authentication Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 4 | 4 | External responses and timing are uniform; detailed reasons go only to protected logs with rate limits and correlation IDs. |
| 1045 | Build cryptographic incident recovery before compromise. | Entropy failures and key compromise require inventory, revocation, rekeying, and replacement paths before the emergency. | [NIST SP 800-133 Rev. 2](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.800-133r2.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Key inventory records origin, purpose, algorithm, owner, lifetime, and dependents; rotation and weak-key detection are rehearsed. |
| 1046 | Version behavior, not just code. | Serious systems change through data, configs, prompts, models, thresholds, dependencies, and feature definitions, not only source commits. | [MLflow - Tracking](https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/ml/tracking/) | 5 | 5 | Release manifests pin code commit, data hash, config, model or prompt version, dependency lockfile, and evaluator version. |
| 1047 | Make provenance queryable, not only documented. | Reproducibility and incident analysis require asking which inputs, parameters, artifacts, and executions produced a specific output. | [TFX - ML Metadata](https://www.tensorflow.org/tfx/guide/mlmd) | 5 | 5 | Given any prediction, report, build, or generated artifact, tooling can trace upstream data, run, config, owner, and artifact IDs. |
| 1048 | Treat configuration as reviewed code. | Hidden technical debt often accumulates in configuration that changes behavior while escaping diff review, tests, and ownership. | [Hidden Technical Debt in Machine Learning Systems](https://papers.neurips.cc/paper/5656-hidden-technical-debt-in-machine-learning-systems.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Configs are versioned, code-reviewed, diffable, schema-checked, tested with assertions, and free of manual dashboard-only drift. |
| 1049 | Ship rollback and blast-radius controls with adaptive artifacts. | Model, prompt, rules, and data-dependent releases can fail nonlocally, so the artifact needs staged rollout and fast reversal paths. | [Google Cloud - MLOps continuous delivery](https://docs.cloud.google.com/architecture/mlops-continuous-delivery-and-automation-pipelines-in-machine-learning) | 4 | 5 | Releases support canaries, alias flips, kill switches, previous-artifact restore, and action-rate limits. |
| 1050 | Treat retrieval context and generated output as untrusted data. | Prompt injection, poisoned embeddings, insecure output handling, and excessive agency turn text and retrieved context into an attack surface. | [OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications 2025](https://genai.owasp.org/llm-top-10/) | 5 | 5 | Retrieval is permission-aware and source-authenticated; outputs are validated before tool calls, rendering, storage, or external side effects. |
| 1051 | Evaluate retrieval systems as pipelines, not single answers. | Retrieval, context assembly, generation, citation, and safety can fail independently even when the final answer looks plausible. | [Evidently AI - RAG Evaluation Guide](https://www.evidentlyai.com/llm-guide/rag-evaluation) | 4 | 4 | Metrics cover retrieval recall, context relevance, faithfulness, answer relevance, citation quality, safety, and adversarial cases. |
| 1052 | Treat timing as a requirement, not a performance hope. | Realtime and safety-critical guidance shows correctness can depend on worst-case deadlines, not average latency after the fact. | [FAA - Real-Time Scheduling Analysis](https://www.faa.gov/sites/faa.gov/files/aircraft/air_cert/design_approvals/air_software/05-27_RTSA.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Critical tasks declare period, deadline, worst-case execution budget, overload behavior, and schedulability or load-test evidence. |
| 1053 | Treat rule deviations as auditable risk decisions. | Safety rule systems lose credibility when suppressions and deviations are informal, permanent, or detached from risk rationale. | [MISRA Compliance:2020](https://misra.org.uk/app/uploads/2021/06/MISRA-Compliance-2020.pdf) | 5 | 4 | Suppression records include rationale, risk, scope, approver, owner, review date, and expiry or removal condition. |
| 1054 | Attach machine-readable privacy metadata to personal data. | Purpose limitation, lawful basis, accountability, and retention cannot be enforced when data is merely labeled as generic PII. | [CNIL - Legal basis in technical implementation](https://www.cnil.fr/en/sheet-ndeg15-take-account-legal-basis-technical-implementation) | 5 | 5 | Schemas and events declare purpose, legal basis, owner, subject type, retention class, and sensitivity; CI blocks missing metadata. |
| 1055 | Maintain a live data map from collection to deletion. | Teams cannot protect, delete, export, or explain data they cannot locate across stores, processors, exports, backups, and regions. | [FTC - Protecting Personal Information](https://www.ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/protecting-personal-information-guide-business) | 5 | 5 | Data inventory is generated or verified from schemas, pipelines, vendors, infrastructure tags, processors, recipients, and deletion paths. |
| 1056 | Enforce purpose-scoped processing in code. | Data collected for one purpose should not silently feed analytics, AI training, ads, support tooling, or unrelated operations. | [NIST Privacy Framework Core](https://www.nist.gov/document/nist-privacy-framework-version-1-core-pdf) | 5 | 5 | APIs, jobs, and queries require purpose scopes; tests fail when a purpose-incompatible caller reads or exports data. |
| 1057 | Model consent as granular, versioned, and revocable state. | Consent must be specific, informed, withdrawable, and tied to a purpose; a single boolean cannot carry that contract. | [GDPR consolidated text](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/HTML/?uri=CELEX%3A02016R0679-20160504) | 5 | 5 | Consent ledger stores notice version, purpose, timestamp, mechanism, withdrawal, actor, and downstream propagation status. |
| 1058 | Test privacy controls like product behavior. | Retention, revocation, deletion, anonymization, exports, and purpose checks regress when treated as policy paperwork instead of executable behavior. | [EDPB - Guidelines 4/2019 on Article 25](https://www.edpb.europa.eu/sites/default/files/files/file1/edpb_guidelines_201904_dataprotection_by_design_and_by_default_v2.0_en.pdf) | 5 | 4 | CI and scheduled canaries exercise purge jobs, erasure workflows, consent withdrawal, access exports, anonymization, and purpose denial. |
| 1059 | Treat CI inputs as attacker-controlled. | Pull requests, titles, refs, workflow inputs, and unreviewed code can become privileged build inputs when CI has secrets or write tokens. | [OpenSSF OSPS Baseline v2026.02.19](https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19.html) | 5 | 5 | Workflows default to read-only permissions, keep secrets from untrusted PRs, audit `pull_request_target`, and isolate write-capable jobs. |
| 1060 | Signpost bad releases instead of erasing history. | Deleting released packages or tags can break downstream users; yanking and deprecation preserve history while warning consumers. | [PyPI - Yanking](https://docs.pypi.org/project-management/yanking/) | 5 | 4 | Yank or deprecate affected versions, publish a fixed version, link the advisory, and document remediation steps. |
| 1061 | Make dependency governance a written policy. | Lockfiles alone do not say how dependencies are selected, obtained, updated, audited, remediated, or excepted. | [OpenSSF Best Practices Criteria](https://www.bestpractices.dev/en/criteria) | 5 | 5 | Project policy defines approved sources, update cadence, vulnerability SLA, SBOM expectations, ownership, and exception handling. |
| 1062 | Create a security disclosure path before the first report. | Vulnerability handling needs a known private channel, supported-version policy, response expectations, and advisory workflow before an incident. | [GitHub - Add a security policy](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/how-tos/report-and-fix-vulnerabilities/configure-vulnerability-reporting/add-security-policy) | 5 | 5 | `SECURITY.md`, private reporting, response SLA, advisory process, CVE path, and supported-version scope are present and tested. |
| 1063 | Treat maintainer authority as a controlled access ceremony. | Contributor promotion is a security boundary because repository, package, CI, and release access can be socially engineered. | [OpenSSF/OpenJS - Social engineering takeover alert](https://openssf.org/blog/2024/04/15/open-source-security-openssf-and-openjs-foundations-issue-alert-for-social-engineering-takeovers-of-open-source-projects/) | 5 | 5 | Maintainer access has written criteria, two-maintainer approval, cooling-off, scoped permissions, offboarding, and periodic access review. |
| 1064 | Treat project continuity as a security requirement. | Single-person bottlenecks can block fixes, releases, account recovery, incident response, and credential rotation. | [OpenSSF Best Practices Silver Criteria](https://www.bestpractices.dev/en/criteria/1) | 5 | 4 | Repo, registry, domain, CI, signing, and incident access have backups, emergency process, and release deputies. |
| 1065 | Ship runnable artifacts for important claims. | Independent review is strongest when code, scripts, inputs, outputs, and instructions can actually be exercised. | [ACM - Artifact Review and Badging](https://reviewers.acm.org/training-course/artifact-review-and-badging) | 5 | 5 | Important analyses, benchmarks, migrations, and claims include a reproduce directory, README, scripts, data links, expected outputs, and license. |
| 1066 | Make every data transformation executable and logged. | Narrated workflows drift; executable pipelines preserve exact step order, inputs, parameters, versions, and outputs. | [Ten Simple Rules for Reproducible Computational Research](https://journals.plos.org/ploscompbiol/article?id=10.1371%2Fjournal.pcbi.1003285) | 4 | 5 | Transformations run through scripts, Makefiles, workflow engines, or CI jobs and capture input hashes, parameters, code version, and actor. |
| 1067 | Capture the runtime environment with the code. | Reproducibility depends on operating system, runtime, dependencies, libraries, compiler flags, and sometimes hardware capabilities. | [The Turing Way - Reproducible Environments](https://book.the-turing-way.org/reproducible-research/renv/) | 4 | 5 | Lockfiles, containers, environment specs, compiler flags, and fresh-machine setup are tested in CI. |
| 1068 | Give artifacts persistent version-specific identities. | Data and software citation principles require stable, specific identifiers so later users can find the exact artifact version. | [Software Citation Principles](https://danielskatz.org/papers/peerj-cs-86.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Releases include tags, DOIs, SWHIDs, dataset version IDs, CITATION metadata, and immutable dependency manifests. |
| 1069 | Prefer stable numerical formulations before speed tricks. | Floating-point standards define behavior, but unstable formulas still amplify cancellation, overflow, underflow, NaNs, and rounding error. | [IEEE Std 754-2019](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/8766229) | 5 | 5 | Numeric code uses stable library algorithms, explicit dtypes, condition checks, NaN and overflow handling, and precision-sensitive tests. |
| 1070 | Notebooks must run clean from top to bottom. | Hidden state, out-of-order cells, local paths, and undeclared dependencies turn notebooks into unreproducible code. | [Pimentel et al. - Jupyter notebook quality and reproducibility](https://leomurta.github.io/papers/pimentel2019a.pdf) | 4 | 4 | CI restart-runs notebooks, checks execution order, forbids hardcoded local paths, and moves reusable logic into importable modules. |
| 1071 | Declare resource contracts for every workload. | Kubernetes scheduling and stability depend on explicit resource requests and limits, not ambient cluster overcapacity. | [Kubernetes - Resource Management for Pods and Containers](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/) | 5 | 5 | Workloads declare CPU, memory, storage, and ephemeral-storage requests and limits; quotas and dashboards catch missing or unrealistic values. |
| 1072 | Default-deny internal network reachability. | Flat service networks allow one compromised workload to reach everything unless allowed communication paths are explicit. | [Kubernetes - Network Policies](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/services-networking/network-policies/) | 5 | 5 | Namespaces or segments default-deny ingress and egress; policies allow only required DNS, dependencies, and management traffic. |
| 1073 | Enforce restricted runtime posture by default. | Containers should not run with host-like privileges unless a narrow, reviewed exception proves the need. | [Kubernetes - Pod Security Standards](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/security/pod-security-standards/) | 5 | 5 | Default posture is non-root, unprivileged, no privilege escalation, dropped capabilities, seccomp or AppArmor, and read-only root filesystems. |
| 1074 | Design code to survive preemption. | Mobile platforms can suspend or kill processes without graceful teardown, and the same failure mode exists for containers, workers, and serverless runtimes. | [Android - Processes and app lifecycle](https://developer.android.com/guide/components/activities/process-lifecycle) | 5 | 5 | Critical state is checkpointed at lifecycle boundaries; process-kill and restart tests prove recovery without destructor or exit-hook assumptions. |
| 1075 | Make background work declared, schedulable, cancellable, and budgeted. | Platforms ration background runtime for battery, privacy, and foreground responsiveness, exposing the general danger of unowned background loops. | [Android - Task scheduling](https://developer.android.com/develop/background-work/background-tasks/persistent) | 5 | 5 | Background jobs have owner, constraints, timeout, retry/backoff, cancellation, persistence, and no unbounded polling. |
| 1076 | Make offline and resume behavior part of the data model. | Network loss, app suspension, interrupted transfers, and retry ambiguity require local truth, persisted intent, and idempotent sync. | [Android - Build an offline-first app](https://developer.android.com/topic/architecture/data-layer/offline-first) | 5 | 5 | Local store, persisted sync queue, idempotency keys, conflict policy, and resumable upload or download tests exist. |
| 1077 | Treat backups, caches, logs, and external files as data exits. | Local data can leak through backups, shared storage, temporary files, support exports, and logs even when primary storage is protected. | [Android - Security recommendations for backups](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/risks/backup-best-practices) | 5 | 5 | Data classification drives backup rules, external storage policy, cache TTLs, temp-file cleanup, log redaction, and export tests. |
| 1078 | Use platform key custody for long-lived sensitive keys. | Keystores, keychains, secure enclaves, KMS, and HSMs reduce exposure compared with app-owned key files or hidden config values. | [Android - Keystore system](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/keystore) | 5 | 4 | Keys are generated or wrapped by platform custody with access controls, use scopes, rotation, and no raw long-lived private keys in files. |
| 1079 | Design client releases for weak rollback. | Native and distributed clients can remain on old versions for long periods, so bad releases need staged exposure and forward-compatible recovery. | [Google SRE - Engineering Reliable Mobile Applications](https://sre.google/static/pdf/EngineeringReliableMobileApplications.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Client rollouts are staged, kill-switched, protocol-compatible, telemetry-gated, and able to roll forward quickly. |
| 1080 | Model durable API concepts as stable resources with opaque identifiers. | Stable object identity anchors caching, refetching, linking, authorization, and cross-version continuity. | [Google AIP-121 - Resource-oriented design](https://google.aip.dev/121) | 5 | 5 | Durable objects have stable IDs or names; clients never parse IDs; action endpoints need explicit justification. |
| 1081 | Make schemas executable and authoritative. | OpenAPI, protobuf, GraphQL, and registry schemas should be the source that humans, validators, generated clients, and compatibility gates trust. | [OpenAPI Specification v3.1.0](https://spec.openapis.org/oas/v3.1.0.html) | 5 | 5 | CI validates examples, requests, responses, generated clients, server behavior, and compatibility diffs against the schema. |
| 1082 | Design enum-like values for future unknowns. | New response values break clients with exhaustive switches unless the contract provides unknown handling or a truly closed set. | [Google AIP-126 - Enumerations](https://google.aip.dev/126) | 5 | 4 | Enums include unspecified or unknown handling, SDKs preserve raw values, and reviews flag values expected to change often. |
| 1083 | Evolve graph-style schemas at the field level. | GraphQL-style contracts avoid whole-API versioning only when old fields remain, new fields are additive, and tightening is treated as breaking. | [GraphQL - Schema Design](https://graphql.org/learn/schema-design/) | 5 | 4 | Schema diff blocks field removals, renamed fields, new required arguments, and non-null tightening until deprecated and migrated. |
| 1084 | Plan DDL like a production workload. | Schema changes can take locks, rewrite tables, copy data, or block writers, so migration risk must be known before merge. | [PostgreSQL - ALTER TABLE](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-altertable.html) | 5 | 5 | Migration PRs state lock mode, rewrite or copy risk, duration, timeout, abort path, online migration plan, and backfill strategy. |
| 1085 | Never edit applied migration history. | Migration tools use schema history and checksums so drift is caught and production state remains explainable. | [Flyway - Validate](https://documentation.red-gate.com/fd/validate-277578898.html) | 5 | 5 | CI validates migrations and rejects edits to already-applied files except documented repair procedures. |
| 1086 | Put machine-checkable preconditions before destructive changes. | Drops, rewrites, deletes, and type narrowing should prove target assumptions before they can damage data. | [Liquibase - Preconditions](https://docs.liquibase.com/secure/user-guide-5-1-1/what-are-preconditions) | 5 | 5 | Destructive migrations check database type, dependency absence, row counts, nullability, constraint state, and expected object versions. |
| 1087 | Deploy shared schema changes as expand-contract migrations. | Old and new code often run together, so breaking schema changes need additive expansion, migration, verification, and delayed contraction. | [dbt - Contract](https://docs.getdbt.com/reference/resource-configs/contract) | 5 | 5 | Separate add, dual-write or backfill, enforce, and drop steps; app versions tolerate both old and new schemas during rollout. |
| 1088 | Separate semantic data interfaces from raw storage churn. | Downstream consumers need stable metric and semantic definitions even while warehouse tables and pipelines evolve. | [Airbnb - Minerva](https://medium.com/airbnb-engineering/how-airbnb-achieved-metric-consistency-at-scale-f23cc53dea70) | 4 | 4 | Metrics and semantic models have canonical definitions, owners, tests, versions, deprecation paths, and independence from physical table layout. |
| 1089 | Retain history long enough for replay, audit, and lag. | Time travel, CDC, reproducible analytics, and delayed consumers all fail if logs or table history are purged too aggressively. | [Debezium - MySQL connector](https://debezium.io/documentation/reference/stable/connectors/mysql.html) | 5 | 5 | Retention policy covers max CDC lag, backfill windows, audit needs, time travel, replay, and cleanup minimums. |
| 1090 | Put blast-radius controls on high-impact data automation. | Mutating jobs can cascade quickly, so they need canaries, caps, thresholds, stop controls, and actionable ownership. | [SEC - Knight Capital order](https://www.sec.gov/files/litigation/admin/2013/34-70694.pdf) | 5 | 5 | Jobs enforce row, byte, money, or partition caps, canary runs, automatic abort thresholds, owner paging, and tested kill switches. |
| 1091 | Separate human output from automation contracts. | CLI and tool output becomes an integration surface, so prompts, logs, prose, and parseable data must not collide. | [Google Cloud CLI overview](https://docs.cloud.google.com/sdk/gcloud) | 5 | 5 | Tests assert stdout and stderr separation, stable exit codes, JSON or formatted output modes, quiet mode, and noninteractive behavior. |
| 1092 | Errors must include diagnosis and next action. | Users and operators recover faster when failures say what happened, where, why if known, and what to do next. | [Microsoft - Error Message Guidelines](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/debug/error-message-guidelines) | 5 | 5 | Error types require message, category or cause, affected field or resource, recommendation, correlation ID, and tests rejecting generic messages. |
| 1093 | Onboarding must prove first success safely. | A first-run path should be executable, low-risk, visibly successful, and close enough to real use to build correct habits. | [Stripe - Send your first API request](https://docs.stripe.com/get-started/api-request) | 5 | 5 | Quickstarts include prerequisites, sandbox or test mode, copyable command, expected output, cleanup, and troubleshooting path. |
| 1094 | Treat migration guides as execution plans with rollback. | Migration documentation should be staged work with validation, fallback, dependency mapping, and retirement criteria, not narrative advice. | [Google Cloud - Migration best practices](https://docs.cloud.google.com/architecture/migration-to-google-cloud-best-practices) | 5 | 5 | Guides include prerequisites, inventory, step order, success criteria, rollback per step, max duration, support channel, and post-migration checks. |
| 1095 | Automate mechanical review gates and reserve people for judgment. | Human reviewers should focus on design, behavior, tests, and maintainability while machines enforce formatting, linting, type checks, and generated diffs. | [Google Engineering Practices - What to look for in a code review](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html) | 5 | 5 | Formatting, lint, type, generated-code freshness, and policy checks run in CI; review checklists distinguish blocking issues from nits. |
| 1096 | Operational handoffs need explicit ownership transfer. | Incidents, migrations, and long-running operations fail when current state, attempted actions, next steps, and owner authority are implicit. | [Google SRE - Managing Incidents](https://sre.google/sre-book/managing-incidents/) | 5 | 5 | Handoff records owner, roles, current state, attempted actions, pending risks, next checks, escalation, and acknowledged acceptance. |
| 1097 | Serialize data, not executable object graphs. | Native object deserializers can execute gadget logic before the application receives a safely typed object. | [OWASP - Deserialization Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Deserialization_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Unsafe object deserializers are banned or allowlisted; schemas, signatures, size limits, and depth limits gate incoming serialized data. |
| 1098 | Treat outbound fetch as privileged RPC. | Server-side fetch uses ambient network position and credentials, so user-provided URLs are capability requests, not plain strings. | [OWASP - SSRF Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Server_Side_Request_Forgery_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | User choices map to declared destinations; redirects, private ranges, link-local metadata, non-HTTP schemes, and localhost variants are blocked. |
| 1099 | Parse, canonicalize, authorize, then freeze the exact object used by the sink. | URL, path, Unicode, and IDN parser differences bypass controls when validation and consumption use different representations. | [CERT IDS01-J](https://cmu-sei.github.io/secure-coding-standards/sei-cert-oracle-coding-standard-for-java/rules/input-validation-and-data-sanitization-ids/ids01-j/) | 5 | 5 | One parser feeds both policy and use; code rejects ambiguous encodings, alternate host forms, double decoding, and post-validation mutation. |
| 1100 | Never concatenate user text into interpreter surfaces. | Template engines, expression languages, shell snippets, SQL fragments, logging lookups, and prompt templates become execution surfaces when text is treated as syntax. | [CWE-1336 - Improper Neutralization of Special Elements Used in a Template Engine](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1336.html) | 5 | 5 | Use fixed template IDs and context objects; ban `eval`, unsafe template constructors, expression injection, and unreviewed custom template editors. |
| 1101 | Reject ambiguous HTTP framing at the first boundary. | Request smuggling exploits disagreement between front-end and back-end parsers over content length, transfer encoding, and HTTP edge cases. | [CWE-444 - HTTP Request/Response Smuggling](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/444.html) | 5 | 5 | Edge parsers reject duplicate or conflicting length headers, invalid chunking, HTTP/1.0 transfer encoding, obs-fold, and malformed newlines. |
| 1102 | Pin dependency identity to registry and integrity. | Dependency confusion exploits resolver fallback and package-name collisions, so version pinning alone does not prove source identity. | [OWASP - NPM Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/NPM_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html) | 5 | 5 | Private scopes map to private registries, public fallback is blocked, lockfile integrity is enforced, and internal names are reserved. |
| 1103 | Sandboxes need brokered capabilities. | Escapes often abuse IPC, leaked handles, host mounts, shared kernels, or privileged brokers after code execution begins inside the sandbox. | [Chromium - Sandbox](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/%2B/HEAD/docs/design/sandbox.md) | 5 | 5 | Host actions go through allowlisted brokers; code runs with dropped capabilities, isolated users, no raw Docker socket, and fuzzed IPC boundaries. |
| 1104 | Never block the executor needed for progress. | Runtime docs warn that blocking a bounded pool, event loop, or async scheduler can deadlock or starve the work that would unblock it. | [Node.js - Don't Block the Event Loop](https://nodejs.org/learn/asynchronous-work/dont-block-the-event-loop) | 5 | 5 | Async and pool code avoids sync waits, nested same-pool submissions, CPU-heavy callbacks, and blocking I/O on progress-critical threads. |
| 1105 | Make deadlines cancel the actual work. | Timeout at the caller is incomplete if the child process, goroutine, task, or I/O operation keeps consuming resources after abandonment. | [Go context package](https://pkg.go.dev/context) | 5 | 5 | APIs accept context, cancellation token, or abort signal; timeouts clean up child processes, sockets, leases, and spawned work. |
| 1106 | Keep render and selector paths pure. | UI frameworks may replay, defer, hydrate, memoize, or debug render logic; side effects in render make output nondeterministic. | [React - Components and Hooks must be pure](https://react.dev/reference/rules/components-and-hooks-must-be-pure) | 5 | 5 | Render, template, selector, and reducer paths do not perform mutation, network access, storage writes, subscriptions, randomness, or clock reads. |
| 1107 | Do not mutate framework-owned state behind the framework. | Direct DOM, prop, or internal state mutation bypasses the framework source of truth and can break updates, hydration, and accessibility. | [Angular - Hydration](https://angular.dev/guide/hydration) | 5 | 4 | Imperative libraries are isolated behind lifecycle adapters with cleanup; framework-rendered state remains authoritative. |
| 1108 | Cache keys must include every response-varying input. | If origin output varies by header, cookie, query, language, device, auth context, or body assumption, an unkeyed cache can leak or poison responses. | [Amazon CloudFront - Understand the cache key](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/understanding-the-cache-key.html) | 5 | 5 | Each cached route inventories variation inputs and contract-tests the edge cache key against origin behavior. |
| 1109 | Cache only what stays safe under cache semantics. | Cache deception and poisoning happen when the origin and edge disagree about whether a response is public, static, personalized, or route-derived. | [Cloudflare - Avoid web cache poisoning](https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/cache-security/avoid-web-poisoning/) | 5 | 5 | Personalized and dynamic routes default to private or no-store; extension, content type, GET body, and auth variation assumptions are tested. |
| 1110 | Purge through the same dimensions used to store. | Custom cache keys create variants; purge-by-URL can leave stale language, device, header, query, or tenant variants behind. | [Cloudflare - Purge cache key resources](https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/how-to/purge-cache/purge-cache-key/) | 5 | 4 | Purge tools derive keys from the same schema as storage or use tags and prefixes with tests proving all variants disappear. |
| 1111 | Treat DNS changes as TTL-bounded migrations. | Positive and negative DNS answers persist in resolver caches, so DNS cutovers are timed migrations rather than instant configuration flips. | [RFC 2308 - DNS Negative Caching](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc2308) | 5 | 5 | Lower TTL before cutover, account for SOA negative TTL, keep old endpoints serving, and verify propagation through independent resolvers. |
| 1112 | Design DNS authority for independent failure modes. | Multiple nameservers do not provide resilience if they share physical, network, provider, or control-plane dependencies. | [RFC 2182 - Selection and Operation of Secondary DNS Servers](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc2182) | 5 | 5 | Authoritative DNS has provider or network diversity, separate health checks, and data-plane failover that does not depend on a broken control plane. |
| 1113 | Give every deployed unit an accountable identity. | Fleets become unpatchable and unauditable when devices, agents, workloads, or nodes share credentials or cannot be individually revoked. | [NIST IoT Device Cybersecurity Capabilities Catalog](https://pages.nist.gov/IoT-Device-Cybersecurity-Requirement-Catalogs/technical/) | 5 | 5 | Each deployed unit has a unique key, certificate, or workload identity, registry owner, revocation path, and rotation test. |
| 1114 | Do not ship fielded code without a safe update path. | Remote or expensive-to-touch systems need update capability as the recovery channel for vulnerabilities, expired credentials, and operational defects. | [FreeRTOS - OTA Updates](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/freertos/latest/userguide/freertos-ota-dev.html) | 5 | 5 | Supported prior versions can update through authorized channels before first production deployment, and the update path is tested. |
| 1115 | Treat every update as untrusted until signed, compatible, and fresh. | Robust update systems defend against rollback, stale metadata, wrong-target delivery, mix-and-match metadata, and key compromise, not just transport failure. | [The Update Framework - Security](https://theupdateframework.io/docs/security/) | 5 | 5 | Updaters verify signature, hash, size, target identity, compatibility, version monotonicity, metadata expiry, and anti-rollback state before activation. |
| 1116 | Preserve a known-good path during updates. | OTA and edge deployments fail through power loss, bad flashes, corrupt downloads, failed boots, and incompatible environments. | [Android - A/B system updates](https://source.android.com/docs/core/ota/ab) | 5 | 5 | Updates use A/B slots or equivalent recovery, self-test before commit, automatic rollback, power-loss tests, and manual recovery. |
| 1117 | Reconcile remote intent with reported state. | Offline and constrained fleets cannot rely on fire-and-forget commands; desired state and actual state must converge durably. | [Azure IoT Hub - Device twins](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/iot-hub/iot-hub-devguide-device-twins) | 5 | 5 | Remote jobs use desired and reported state, monotonic operation IDs, idempotent handlers, resumable progress, and terminal failure states. |
| 1118 | Separate release authority from delivery convenience. | Fast online delivery must not mean one online credential can bless arbitrary code for an entire fleet. | [Uptane - Design](https://uptane.org/learn-more/design) | 5 | 5 | Release systems use offline root or release keys, limited online delivery keys, threshold signing, key rotation, and revocation drills. |
| 1119 | Define the end-of-life contract before shipment. | Long-lived software may outlive vendors, certificates, ownership, or connected services, so support and decommission behavior must be explicit. | [NIST IR 8259](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/ir/8259/final) | 5 | 4 | Products publish support windows, vulnerability-disclosure paths, credential-expiry handling, decommission mode, ownership transfer, and post-support risk. |
| 1120 | Validate physical-world impact before remote releases. | Software that affects devices, vehicles, buildings, or support systems can harm users physically or operationally when remote changes go wrong. | [NHTSA Tesla Recall 23V-838](https://static.nhtsa.gov/odi/rcl/2023/RCLRPT-23V838-8276.PDF) | 5 | 4 | Release criteria include environment assumptions, manual fallback, user impact, support capacity, rollback, and customer communication. |
| 1121 | Treat asset pipelines as build systems. | Content workflows need dependency tracking, deterministic transforms, stable identities, caching, and reproducible generated artifacts. | [Unity - Asset Database](https://docs.unity3d.com/2021.2/Documentation/Manual/AssetDatabase.html) | 5 | 5 | Asset imports are deterministic, dependency-tracked, cacheable, CI-reproducible, and governed like generated code. |
| 1122 | Use budgets and percentiles instead of averages. | Average frame rate or latency hides hitches, tail behavior, and subsystem regressions that users actually experience. | [Unity - Best practices for profiling game performance](https://unity.com/how-to/best-practices-for-profiling-game-performance) | 5 | 5 | Track frame time, p95 or p99 latency, subsystem budgets, target-hardware profiles, and CI budget regressions. |
| 1123 | Choose synchronization models from invariants. | Lockstep, rollback, client-server authority, and snapshot interpolation trade determinism, responsiveness, bandwidth, player count, and correction tolerance differently. | [Gaffer on Games - Snapshot Interpolation](https://gafferongames.com/post/snapshot_interpolation/) | 4 | 4 | Architecture docs state authority, determinism, max participants, bandwidth, correction policy, and failure behavior before implementation. |
| 1124 | Design hot paths around data movement. | Cache locality, compact storage, batching, and memory bandwidth often dominate performance more than object structure or line count. | [Godot - General optimization tips](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/performance/general_optimization.html) | 5 | 5 | Hot loops track working set, cache misses, contiguous access, allocation rate, and batch opportunities before abstraction cleanup. |
